# Sūrah al-Isrāʾ (Chapter 17) — Expanded Verse-by-Verse Commentary

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:1

> **Glory be to the One Who took His servant ˹Muḥammad˺ by night from the Sacred Mosque to the Farthest Mosque whose surroundings We have blessed, so that We may show him some of Our signs. Indeed, He alone is the All-Hearing, All-Seeing.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Opening With Glorification**

Subḥāna al-ladhī asrā bi-ʿabdihi laylan — glory be to the One who took His servant by night. The sūrah opens with subḥāna, glorification, declaring Allah free of any imperfection, far above what is ascribed. This opening is unique: only this sūrah begins with subḥāna as a standalone glorification of an act. Al-Ṭabarī explains that subḥāna here is to declare Allah's transcendence and power in performing the Night Journey, al-isrā', which is beyond ordinary human capability. The act described is asrā bi-ʿabdihi, He took His servant by night, from a-s-r, to travel by night. The servant, ʿabdihi, is Muḥammad ﷺ, called servant to emphasize humility and honor: highest honor is to be called servant of Allah, as in 18:1 and 25:1. Laylan, by night, emphasizes that journey occurred at night, in darkness, yet with divine light, and that it was accomplished in a portion of night, not over months. The addition ˹Muḥammad˺ in brackets clarifies who servant is.

**From Sacred Mosque To Farthest Mosque**

Min al-masjidi al-ḥarāmi ilā al-masjidi al-aqṣā — from the Sacred Mosque to the Farthest Mosque. Al-masjid al-ḥarām is the Sacred Mosque in Makkah containing Kaʿbah. Al-masjid al-aqṣā is the Farthest Mosque in Jerusalem, site of ancient Temple. Al-Ṭabarī reports that Prophet ﷺ was taken from Sacred Mosque itself, not just from Makkah generally, as hadith describes him being roused in ḥaram near Kaʿbah. Al-aqṣā means farthest, indicating distance from Makkah and also farthest in sanctity visited by Prophet during his lifetime. At time of revelation, al-masjid al-aqṣā referred to Temple Mount area in Jerusalem, which had no standing mosque building as today but was sacred precinct. The journey from Makkah to Jerusalem is about 1,200 km, which would ordinarily take over a month by camel, yet was accomplished in part of night, demonstrating divine power. The phrase alladhī bāraknā ḥawlahu — whose surroundings We blessed — refers to Jerusalem and its environs, blessed with fertility and with presence of many prophets, as 7:137 and 21:71 mention blessed land.

**So That We May Show Him Some Of Our Signs**

Li-nuriyahu min āyātinā — so that We may show him some of Our signs. Li- indicates purpose: journey was to show servant some of Allah's signs. Āyātinā, Our signs, includes miraculous journey itself, visions of heavens during ascension, encounters with previous prophets, and divine presence. Al-Qurṭubī says signs include both horizontal signs, the blessed land of Jerusalem, and vertical signs, the heavens ascended in miʿrāj, which is alluded to in 53:1-18. The purpose clause shows that Night Journey was not for Allah's need but for servant's honor and education, to show him signs that would strengthen heart after Year of Sorrow. The Prophet ﷺ had lost Khadījah and Abū Ṭālib, faced intense persecution, and was shown that his mission is connected to all previous prophets, whose center was Jerusalem. The journey therefore re-centers prophetic tradition around him.

**Indeed He Alone Is All-Hearing, All-Seeing**

Innahu huwa al-samīʿu al-baṣīr — indeed He is All-Hearing, All-Seeing. Innahu refers to Allah. Huwa for emphasis, He alone. Samīʿ, All-Hearing, hears all, including Prophet's supplications and Quraysh's mockery. Baṣīr, All-Seeing, sees all, including Prophet's sorrow and enemies' plotting. Al-Rāzī explains that seal with hearing and seeing is appropriate after journey that was in response to Prophet's situation: Allah heard and saw his sorrow and honored him. It also indicates that Allah heard and saw those who denied Night Journey and will hold accountable. The two names also prepare for next verses about Children of Israel and their corruption, which Allah heard and saw.

**Why Night Journey Matters For This Sūrah**

Sūrah al-Isrā' is also called Sūrat Banī Isrā'īl because verses 2-8 discuss Children of Israel's two corruptions and punishments. Placing Night Journey before that history is deliberate: Prophet's journey to Jerusalem, city of Israelite prophets, shows that prophetic inheritance has passed to him. Jerusalem was first qiblah, direction of prayer, for Muslims, before change to Makkah in 2:144. The journey therefore links Makkah and Jerusalem, showing continuity and transfer. Al-Qurṭubī notes that taking servant by night from Sacred Mosque to Farthest Mosque indicates that both mosques are sacred and connected, and that blessings of Jerusalem's surroundings are linked to Makkah's sanctity. The journey also demonstrates that Allah's power is not limited by distance or time: He can take servant by night across vast distance, just as He can show signs, punish corrupt, and guide to most upright as verse 9 will say.

**The Historical Context Of Year Of Sorrow**

Most authorities date isrā' to around 619 CE, Year of Sorrow, ʿĀm al-Ḥuzn, when Prophet lost wife Khadījah and uncle Abū Ṭālib. After their loss, persecution intensified, and Prophet sought support in Ṭā'if but was rejected and stoned. The Night Journey came after these losses as divine consolation and elevation. Al-Ṭabarī and Ibn Kathīr report that journey was physical, bodily, not only spiritual, though some early authorities said it was spiritual. Mainstream holds it was bodily, with Burāq as mount, Jibrīl as guide, and that Prophet returned same night, with door still swinging. The event is foundational for Islamic spirituality and is commemorated as Laylat al-Isrā' wa-l-Miʿrāj. A well-known hadith says prayer is ascension of believers, linking daily prayers to this journey.

**Linguistic Note On Subḥāna And Asrā**

Subḥāna from s-b-ḥ means to be free, far removed, to swim away from defect, from same root as tasbīḥ, glorification. Asrā from a-s-r means to travel by night, form IV, to take by night. ʿAbd from ʿ-b-d means servant, slave, worshipper. Laylan, by night, accusative indicating time and emphasis that journey accomplished in night. Masjid from s-j-d, place of prostration. Ḥarām from ḥ-r-m, sacred, forbidden. Aqṣā from q-ṣ-w, farthest. Bāraknā from b-r-k, to bless, increase. Ḥawlahu, around it, surroundings. Li-nuriyahu, to show him, from r-ʾ-y, to see. Āyāt from a-y-y, signs. Samīʿ from s-m-ʿ, hearing, Baṣīr from b-ṣ-r, seeing. The verse's structure — glorification, act of taking servant by night, from Sacred to Farthest Mosque whose surroundings blessed, purpose to show signs, seal with All-Hearing All-Seeing — moves from transcendence to act to places to purpose to divine attributes, creating opening that is both awe-inspiring and purposeful, setting stage for history of Children of Israel and moral code that follows.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:2

> **And We gave Moses the Scripture and made it a guide for the Children of Israel, ˹stating:˺ “Do not take besides Me any other Trustee of Affairs,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Moses Given Scripture As Guide**

Wa-ātaynā Mūsā al-kitāba wa-jaʿalnāhu hudan li-banī isrā'īl — and We gave Moses the Scripture and made it a guide for Children of Israel. Ātaynā from a-t-y means We gave. Mūsā, Moses, prophet who led Israelites from Egypt. Al-kitāb, the Scripture, Torah, containing law and guidance. Wa-jaʿalnāhu hudan, We made it a guide, from h-d-y, to guide. Li-banī isrā'īl, for Children of Israel, descendants of Yaʿqūb. Al-Ṭabarī explains that after mentioning Night Journey to Jerusalem, city of Israelite prophets, the sūrah mentions Moses and Torah as guide for Children of Israel, showing continuity of guidance from Moses to Muḥammad. The Torah was guide for Israelites as Qur'an is guide for all, as verse 9 will say this Qur'an guides to most upright. The connection between isrā' and Moses is also that Moses was shown signs, as Muḥammad was shown signs, and both faced corruption of their peoples.

**Do Not Take Besides Me Any Trustee**

Allā tattakhidhū min dūnī wakīlā — that you not take besides Me any trustee, guardian, disposer of affairs. Wakīl from w-k-l means trustee, one entrusted with affairs, disposer. Min dūnī, besides Me, apart from Me. Al-Ṭabarī says meaning is do not take besides Allah any trustee to whom you entrust affairs, whom you worship, rely upon, as protector besides Allah. This is same message as in previous chapter: worship Allah and shun ṭāghūt, do not take two gods. For Children of Israel, this meant not taking idols or tyrants as trustees besides Allah. Al-Rāzī notes that wakīl here includes both deity and guardian: do not take any as god or as ultimate trustee of affairs besides Allah. The prohibition allā tattakhidhū is prohibition that is core of tawḥīd: do not take besides Allah any trustee.

**Why This Command Follows Night Journey**

Night Journey took Prophet to Jerusalem, city of Israelites, and showed him signs. Then mentioning Moses and Torah as guide for Israelites shows that Jerusalem's blessed surroundings were blessed because many prophets worshipped there and received revelation. This prohibition against taking besides Him any trustee was given to Israelites via Torah, but they later violated by causing corruption twice, as verse 4 will say. The sūrah therefore uses Israelite history as lesson: they were given Scripture as guide and commanded not to take trustee besides Allah, but they corrupted, so they were punished. The lesson for Makkans is similar: you are given Qur'an as guide, commanded not to take two gods, so do not corrupt like Israelites did.

**The Continuity Of Guidance**

We gave Moses Scripture and made it guide for Children of Israel — this shows that guidance is not new; it has history. Every community had messenger, as 16:36 said, with message worship Allah and shun false gods. Moses' message to Israelites was same: do not take besides Me any trustee. The Qur'an's message to Arabs is same: do not take two gods. The continuity shows that tawḥīd is original, universal, not particular. Al-Qurṭubī notes that mentioning Moses after isrā' also honors Moses, who was encountered by Prophet during ascension, where Moses advised Prophet to ask for reduction in prayers. The Night Journey therefore links Prophet to Moses personally, not only historically.

**A Lesson On Trusteeship**

Wakīl, trustee, is one to whom you entrust affairs, relying on him to manage. In worldly affairs, you may have trustee for business, but in ultimate affairs, trustee should be Allah alone. To take besides Allah any trustee as ultimate disposer is shirk. Al-Zamakhsharī explains that wakīl here means protector, helper, to whom you entrust your affairs, expecting help, provision, intercession. If you take idol as wakīl, you entrust to one who knows nothing, creates nothing, is dead, as previous chapter described idols. If you take Allah as wakīl, you entrust to One who hears, sees, owns heavens and earth, whose devotion is everlasting. The command therefore is to entrust ultimate affairs to Allah alone.

**Linguistic Note On Kitāb And Wakīl**

Ātaynā from a-t-y, to give. Mūsā, Moses. Al-kitāb, Scripture, from k-t-b, to write. Jaʿalnāhu from j-ʿ-l, to make. Hudan from h-d-y, guidance. Banī isrā'īl, Children of Israel, banī from b-n-y, sons, children. Allā tattakhidhū, that you not take, from a-kh-dh. Min dūnī, besides Me. Wakīl from w-k-l, trustee, disposer, from same root as tawakkul, trust. The verse's structure — We gave Moses Scripture and made it guide for Children of Israel, stating do not take besides Me any trustee — moves from giving to purpose to command, showing that Scripture was given as guide containing command of tawḥīd, which is core of all Scriptures.

**How This Verse Prepares For History Of Two Corruptions**

Verse 4 will warn Children of Israel in Scripture you will cause corruption twice and become extremely arrogant. The command not to take trustee besides Me is what they violated when they corrupted. Their corruption included taking others as trustees, worshipping idols, following tyrants, and becoming arrogant. The sūrah therefore sets up command then violation then punishment, as pattern for all communities. The reader is invited to see that same pattern applies to Makkans: given Qur'an as guide, commanded not to take two gods, if they corrupt and become arrogant, they will be punished as Israelites were. The verse therefore is both historical and warning.


**Why Torah As Guide Matters For Qur'an**

Torah as guide for Children of Israel matters for Qur'an because Qur'an confirms previous Scriptures and completes them. The Qur'an says *We sent down Torah containing guidance and light* (5:44). By mentioning Torah as guide, the sūrah shows that guidance has always been via Books, not only via signs, and that Qur'an is latest Book in that chain. The command not to take trustee besides Me is in Torah and Qur'an, showing that core message unchanged. The sūrah's mention of Torah also answers those who asked for Book: you were given Book before, Torah, and you corrupted, so being given Book alone does not guarantee guidance; you must follow its command not to take trustee besides Allah. The lesson is that receiving Scripture is blessing that requires gratitude and obedience, not arrogance.

**Practical Implications Of Taking Allah As Trustee**

Taking Allah as Trustee, wakīl, has practical implications: you rely on Him for provision, protection, and outcome, while taking permissible means. You do not rely on idols, stars, or tyrants as ultimate trustees. In daily life, this means you may have worldly trustees like agents, lawyers, but ultimate trust is in Allah. Al-Qurṭubī says tawakkul, trust, is to take Allah as wakīl, entrusting affairs to Him, as 16:42 said those who emigrated were patient and trusted Lord. This prohibition against taking besides Him any trustee therefore is command to have tawakkul in Allah alone for ultimate affairs, which is part of tawḥīd that brings good life and Gardens as reward.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:3

> **˹O˺ descendants of those We carried with Noah ˹in the Ark˺! He was indeed a grateful servant.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Descendants Of Those Carried With Noah**

Dhurriyyata man ḥamalnā maʿa Nūḥ — O progeny, descendants of those We carried with Noah. Dhurriyyah from dh-r-r means progeny, descendants, offspring. Man ḥamalnā maʿa Nūḥ, those We carried with Noah in Ark, meaning believers who were saved with Noah from flood, as 11:40 says *We said, carry in it of each kind two, and your family, and those who believed*. The address dhurriyyata man ḥamalnā maʿa Nūḥ is to Children of Israel, reminding them that they are descendants of those saved with Noah, not of those drowned. Al-Ṭabarī explains that this is to remind them of Allah's favor in saving their ancestors, so they should be grateful and not take trustee besides Him. The phrase is vocative, O descendants, calling them to remember origin. The brackets ˹O˺ and ˹in the Ark˺ in translation clarify vocative and context: they are descendants of those carried with Noah in Ark.

**He Was Indeed A Grateful Servant**

Innahu kāna ʿabdan shakūran — indeed he was a grateful servant. Huwa refers to Noah, Nūḥ. ʿAbdan, servant, same word used for Muḥammad in verse 1, ʿabdihi, His servant, showing that highest honor is servanthood. Shakūran, grateful, thankful, intensive form indicating abundant gratitude. Al-Ṭabarī says Noah was grateful servant because he thanked Allah for blessings, obeyed commands, and was patient in calling his people for 950 years. Al-Qurṭubī notes that shakūr is one who shows gratitude with heart, tongue, and limbs, abundantly. Noah's gratitude is mentioned here to set example for his descendants, Children of Israel, who are being told not to take trustee besides Allah and who will be warned about two corruptions. If ancestor Noah was grateful servant, descendants should be grateful too.

**Why Mention Noah After Moses**

Verse 2 mentioned Moses given Scripture as guide for Children of Israel, commanded not to take trustee besides Allah. Verse 3 adds O descendants of those We carried with Noah, He was indeed grateful servant. The connection is that Children of Israel are descendants of those saved with Noah, and Noah was grateful servant, so they should follow his gratitude, not corruption. Al-Rāzī explains that mentioning Noah after Moses shows that Israelite lineage goes back to those saved with Noah, not only to Jacob, and that gratitude is ancient prophetic quality. The sūrah's theme of gratitude appears: Noah was shakūr, grateful, while many humans are hasty and ungrateful, as verse 11 will say human prays for evil as for good and is ever hasty, and as previous chapter said most are ungrateful.

**The Significance Of Being Carried With Noah**

Being carried with Noah in Ark is great favor: saved from flood that drowned all disbelievers. Al-Ṭabarī says those carried with Noah were believers, few, who were saved because they believed and followed Noah. Their descendants, Children of Israel, are therefore descendants of saved believers, not of drowned. This should make them grateful and obedient, not corrupt and arrogant. The phrase ḥamalnā, We carried, indicates Allah's action in saving, not their own power. As with Night Journey where Allah took servant by night, here Allah carried believers with Noah. Both are acts of divine carrying that show power and mercy. The verse therefore reminds Israelites that their existence is due to divine carrying and saving, so they should not take trustee besides Him.

**Grateful Servant As Model**

ʿAbdan shakūran, grateful servant, is model for all servants. Noah is described as grateful servant in Qur'an in several places, as in 17:3 and implied in 11:48. His gratitude included thanking Allah after being saved, as 23:28 says *When you and those with you are settled in Ark, say Praise be to Allah who saved us from wrongdoing people* (23:28). His gratitude also included obedience in building Ark despite mockery, patience in calling people, and trust in Allah. Al-Zamakhsharī notes that shakūr is intensive, indicating Noah was constantly grateful, not occasionally. For Children of Israel who were given Torah as guide and commanded not to take trustee besides Allah, Noah's example of constant gratitude is relevant: they should be grateful for Torah and for being descendants of saved, not corrupt.

**Linguistic Note On Dhurriyyah And Shakūr**

Dhurriyyah from dh-r-r means progeny, descendants, from same root as dharr, small ants, indicating many small offspring. Man ḥamalnā, those We carried, from ḥ-m-l, to carry, same root as ḥaml, carrying, as in cattle bearing loads. Maʿa Nūḥ, with Noah. Innahu kāna ʿabdan shakūran, indeed he was grateful servant, with innahu for emphasis, kāna for past, ʿabdan for servant, shakūran intensive form of shākir, grateful, from sh-k-r, gratitude. The verse's structure — O descendants of those We carried with Noah, indeed he was grateful servant — with vocative and statement, calls Israelites to remember ancestry and to follow ancestor's gratitude, preparing for warning about two corruptions that will come because they did not follow gratitude.

**How This Verse Connects To Sūrah's Themes**

Sūrah al-Isrā' themes include Night Journey, Israelite history, Qur'an guiding to most upright, and moral code. This verse connects Night Journey to Israelite history via Noah, showing that all prophetic history is linked: Noah saved, Moses given Scripture, Muḥammad taken by night to Jerusalem, all part of one divine plan. The mention of grateful servant also connects to verse 9 which says Qur'an gives good news to believers who do good that they will have mighty reward, and to verse 19 about striving for Hereafter appreciated. Grateful servanthood leads to mighty reward and appreciated striving. The verse therefore is not only historical note but moral call: be grateful servant like Noah, not corrupt like those who will cause corruption twice.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:4

> **And We warned the Children of Israel in the Scripture, “You will certainly cause corruption in the land twice, and you will become extremely arrogant.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Warning In Scripture About Two Corruptions**

Wa-qaḍaynā ilā banī isrā'īla fī al-kitābi la-tufsidunna fī al-arḍi marratayni — and We decreed, warned, to Children of Israel in Scripture, you will certainly cause corruption in land twice. Qaḍaynā from q-ḍ-y means to decree, decide, inform, warn, from same root as qaḍā', decree. Ilā banī isrā'īl, to Children of Israel. Fī al-kitāb, in Scripture, meaning Torah, or Preserved Tablet, or Scripture generally. La-tufsidunna, you will certainly cause corruption, from f-s-d, to corrupt, with la- and nun for emphasis, surely. Fī al-arḍi, in land, meaning Holy Land, Jerusalem and surroundings, or land generally. Marratayni, twice, two times. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Allah decreed in Torah that Children of Israel would cause corruption twice, meaning they would disobey, commit sins, kill prophets, and cause mischief. The warning was in Scripture to make them aware and to establish proof.

**Becoming Extremely Arrogant**

Wa-la-taʿlunna ʿuluwwan kabīran — and you will become extremely arrogant, exalt yourselves with great arrogance. Taʿlunna from ʿ-l-w, to be high, exalt, with la- and nun for emphasis, you will surely become high, arrogant. ʿUluwwan kabīran, great highness, great arrogance, from ʿ-l-w and k-b-r, great. Al-Ṭabarī says ʿuluwwan kabīran means great arrogance, tyranny, exceeding bounds, claiming greatness, oppressing others. Al-Rāzī explains that corruption and arrogance are linked: corruption in land is outward mischief, arrogance is inward attitude that leads to corruption. The Israelites' arrogance included claiming they are chosen and can do whatever, killing prophets, and attributing to Allah what does not befit.

**Why Two Corruptions Are Mentioned**

Two corruptions indicate that Israelite history has pattern: corruption, punishment, repentance, restoration, then corruption again, punishment again, then possibility of mercy. Al-Ṭabarī reports various opinions on what two corruptions were: some say first was killing of prophet like Shuʿayb or Zakariyyā, and second was killing of Yaḥyā and ʿĪsā's attempted killing; others say first was corruption that led to Nebuchadnezzar's destruction of Jerusalem, second was corruption that led to Roman destruction. Al-Qurṭubī says first corruption was when they killed prophets and were punished by Nebuchadnezzar who destroyed Temple and enslaved them, second was when they killed again and were punished by Romans under Titus. The exact identification differs, but principle is that they corrupted twice and were punished twice, showing divine law that corruption leads to punishment.

**The Purpose Of Warning**

Wa-qaḍaynā, We decreed, warned, indicates that warning was given in advance, in Scripture, so that they would avoid corruption, but they did not. The warning serves as proof that Allah knows future and warns, and that punishment is not arbitrary but after warning. The same pattern applies to Makkans: they are warned via Qur'an that if they cause corruption and become arrogant, they will be punished as Israelites were. The sūrah's mention of two corruptions therefore is both history and warning for current community. Al-Zamakhsharī notes that la-tufsidunna and la-taʿlunna with emphatic la- and nun indicate certainty: you will certainly corrupt and become arrogant, because Allah knows their nature, but warning still given to establish proof and to invite repentance.

**Corruption And Arrogance As Linked Sins**

Fasād, corruption, and ʿuluww, arrogance, are often paired in Qur'an as root sins that lead to destruction, as in 28:83: *That home of Hereafter We assign to those who do not desire exaltation in land nor corruption* (28:83). Corruption is outward harm to land and people, arrogance is inward claim of superiority that justifies corruption. For Israelites, corruption included killing prophets, shedding blood, and oppression; arrogance included claiming they are above law. For Makkans, corruption included persecuting believers and plotting against Prophet, arrogance included saying we are too great to follow human messenger. The verse therefore diagnoses that corruption and arrogance go together and lead to punishment.

**Linguistic Note On Qaḍā' And Fasād**

Qaḍaynā from q-ḍ-y means to decree, decide, judge, inform, from same root as qaḍā', decree, judgment. Fī al-kitāb, in Scripture, from k-t-b. La-tufsidunna from f-s-d, to corrupt, spoil, with la- for oath and nun for emphasis. Fī al-arḍi, in land, from a-r-ḍ. Marratayni, twice, dual of marrah, time, occurrence. Wa-la-taʿlunna from ʿ-l-w, to be high, exalt, with la- and nun. ʿUluwwan from ʿ-l-w, highness, exaltation. Kabīran from k-b-r, great. The verse's structure — We decreed to Children of Israel in Scripture you will corrupt twice and become extremely arrogant — with decree, address, location, two sins, creates warning that is both specific, twice, and general, corruption and arrogance, showing that divine knowledge includes future and that warning is given to prevent.

**How This Verse Prepares For Punishment And Mercy**

Verses 5-8 will detail punishment for first corruption, restoration, second corruption punishment, and possibility of mercy if they repent. This verse is introduction: you will corrupt twice and become extremely arrogant. It sets up history that will be detailed next, showing that divine law is consistent: corruption and arrogance lead to punishment via servants of great might who ravage homes, then restoration with wealth and offspring, then if you do good it is for yourselves, if you do wrong it is against yourselves, then second punishment with disgrace and Temple entry and destruction, then perhaps mercy if you repent, but if you return to sin, punishment returns, and Hell is confinement for disbelievers. The verse therefore is not only about Israelites but about universal law that applies to all communities, including Makkans.


**Why The Warning Is In The Scripture**

Fī al-kitāb, in the Scripture, indicates that warning was written, recorded, not only oral, to be preserved and to establish proof. Al-Qurṭubī says al-kitāb here can mean Torah, which contained warning about two corruptions, or Preserved Tablet, al-lawḥ al-maḥfūẓ, where all decrees written. In either case, warning in Scripture shows that Allah's knowledge and decree precede events, and that humans are warned via revelation. The Israelites had warning in their own Scripture, yet they corrupted, showing that having Scripture alone does not prevent corruption; following its commands does. The same applies to Makkans who have Qur'an as guide to most upright as verse 9 will say: having Qur'an does not prevent corruption unless followed.

**A Lesson On Arrogance As Root**

ʿUluwwan kabīran, great arrogance, is described as root of corruption. Arrogance makes person think he is above law, above accountability, so he corrupts. For Israelites, arrogance was thinking they are chosen people who will not be punished, or that they can kill prophets without consequence. For Makkans, arrogance was thinking they are too great to follow human messenger, or that their idols will protect them. The sūrah's repeated condemnation of arrogance, mustakbirīn, in previous chapter and in this chapter's verses about those who do not believe in Hereafter having denying hearts and being arrogant, shows that arrogance is central sin that prevents acceptance of truth. The remedy is humility, being humble with parents out of mercy as verse 24 will say, and not walking exultantly in land as verse 37 will say.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:5

> **“When the first of the two warnings would come to pass, We would send against you some of Our servants of great might, who would ravage your homes. This would be a warning fulfilled.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**First Warning Fulfilled Via Servants Of Great Might**

Fa-idhā jā'a waʿdu ūlāhumā baʿathnā ʿalaykum ʿibādan lanā ulī ba'sin shadīdin fa-jāsū khilāla al-diyār — then when first of two warnings came to pass, We sent against you servants of Ours of great might who ravaged through homes. Fa-idhā, then when, indicates time of first corruption. Jā'a waʿdu ūlāhumā, first warning came to pass, meaning first time they corrupted, promise of punishment came true. Baʿathnā ʿalaykum, We sent against you, from b-ʿ-th, to send, raise. ʿIbādan lanā, servants of Ours, meaning people, soldiers, who are servants in sense of being under Allah's control, not necessarily believers, but used as instrument. Ulī ba'sin shadīdin, possessors of great might, severe violence, from b-ʾ-s, might, and sh-d-d, severe. Fa-jāsū khilāla al-diyār, they ravaged through homes, searched, went through houses, from j-w-s, to go through, search, ravage, and diyār, homes, houses.

**Who Were Servants Of Great Might**

Al-Ṭabarī reports various opinions: some say they were Nebuchadnezzar and his army who destroyed Jerusalem and Temple and enslaved Israelites; others say they were people of Iraq, or Persians, or others. The exact identity is not crucial; what matters is they were ulī ba'sin shadīdin, of great might, severe, who ravaged homes, showing that first corruption was punished by foreign invasion that destroyed homes and Temple. Al-Qurṭubī says first punishment was Nebuchadnezzar's destruction of Jerusalem in 586 BCE, when Temple destroyed and Israelites taken to Babylon. This is historical event that matches description: servants of great might ravaging homes. The phrase ʿibādan lanā, servants of Ours, indicates that even disbelieving invaders are under Allah's control and used as punishment, as 8:17 says *You did not kill them but Allah killed them* (8:17), showing divine use of human agents.

**Ravaging Through Homes**

Fa-jāsū khilāla al-diyār — they went through homes, ravaged, searched. Jāsū from j-w-s means to go through, to search, to ravage, to move through with force. Khilāla al-diyār, through homes, between houses, indicating they entered homes, searched, destroyed, showing total defeat and humiliation. Al-Rāzī explains that ravaging through homes indicates that punishment reached private spaces, not only public, showing that corruption that was private and public both punished. The Israelites who corrupted in land and became arrogant had their private homes ravaged, showing that arrogance that made them feel secure in homes was broken.

**Warning Fulfilled**

Wa-kāna waʿdan mafʿūlan — and it was warning, promise fulfilled, done. Waʿd, promise, warning. Mafʿūl, done, fulfilled, from f-ʿ-l, to do. Al-Ṭabarī says this means first warning, promise of punishment for first corruption, was fulfilled, done, as decreed. The phrase indicates that divine warnings are not empty threats; they are fulfilled when corruption occurs. The same applies to second warning and to warnings to Makkans: if they corrupt and become arrogant, warning will be fulfilled. The phrase also shows that divine decree is certain: when condition of corruption occurs, punishment follows as fulfillment.

**Why First Corruption Punished Via Foreign Invasion**

First corruption of Israelites was punished via foreign invasion because corruption was in land and involved arrogance and oppression, so punishment came via people of great might who would ravage homes, showing that might they boasted was overcome by greater might. Al-Qurṭubī notes that using foreign invaders as punishment is divine law: when community corrupts, Allah sends against them others who are stronger, to humble them. This happened to Israelites twice, and happened to Makkans at Badr where they were defeated by believers who were fewer but aided by Allah. The lesson is that might belongs to Allah, not to arrogant.

**Connection To Night Journey And Blessed Surroundings**

First warning involved ravaging homes in blessed land whose surroundings We blessed as verse 1 said. The blessed land was blessed with fertility and prophets, but when its people corrupted, blessings did not prevent punishment; instead, homes in blessed land were ravaged. This shows that being in blessed land does not guarantee security; obedience does. The Israelites were in blessed land, yet when they corrupted, they were punished in that land. The Makkans were in Sacred Mosque, yet if they corrupt, they will be punished. The Night Journey from Sacred Mosque to Farthest Mosque in blessed surroundings therefore is reminder that sacred places require gratitude and obedience, not corruption and arrogance.

**Linguistic Note On Ba's And Jaws**

Ba's from b-ʾ-s means might, strength, violence, from same root as ba'sā', hardship. Shadīd from sh-d-d means severe, strong. Ulī ba'sin shadīdin, possessors of severe might, indicates great military power. Jāsū from j-w-s means to go through, search, ravage, from same root as jāsūs, spy, one who goes through. Khilāla from kh-l-l, through, between. Diyār from d-w-r, homes, houses, from same root as dār, home. Waʿdan mafʿūlan, promise fulfilled, from w-ʿ-d, promise, and f-ʿ-l, to do. The verse's structure — when first warning came, We sent against you servants of great might who ravaged homes, it was promise fulfilled — with time clause, divine sending, description of agents, action, and fulfillment, creates historical account that is both specific and universal, showing that first corruption was punished and warning fulfilled.

**How This Verse Teaches About Divine Law**

This verse teaches that divine law for corruption is consistent: when community corrupts and becomes arrogant, Allah sends against them servants of great might who ravage homes, as punishment and as fulfillment of warning. The law applies to Israelites and to all, including Makkans. The verse also teaches that even servants of great might who are used as punishment are servants of Allah, under His control, showing that all power belongs to Him. The believer should therefore not fear people of great might besides Allah, but fear Allah alone, as previous chapter said be in awe of Me alone and to Him belongs whatever in heavens and earth. The practical lesson is to avoid corruption and arrogance, to be grateful for being in blessed land or sacred mosque, and to know that warnings are fulfilled if corruption persists.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:6

> **Then ˹after your repentance˺ We would give you the upper hand over them and aid you with wealth and offspring, causing you to outnumber them.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Restoration After First Punishment**

Thumma radadnā lakum al-karrata ʿalayhim — then We gave you return, upper hand, over them. Thumma, then, after first punishment and after repentance. Radadnā from r-d-d, to return, give back. Lakum al-karrata, for you the return, upper hand, turn to prevail, from k-r-r, to return, to attack again. ʿAlayhim, over them, over enemies who ravaged homes. Al-Ṭabarī explains that after Israelites were punished by servants of great might who ravaged homes, and after they repented, Allah gave them return, upper hand over enemies, allowing them to return to Holy Land, rebuild Temple, and prevail. The phrase radadnā lakum al-karrata ʿalayhim indicates that victory and dominance were returned to Israelites after being taken away, showing that restoration is possible after repentance.

**Aid With Wealth And Offspring**

Wa-amdadnākum bi-amwālin wa-banīn — and We aided you with wealth and sons, offspring. Amdadnākum from m-d-d, to aid, extend, supply, from same root as madd, extension. Bi-amwālin, with wealth, money, property. Wa-banīn, and sons, offspring, children. Al-Ṭabarī says Allah aided Israelites after restoration with wealth and offspring, increasing their numbers and provision, as part of returning upper hand. Wealth and offspring are two main sources of strength and honor in ancient society: wealth for economy, sons for manpower and defense. Al-Rāzī notes that amwāl and banīn are mentioned together often in Qur'an as adornment of worldly life, as 18:46 says *Wealth and children are adornment of worldly life* (18:46). Their increase after punishment shows that repentance brings restoration of both material and human resources.

**Causing You To Outnumber Them**

Wa-jaʿalnākum akthara nafīran — and We made you more numerous, greater in manpower, outnumbering. Jaʿalnākum from j-ʿ-l, to make. Akthara, more, greater, from k-th-r, many. Nafīran from n-f-r, to go forth, to rally, manpower, number of people who go forth for war or support. Al-Ṭabarī explains akthara nafīran means more numerous in manpower, soldiers, supporters, outnumbering enemies. Al-Qurṭubī says nafīr means those who go forth to help, so more nafīr means more helpers, larger army, greater numbers. After being enslaved and reduced, Israelites were made more numerous, showing that Allah can increase numbers after decrease, as He can give upper hand after defeat.

**Why Restoration Follows Repentance**

Thumma radadnā lakum al-karrata ʿalayhim — then We gave you return over them — indicates that restoration came after repentance, as brackets ˹after your repentance˺ clarify. Al-Ṭabarī says Israelites repented after first punishment, returned to obedience, so Allah restored them. The pattern is: corruption, punishment, repentance, restoration with wealth and offspring and numbers. This pattern appears throughout Qur'an for many communities, showing that mercy follows repentance. The same pattern applies to Makkans: if they repent after warning, they will be given upper hand, as happened to some who became Muslims and were aided. The verse therefore offers hope: even after punishment, repentance leads to restoration.

**The Cycle Of History**

This verse describes part of cycle: first corruption punished, then restoration. Next verses will say if you do good it is for yourselves, if you do wrong it is against yourselves, then second corruption punished with disgrace and Temple entry and destruction, then perhaps mercy if you repent, but if you return to sin, punishment returns. The cycle shows that history is not random but governed by law: corruption leads to punishment, repentance leads to restoration, doing good benefits self, doing wrong harms self. Al-Rāzī explains that this cycle is to teach that divine law is consistent and that human choice determines where in cycle they are.

**Connection To Blessings And Gratitude**

Wealth and offspring as aid after restoration are blessings, as previous chapter listed wealth and offspring as blessings and as adornment of worldly life. The verse says We aided you with wealth and offspring, showing that restoration includes blessings, material and human, both needed for strength. The appropriate response is gratitude, not arrogance and second corruption, not using wealth and offspring to transgress. The Israelites were given upper hand, wealth, offspring, numbers after first punishment, but they later corrupted again, using blessings to become extremely arrogant, leading to second punishment with disgrace and Temple entry and utter destruction. The lesson is that blessings after restoration should lead to gratitude, shukr, and obedience, not to second corruption and arrogance. The sūrah's theme of gratitude appears: blessings are from Allah, should be thanked, not used for corruption, and grateful servant Noah was example.

**Why Numbers Matter**

Akthara nafīran — more numerous — shows that numbers matter after restoration, as community strength depends on manpower. After being reduced by punishment, being made more numerous indicates demographic recovery, which is mercy. The Israelites after Babylonian exile returned and multiplied, as history shows. The phrase akthara nafīran also indicates that upper hand requires numbers, and Allah made them more numerous to give them upper hand. This is part of restoration: not only return to land but increase in numbers so that they outnumber enemies who had ravaged homes.

**Linguistic Note On Karrat And Nafīr**

Karrat from k-r-r means return, attack, turn, from same root as karr, to return, attack again. In war context, karr means attack after retreat, so al-karrata ʿalayhim means return of attack, upper hand over them. Amdadnākum from m-d-d, to aid, extend, supply. Amwāl from m-w-l, wealth, property. Banīn from b-n-y, sons, offspring, from same root as ibn, son. Nafīr from n-f-r, to go forth, rally, from same root as nafara, to go forth, and nafr, manpower. Akthara nafīran, more numerous in manpower, indicates greater numbers. The verse's structure — then We gave you return over them and aided you with wealth and offspring and made you more numerous — with three aids, shows comprehensive restoration: dominance, wealth, offspring, numbers, all from Allah.

**How This Verse Teaches About Restoration**

This verse teaches that restoration after punishment is possible via repentance and that restoration includes upper hand, wealth, offspring, and numbers. It shows that Allah's mercy includes giving second chance after first punishment. The practical lesson is to repent after sin and to use restoration with gratitude, not to return to corruption. For Makkans who were being warned, it offers hope: if you repent and follow Qur'an that guides to most upright, you will be given upper hand and aid, as believers were after emigration given good home in world. The verse also warns that restoration is not guarantee against second corruption; if you corrupt again, second punishment will come, more severe, with disgrace and Temple entry.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:7

> **If you act rightly, it is for your own good, but if you do wrong, it is to your own loss. And when the second warning would come to pass, your enemies would ˹be left to˺ totally disgrace you and enter the Temple ˹of Jerusalem˺ as they entered it the first time, and utterly destroy whatever would fall into their hands.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**If You Do Good, For Yourselves; If You Do Wrong, Against Yourselves**

In aḥsantum aḥsantum li-anfusikum wa-in asa'tum fa-lahā — if you do good, you do good for yourselves, if you do wrong, it is against yourselves, for yourselves. Aḥsantum from ḥ-s-n, to do good, excellence. Li-anfusikum, for yourselves, for your own souls. Asa'tum from s-w-ʾ, to do wrong, evil. Fa-lahā, then for it, against it, meaning against yourselves, your own loss. Al-Ṭabarī explains that doing good benefits doer, not Allah, because Allah is free from need; doing wrong harms doer, not Allah. This principle appears throughout Qur'an, as in 41:46: *Whoever does righteousness, it is for his own soul, and whoever does evil, it is against it* (41:46). The verse is placed after restoration with upper hand, wealth, offspring, numbers, to remind that restoration is opportunity: if you do good after restoration, it benefits you; if you do wrong, it harms you. The choice determines next stage.

**Second Warning And Total Disgrace**

Fa-idhā jā'a waʿdu al-ākhirati li-yasū'ū wujūhakum — then when second warning comes to pass, to disgrace your faces, to make faces gloomy. Fa-idhā, then when, indicating second corruption. Jā'a waʿdu al-ākhirati, second warning, last warning came to pass. Li-yasū'ū wujūhakum, to disgrace your faces, make faces bad, gloomy, from s-w-ʾ, evil, bad, and w-j-h, face. Al-Ṭabarī says li-yasū'ū wujūhakum means enemies will disgrace you, make your faces gloomy with humiliation, as happened when Romans destroyed Jerusalem second time. Al-Qurṭubī says second punishment was by Romans under Titus in 70 CE, who destroyed Second Temple, killed many, enslaved others, causing faces to be disgraced.

**Entering Temple As First Time**

Wa-li-yadkhulū al-masjida kamā dakhalūhu awwala marratin — and to enter Temple as they entered it first time. Yadkhulū, they enter, from d-kh-l. Al-masjid, Temple, Mosque, meaning Temple in Jerusalem, Farthest Mosque. Kamā dakhalūhu awwala marratin, as they entered it first time, meaning as first invaders entered and destroyed Temple first time, second invaders will enter similarly. Al-Ṭabarī explains that second entry to Temple was by Romans who entered and destroyed as Nebuchadnezzar did first time. The phrase shows pattern: first corruption punished by entry and destruction of Temple, second corruption punished similarly, showing that sacred place does not protect corrupt people.

**Utterly Destroy Whatever Falls Into Hands**

Wa-li-yutabbirū mā ʿalaw tatbīran — and to utterly destroy whatever they rise over, whatever falls into their hands, with total destruction. Yutabbirū from t-b-r, to destroy, crush, from same root as tabār, destruction. Mā ʿalaw, whatever they rise over, overcome, dominate. Tatbīran, utterly, with emphasis, complete destruction. Al-Ṭabarī says this means second invaders will utterly destroy whatever they overcome, including buildings, people, etc., showing severity of second punishment. Al-Rāzī notes that tatbīran indicates total, not partial, destruction, fitting second corruption that was worse or repeated.

**Why Second Punishment More Severe**

Second punishment includes disgracing faces, entering Temple as first time, and utter destruction, which is more severe than first that was ravaging homes, scouring houses. The increase in severity shows that repeated corruption leads to greater punishment, as sin after being warned and restored is worse than first sin. Al-Qurṭubī says second punishment was more humiliating because it included disgracing faces, li-yasū'ū wujūhakum, indicating public humiliation, faces made gloomy with shame, not only private homes ravaged. Romans under Titus in 70 CE entered Temple, destroyed it, killed, enslaved, causing faces to be disgraced, as history records. The pattern warns that if you are restored after first punishment and you corrupt again, second punishment will be worse, with total disgrace and Temple entry and utter destruction, showing that repeated rejection after mercy leads to severe consequence.

**A Universal Principle Of Self-Benefit And Self-Harm**

In aḥsantum li-anfusikum wa-in asa'tum fa-lahā — if you do good for yourselves, if you do wrong against yourselves — is universal principle that applies to all, including Israelites and Makkans. Al-Rāzī explains that doing good benefits self in world and Hereafter, as verse 9 will say Qur'an gives good news to believers who do good of mighty reward, and doing wrong harms self in world and Hereafter, as verse 10 will say those who do not believe in Hereafter have painful punishment. The principle also appears in previous chapter: those who do good in world have goodness, but Hereafter better. The verse therefore teaches responsibility: your deeds return to you.

**Connection To Night Journey And Temple**

Second warning involves entering Temple, al-masjid, same word as Farthest Mosque, al-masjid al-aqṣā, in verse 1. The Temple that was entered and destroyed twice is same Temple whose surroundings blessed and to which Prophet was taken by night. The connection shows that Temple's sanctity does not prevent punishment of corrupt inhabitants; instead, its entry by enemies is part of punishment. The Night Journey to Farthest Mosque therefore is not only honor for Prophet but also reminder that sacred places require obedience. The Temple entered twice by invaders will be visited by Prophet in Night Journey as honored servant, showing transfer of custodianship from corrupt to grateful servant.

**Linguistic Note On Sā'a And Tabbār**

Aḥsantum from ḥ-s-n, to do good. Asa'tum from s-w-ʾ, to do evil. Li-anfusikum, for yourselves, from n-f-s. Fa-lahā, then for it, against it. Jā'a waʿdu al-ākhirati, second warning came. Li-yasū'ū from s-w-ʾ, to make bad, disgrace. Wujūhakum, your faces, from w-j-h. Yadkhulū from d-kh-l, to enter. Al-masjid, Temple, from s-j-d. Kamā dakhalūhu awwala marratin, as they entered it first time, from d-kh-l and a-w-l and m-r-r. Yutabbirū from t-b-r, to destroy. Mā ʿalaw, what they rise over, from ʿ-l-w. Tatbīran, destruction, from t-b-r. The verse's structure — if you do good for yourselves, if you do wrong against yourselves, then when second warning comes, they will disgrace faces and enter Temple as first time and utterly destroy — with principle then history, teaches that deeds benefit or harm self and that second corruption leads to severe punishment.

**How This Verse Teaches Accountability**

This verse teaches accountability: your good is for you, your wrong against you. It removes idea that you can do good for Allah's benefit or wrong that harms Allah; Allah is free from need. It also teaches that second chances exist, as restoration after first punishment, but repeated corruption leads to worse punishment. The practical lesson is to do good after being given upper hand, wealth, offspring, numbers, not to become arrogant and corrupt again. For Makkans who were given many blessings and were being warned, it says if you do good after being warned, it is for your own good, if you do wrong, it is your own loss, and if you corrupt again after being given chance, punishment will be severe with disgrace and destruction.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:8

> **Perhaps your Lord will have mercy on you ˹if you repent˺, but if you return ˹to sin˺, We will return ˹to punishment˺. And We have made Hell a ˹permanent˺ confinement for the disbelievers.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Perhaps Your Lord Will Have Mercy If You Repent**

ʿAsā rabbukum an yarḥamakum — perhaps your Lord will have mercy on you. ʿAsā from ʿ-s-y means perhaps, maybe, indicating hope and possibility. Rabbukum, your Lord, your Nurturer. An yarḥamakum, that He will have mercy on you, from r-ḥ-m, mercy. Al-Ṭabarī explains that after describing two corruptions and punishments, Allah says perhaps your Lord will have mercy on you if you repent, return to obedience, abandon corruption and arrogance. The phrase ʿasā indicates that mercy is hoped for after repentance, not guaranteed without repentance, but with repentance it is expected because Allah is Merciful. Al-Qurṭubī says this was fulfilled when Israelites repented and were restored, and that it remains open: if they repent, mercy will come. The brackets ˹if you repent˺ clarify condition: mercy if you repent.

**If You Return To Sin, We Will Return To Punishment**

Wa-in ʿudttum ʿudnā — but if you return to sin, We will return to punishment. In ʿudttum, if you return, from ʿ-w-d, to return. ʿUdnā, We will return, from same root, indicating reciprocal return: if you return to corruption, We will return to punishment. Al-Ṭabarī says if you return to sin, corruption, arrogance, We will return to punishment, as happened second time and will happen again if you return. The phrase shows divine law of reciprocity: your return to sin leads to Our return to punishment. It is both warning and justice: you control whether mercy continues or punishment returns by whether you return to sin or not.

**Hell As Permanent Confinement For Disbelievers**

Wa-jaʿalnā jahannama li-l-kāfirīna ḥaṣīran — and We made Hell for disbelievers a confinement, prison, mat. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make. Jahannam, Hell. Li-l-kāfirīna, for disbelievers, those who cover truth. Ḥaṣīran from ḥ-ṣ-r, to confine, surround, from same root as ḥaṣīr, mat, prison, because mat confines and surrounds, or prison that confines. Al-Ṭabarī explains ḥaṣīran means confinement, prison, place that confines and surrounds disbelievers, from which they cannot escape. Al-Qurṭubī says ḥaṣīran means prison, mat that confines, or bed, indicating Hell is confinement, permanent prison for disbelievers. The brackets ˹permanent˺ clarify that confinement is permanent, not temporary.

**Why Mercy And Return Are Paired**

ʿAsā rabbukum an yarḥamakum, perhaps Lord will have mercy, and wa-in ʿudttum ʿudnā, if you return We will return, are paired to show that mercy and punishment both depend on human return: return to repentance leads to mercy, return to sin leads to punishment. Al-Rāzī explains that this pairing shows divine justice and mercy: mercy is offered after punishment if you repent, but if you return to sin, punishment returns, showing that you control outcome by your choice. The verse therefore offers hope and warning together: hope of mercy if you repent, warning of return to punishment if you return to sin.

**Hell As Confinement**

Jaʿalnā jahannama li-l-kāfirīna ḥaṣīran — We made Hell for disbelievers confinement. Ḥaṣīr originally means mat, but also prison, confinement, because it surrounds and confines. Al-Zamakhsharī says ḥaṣīran means prison, confinement that surrounds, indicating Hell surrounds disbelievers from all sides, confining them, as 29:54 says Hell will encompass disbelievers. The image of Hell as confinement contrasts with Gardens of Eternity that are spacious with rivers flowing. Confinement is fitting for those who were arrogant and sought to be high; they will be confined low. The phrase li-l-kāfirīna, for disbelievers, indicates that Hell as confinement is specifically for disbelievers who persist in disbelief and corruption.

**Connection To Two Corruptions History**

This verse concludes history of two corruptions: first corruption punished by servants of great might ravaging homes, then restoration with upper hand, wealth, offspring, numbers, principle if you do good for yourselves if you do wrong against yourselves, second corruption punished with disgracing faces and Temple entry and utter destruction, then perhaps mercy if you repent, if you return to sin We return to punishment, and Hell made confinement for disbelievers. The history shows cycle and ends with possibility of mercy and warning of return and final confinement. The lesson for Makkans is similar: you have been warned, you have been given Qur'an that guides to most upright, if you repent and follow, mercy, if you return to sin and corruption and arrogance, punishment returns, and Hell is confinement for disbelievers.

**Linguistic Note On ʿAsā And Ḥaṣīr**

ʿAsā from ʿ-s-y, perhaps, maybe, indicating hope. Rabbukum from r-b-b, Lord. An yarḥamakum from r-ḥ-m, mercy. Wa-in ʿudttum from ʿ-w-d, to return. ʿUdnā from same root, We return. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make. Jahannam, Hell. Li-l-kāfirīna, for disbelievers, from k-f-r. Ḥaṣīran from ḥ-ṣ-r, to confine, surround, from same root as ḥaṣīr, mat, prison. The verse's structure — perhaps Lord will have mercy if you repent, if you return to sin We return to punishment, We made Hell confinement for disbelievers — with hope, reciprocal warning, and final confinement, creates conclusion to Israelite history that is both hopeful and warning, guiding listener to choose repentance and mercy over return to sin and confinement.

**How This Verse Teaches About Mercy And Justice**

This verse teaches that mercy is possible after punishment if you repent, but justice requires that if you return to sin, punishment returns. It shows that divine mercy does not mean ignoring sin; it means giving chance to repent. If chance is used to return to sin, punishment returns, and final confinement is Hell for disbelievers. The practical lesson is to repent after sin, not to return to sin after being given mercy and restoration, and to know that Hell is confinement for those who persist in disbelief. For Makkans who were being warned, it says perhaps Lord will have mercy if you repent and follow Qur'an that guides to most upright, but if you return to corruption and arrogance and plotting, punishment returns, and Hell is permanent confinement.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:9

> **Surely this Quran guides to what is most upright, and gives good news to the believers—who do good—that they will have a mighty reward.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Qur'an Guides To Most Upright**

Inna hādhā al-qur'āna yahdī li-l-latī hiya aqwamu — indeed this Qur'an guides to what is most upright, most straight, most just. Inna for emphasis, surely. Hādhā al-qur'ān, this Qur'an, which was sent down as Reminder to Prophet to explain to people. Yahdī, guides, from h-d-y, to guide, same root as hudā. Li-l-latī hiya aqwamu, to what is most upright, most straight, from q-w-m, to stand, be upright, straight, just. Aqwam means most upright, most straight, most correct, superlative of qawīm, upright. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Qur'an guides to most upright way, most just, most correct in beliefs, morals, and actions, more upright than previous Scriptures that were also guides but have been altered or are specific to one community. Al-Rāzī says aqwam includes most upright in creed, tawḥīd, not taking trustee besides Allah, not taking two gods, and most upright in law, justice, grace, courtesy to relatives, forbidding indecency, etc., as verses 22-39 will detail as moral code that is most upright.

**Good News To Believers Who Do Good**

Wa-yubashshiru al-mu'minīna al-ladhīna yaʿmalūna al-ṣāliḥāti anna lahum ajran kabīran — and gives good news to believers who do good that they will have mighty reward. Yubashshiru from b-sh-r, to give good news, glad tidings, same root as bushshira for baby girl but here true good news. Al-mu'minīn, believers, those who believe. Al-ladhīna yaʿmalūna al-ṣāliḥāt, who do good deeds, righteous deeds, from ʿ-m-l and ṣ-l-ḥ, good. Anna lahum ajran kabīran, that they will have mighty, great reward, from a-j-r, reward, and k-b-r, great, mighty. Al-Ṭabarī says Qur'an gives good news to believers who do good that they will have mighty reward in Hereafter, Gardens of Eternity with rivers and whatever they desire as previous chapter described, and good home in world. The good news is for believers who do good, not only believe without deeds, showing that faith and deeds together lead to mighty reward.

**Why This Verse Follows Israelite History**

Verses 2-8 described Israelite history: given Torah as guide, commanded not to take trustee besides Allah, warned about two corruptions, punished, restored, principle good for self wrong against self, second punishment with disgrace and Temple entry, perhaps mercy if repent, if return to sin punishment returns, Hell confinement. Verse 9 says surely this Qur'an guides to most upright and gives good news to believers who do good of mighty reward. The connection is that after showing Israelite history of corruption and punishment despite having Torah as guide, the sūrah says this Qur'an guides to most upright, more upright than previous, and gives good news to believers who do good. It implies that Israelites had guide but corrupted; this Qur'an guides to most upright, so follow it to avoid their fate and to receive mighty reward. It also shows that Qur'an is not only warning like previous verses but also good news.

**What Is Most Upright**

Aqwam, most upright, includes most upright in creed, law, and character. In creed, most upright is tawḥīd: your God is One, do not take two gods, to Him belongs whatever in heavens and earth, all blessings from Him, so worship Him alone. In law, most upright is justice, grace, courtesy to relatives, honoring parents, giving relatives due, not spending wastefully, not being tight-fisted nor openhanded excessively, honoring covenant, not breaking oaths, etc., as verses 22-39 will detail. In character, most upright is humility, not walking exultantly, fearing Lord above, doing what commanded like angels, being grateful servant like Noah. Al-Qurṭubī says aqwam means most just, most correct, most balanced, without excess or deficiency, which is characteristic of Qur'anic guidance.

**Mighty Reward For Believers Who Do Good**

Ajran kabīran, mighty reward, is described in previous chapter as Gardens of Eternity with rivers, whatever they desire, good home, peace at death, etc., and in this chapter will be described as Paradise and good life. Al-Rāzī says kabīran, mighty, great, indicates reward is great in quantity, quality, and duration, beyond imagination. The good news is for al-mu'minīna al-ladhīna yaʿmalūna al-ṣāliḥāt, believers who do good deeds, showing that mighty reward is for those who combine faith and deeds, not faith alone without deeds or deeds without faith. The verse therefore encourages both belief and good deeds.

**Linguistic Note On Aqwam And Ajr**

Hādhā al-qur'ān, this Qur'an, from q-r-ʾ, to recite, read. Yahdī from h-d-y, to guide. Li-l-latī hiya aqwamu, to what is most upright, from q-w-m, to stand, upright, with aqwam superlative, most upright. Yubashshiru from b-sh-r, to give good news. Al-mu'minīn from a-m-n, believers. Yaʿmalūna al-ṣāliḥāt, do good deeds, from ʿ-m-l and ṣ-l-ḥ. Ajran from a-j-r, reward. Kabīran from k-b-r, great, mighty. The verse's structure — surely this Qur'an guides to most upright and gives good news to believers who do good of mighty reward — with two functions of Qur'an, guiding and giving good news, creates concise summary of Qur'an's role: guide to most upright way and bearer of good news of mighty reward for those who follow.

**How This Verse Connects To Sūrah's Purpose**

Sūrah al-Isrā' purpose includes showing that Qur'an guides to most upright after showing Israelite history of corruption despite having Torah. It also includes moral code that is most upright, as verses 22-39 will detail. This verse is thesis for moral code: Qur'an guides to most upright, so follow its commands about parents, relatives, spending, etc., which are most upright. It also connects to Night Journey: Prophet taken by night to show signs, then given Qur'an that guides to most upright, so that he may explain to people what was revealed and perhaps they will reflect as previous chapter said. The verse therefore is central to sūrah's message: follow Qur'an that guides to most upright to receive mighty reward and avoid punishment that came to those who corrupted.

**A Lesson On Guidance And Good News**

The verse teaches that Qur'an is both guidance and good news. Guidance to most upright way in beliefs, morals, actions, and good news of mighty reward for believers who do good. Al-Qurṭubī says guidance is for all, but good news is specifically for believers who do good, showing that guidance must be accepted and acted upon to receive good news. The believer who follows most upright way will have mighty reward in Hereafter and good life in world as previous chapter promised. The disbeliever who does not believe in Hereafter, as verse 10 will say, has painful punishment prepared. The verse therefore invites to be among believers who do good and receive good news of mighty reward.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:10

> **And ˹it warns˺ those who do not believe in the Hereafter ˹that˺ We have prepared for them a painful punishment.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Warning For Those Who Do Not Believe In Hereafter**

Wa-anna al-ladhīna lā yu'minūna bi-l-ākhirati aʿtadnā lahum ʿadhāban alīman — and that those who do not believe in Hereafter We have prepared for them painful punishment. Wa-anna, and that, connecting to previous verse: Qur'an guides to most upright and gives good news to believers who do good of mighty reward, and warns those who do not believe in Hereafter that painful punishment prepared. Al-ladhīna lā yu'minūna bi-l-ākhirati, those who do not believe in Hereafter, deny resurrection, Hour, accountability, as Makkan polytheists did who swore Allah will not raise dead and said when we are bones and ashes will we be raised new creation. Aʿtadnā from ʿ-t-d, to prepare, make ready, from same root as ʿatīd, prepared, ready, and ʿatad, preparation. Lahum, for them. ʿAdhāban alīman, painful punishment, from ʿ-dh-b, punishment, torment, and a-l-m, painful, aching, from alam, pain. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Qur'an warns those who do not believe in Hereafter that painful punishment prepared, both in world and Hereafter, as previous verses described disgrace, Temple entry, destruction, Hell confinement, and as later verses will describe Hell confinement for disbelievers. The brackets ˹it warns˺ and ˹that˺ clarify that Qur'an warns, as verb yubashshir, gives good news, implies and warns.

**Why Hereafter Belief Is Central**

Belief in Hereafter is central because it motivates doing good and avoiding evil, gives meaning to mighty reward and painful punishment. If no Hereafter, then mighty reward and painful punishment have no ultimate meaning, and guidance to most upright has no ultimate purpose beyond worldly. The sūrah opened with Night Journey showing signs, then history of Children of Isra'il showing worldly punishment and restoration, then Qur'an guiding to most upright, then pairing of good news and warning based on belief. Previous chapter also said those who disbelieve in Hereafter have denying hearts and are arrogant, and to those who disbelieve in Hereafter belongs evil qualities. Here, Qur'an guides to most upright and gives good news to believers who do good of mighty reward in Hereafter, and warns those who do not believe in Hereafter of painful punishment in Hereafter. The pairing shows two outcomes based on belief in Hereafter and doing good vs disbelief. Belief in Hereafter is therefore foundation for moral code that follows.

**Painful Punishment Prepared**

Aʿtadnā lahum ʿadhāban alīman — We prepared for them painful punishment. Preparation indicates punishment already ready, not to be created later, as Hell is already prepared, as 3:131 says *Fear Fire prepared for disbelievers* (3:131), and Gardens prepared for righteous as 3:133 says *Hasten to forgiveness from Lord and Garden as wide as heavens and earth prepared for righteous* (3:133). Al-Qurṭubī says aʿtadnā indicates that punishment is prepared in advance, waiting, as Gardens prepared for righteous, showing reality of Hereafter. The punishment is alīman, painful, indicating severe, not light, aching, from a-l-m, pain. The preparation is by Allah, showing that He is All-Aware, All-Seeing of sins as verse 17 will say sufficient Lord as All-Aware All-Seeing of sins of servants. The warning is part of Qur'an's guidance to most upright: it guides by showing both reward and punishment, promise and threat, so that people choose reward and avoid punishment.

**Connection To Previous Chapter's Warnings And This Sūrah's Moral Code**

Previous chapter warned about disgrace, Hell as evil home for arrogant, evil consequences overtaking, being overwhelmed by what ridiculed, Fire where abandoned, etc., and said to those who disbelieve in Hereafter belongs evil qualities. This verse continues warning but specifically for those who do not believe in Hereafter, linking disbelief in Hereafter to painful punishment prepared. The sūrah's moral code that will follow, about parents, relatives, spending, children, adultery, killing, orphan wealth, pledges, measuring, knowledge, arrogance, is most upright way that leads to mighty reward and avoids painful punishment. The verse therefore is transition from history and general guidance to specific moral code: Qur'an guides to most upright, which includes moral code, and warns those who do not believe in Hereafter of painful punishment if they do not follow most upright. The pairing of good news and warning is typical Qur'anic method to guide.

**Linguistic Note On Iʿtād And Alīm**

Aʿtadnā from ʿ-t-d, to prepare, make ready, from same root as ʿatād, preparation, and ʿatīd, prepared, ready, as in *Indeed We prepared for wrongdoers Fire* (18:29). Al-ladhīna lā yu'minūna, those who do not believe, from a-m-n, to believe, from amina, to be secure. Bi-l-ākhirati, in Hereafter, from a-kh-r, last, Hereafter, latter, opposite of ūlā, first. ʿAdhāban from ʿ-dh-b, punishment, torment, from ʿadhaba, to punish. Alīman from a-l-m, painful, aching, from alima, to be in pain, from alam, pain. The verse's structure — and that those who do not believe in Hereafter We prepared for them painful punishment — with anna for that, alladhīna for those, lā yu'minūna for do not believe, aʿtadnā for We prepared, ʿadhāban alīman for painful punishment, creates warning that is counterpart to good news in previous verse, showing two outcomes of Qur'an's guidance to most upright, mighty reward vs painful punishment.

**How This Verse Teaches About Consequences And Motivation**

This verse teaches that disbelief in Hereafter has consequence: painful punishment prepared, ready, waiting. It shows that Qur'an's guidance includes both promise and warning, good news and warning, targhīb and tarhīb, to motivate, encourage and deter. Al-Rāzī says good news for believers who do good encourages doing good, warning for those who do not believe in Hereafter deters disbelief and evil. Together they guide to most upright: belief in Hereafter and doing good leads to mighty reward, disbelief leads to painful punishment. The practical lesson is to believe in Hereafter and do good to receive mighty reward and avoid painful punishment, and to follow Qur'an that guides to most upright in all matters, including moral code that follows about parents, relatives, etc., which are most upright.

**A Lesson On Preparation For Hereafter**

Aʿtadnā, We prepared, indicates that both reward and punishment are prepared, ready, waiting, not hypothetical. Gardens prepared for righteous, Fire prepared for disbelievers, as Qur'an says. The preparation shows that Hereafter is real and near, as command at hand in previous chapter said *Command at hand, so do not hasten it* (16:1). The believer should therefore prepare for Hereafter by believing and doing good, not delay, not be hasty to pray for evil. The disbeliever who does not believe in Hereafter will find painful punishment prepared when it is too late to believe. The verse therefore calls to prepare now for Hereafter by following most upright guidance, believing in Hereafter, doing good, seeking mighty reward, avoiding painful punishment, and using night and day as signs, seeking bounty with gratitude, knowing calculation, and remembering that destiny bound to neck and record laid open will show whether believed and did good.

**Why Pairing Good News And Warning**

Pairing good news for believers who do good of mighty reward and warning for those who do not believe in Hereafter of painful punishment is rhetorical method to guide to most upright: it shows both paths, both ends, so that listener chooses better. Qur'an guides to what is most upright by showing most upright leads to mighty reward and deviation leads to painful punishment. The verse therefore is not only warning but guidance: believe in Hereafter, do good, receive mighty reward; disbelieve, painful punishment prepared. The choice is clear, and Qur'an explains everything in detail so that choice informed.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:11

> **And humans ˹swiftly˺ pray for evil as they pray for good. For humankind is ever hasty.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Praying For Evil As For Good**

Wa-yadʿu al-insānu bi-l-sharri duʿā'ahu bi-l-khayr — and human prays for evil as he prays for good, calls for evil as he calls for good. Yadʿu from d-ʿ-w, to call, pray, supplicate. Al-insān, human, generic, includes all. Bi-l-sharri, for evil, bad, from sh-r-r, evil. Duʿā'ahu bi-l-khayr, his supplication for good, prayer for good, from d-ʿ-w and kh-y-r, good. Al-Ṭabarī explains that human, out of haste, prays for evil as he prays for good: when angry, he prays against himself, children, wealth, with evil, like saying may Allah curse me, destroy me, etc., as he prays for good when in need. The phrase bi-l-sharri duʿā'ahu bi-l-khayr indicates that he prays for evil with same eagerness, swiftness, as he prays for good, without thinking. Al-Qurṭubī says this includes praying for punishment to be hastened, as Makkans said bring punishment if truthful, which is praying for evil as for good.

**Humankind Ever Hasty**

Wa-kāna al-insānu ʿajūlan — and human is ever hasty, hasty by nature. Kāna, was, is, indicates state. Al-insān, human. ʿAjūlan from ʿ-j-l, hasty, hastening, from same root as ʿajala, haste, and istiʿjāl, seeking haste, as previous chapter said command at hand so do not hasten it, and man was created of haste as 21:37 says. Al-Ṭabarī says human is hasty in seeking things before their time, praying for evil as for good out of haste, not reflecting. Al-Rāzī explains that haste is part of human makeup that needs training: to be patient, not hasty, to reflect before praying. The verse diagnoses human weakness that leads to praying for evil: haste.

**Why This Verse Follows Good News And Warning**

Verses 9-10 said Qur'an guides to most upright, gives good news to believers who do good of mighty reward, and warns those who do not believe in Hereafter of painful punishment. Verse 11 says human prays for evil as for good, is ever hasty. The connection is that after hearing good news and warning, human may out of haste pray for evil, like asking for punishment to be hastened, or praying against self when angry, which is evil. The sūrah therefore warns against haste that leads to praying for evil. Previous chapter opened with do not hasten command at hand, and said man was created of haste. This verse continues theme: human is ever hasty, so he prays for evil as for good. The remedy is patience, reflection, and following most upright guidance that teaches what is good to pray for.

**The Meaning Of Praying For Evil**

Praying for evil includes several forms: praying against self, family, wealth when angry; praying for punishment to be hastened out of mockery; praying for worldly gain that is actually evil for Hereafter; praying for evil for others out of envy. All are due to haste and lack of reflection. Al-Zamakhsharī notes that yadʿu bi-l-sharri indicates he calls for evil, supplicates for evil, thinking it is good or out of anger, without realizing it is evil. The phrase duʿā'ahu bi-l-khayr, his prayer for good, indicates that he prays for good with eagerness, but also prays for evil with same eagerness, without distinguishing. The cure is to be mindful, to reflect before praying, to follow Qur'an that guides to most upright, which teaches what is good to pray for.

**Connection To Haste And Command At Hand**

Previous chapter said command at hand so do not hasten it, and man was created of haste. This verse says human prays for evil as for good, is ever hasty. The connection is that haste leads to praying for evil, like asking for command, punishment, Hour to be hastened, which is evil because if hastened, no time to repent. The prohibition do not hasten command is to prevent praying for evil out of haste. The verse therefore explains why prohibition needed: because human is hasty and would pray for evil as for good. The remedy is patience, as verses about emigrants being patient and trusting Lord, and as final verses about patience with Allah's help.

**Linguistic Note On Duʿā' And ʿAjūl**

Yadʿu from d-ʿ-w, to call, pray, supplicate. Al-insān, human, from a-n-s, to be familiar, or n-s-y, to forget, indicating human forgets. Bi-l-sharri, for evil, from sh-r-r. Duʿā'ahu, his supplication, from d-ʿ-w. Bi-l-khayr, for good, from kh-y-r. Kāna al-insānu ʿajūlan, human is ever hasty, from ʿ-j-l, haste, with ʿajūl intensive form, ever hasty, very hasty. The verse's structure — human prays for evil as he prays for good, human is ever hasty — with two clauses, first describing action, second explaining nature, diagnoses weakness and invites training to be patient and to pray for good, not evil.

**How This Verse Teaches Patience And Mindful Supplication**

This verse teaches patience and mindful supplication: do not pray for evil out of haste, anger, or mockery; pray for good with reflection. Al-Rāzī says believer should be patient, not hasty, and should reflect before supplicating, asking whether what he asks is truly good for world and Hereafter. The sūrah's moral code that follows includes honoring parents, giving relatives due, not spending wastefully, etc., which are most upright ways that are good to pray for and do. The verse therefore calls to follow most upright guidance that teaches what is good, to be patient, not hasty, and to supplicate for good that benefits self and others in world and Hereafter.

**A Lesson On Human Nature**

Human is ever hasty — this is statement about human nature that is both description and warning. Haste leads to praying for evil as for good, to demanding punishment hastened, to making decisions without reflection. The Qur'an often mentions haste as weakness to be trained: *Man was created of haste, I will show you My signs, so do not hasten Me* (21:37) and *Man prays for evil as for good, man is ever hasty* (17:11). The remedy is patience, reflection, and trust, as emigrants were patient and trusted Lord. The believer who recognizes haste in self can train to be patient, to reflect, to follow most upright guidance, and to pray for good that is truly good.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:12

> **We made the day and night as two signs. So We made the sign of the night devoid of light, and We made the sign of the day ˹perfectly˺ bright, so that you may seek the bounty of your Lord and know the number of years and calculation ˹of time˺. And We have explained everything in detail.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Day And Night As Two Signs**

Wa-jaʿalnā al-layla wa-l-nahāra āyatayni — and We made night and day as two signs. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make. Al-layla, night, and al-nahāra, day, as two signs, āyatayn, dual of āyah, sign, proof. Al-Ṭabarī explains that night and day are two signs of Allah's power, creation, and wisdom, as previous chapter said He subjected for your benefit night and day, sun and moon, stars by His command, signs for those who understand. Night and day as signs indicate they point to Creator, show His power to alternate, and serve human needs. The dual āyatayni emphasizes they are two distinct signs that work together.

**Sign Of Night Devoid Of Light, Sign Of Day Bright**

Fa-maḥawnā āyata al-layli wa-jaʿalnā āyata al-nahāri mubṣiratan — so We made sign of night devoid of light, erased, and made sign of day bright, giving sight. Maḥawnā from m-ḥ-w, to erase, blot out, make devoid, from same root as maḥw, erasure. Āyata al-layl, sign of night. Mubṣiratan, bright, giving sight, making visible, from b-ṣ-r, to see, to be bright. Al-Ṭabarī says We made night dark, devoid of light, so that you rest, and day bright so that you see and seek bounty. The erasure of night's light and making day bright are deliberate acts that show power and wisdom: night for rest and concealment, day for work and seeking bounty. Al-Rāzī explains that maḥw of night indicates that night originally could have been bright but was made dark as sign, and day made bright as sign, showing that both darkness and light are created and controlled.

**Seeking Bounty And Knowing Years And Calculation**

Li-tabtaghū faḍlan min rabbikum wa-li-taʿlamū ʿadada al-sinīna wa-l-ḥisāb — so that you may seek bounty from Lord and know number of years and calculation. Li-tabtaghū from b-gh-y, to seek, desire. Faḍlan, bounty, provision, from f-ḍ-l, bounty. Min rabbikum, from Lord. Wa-li-taʿlamū, and to know, from ʿ-l-m. ʿAdada al-sinīna, number of years, from ʿ-d-d and s-n-w, years. Wa-l-ḥisāb, and calculation, reckoning, from ḥ-s-b. Al-Ṭabarī says day bright so that you may seek bounty via work, trade, travel, and night and day alternation allows you to know number of years and calculation of time, months, seasons, etc. Al-Qurṭubī notes that knowing years and calculation is essential for religious and worldly affairs: fasting, pilgrimage, contracts, etc., all depend on knowing time, which depends on night and day and sun and moon.
