The signs therefore serve practical purposes: seeking bounty and knowing time.

**We Explained Everything In Detail**

Wa-kulla shay'in faṣṣalnāhu tafṣīlan — and everything We explained in detail, with detailed explanation. Kulla shay'in, everything, all things. Faṣṣalnāhu from f-ṣ-l, to explain in detail, separate, clarify, from same root as tafṣīl, detailed explanation. Tafṣīlan, in detail, with emphasis, detailed explanation. Al-Ṭabarī says We explained everything in detail, meaning We made clear in Qur'an everything needed for guidance, including signs of night and day, rulings, stories, etc. Al-Rāzī says faṣṣalnāhu tafṣīlan indicates that Qur'an explains everything in detail, not vaguely, so that people can seek bounty and know years and calculation and follow most upright way. The phrase appears in other verses to indicate Qur'an's detailed explanation, as in 6:126 and 17:12.

**Why Night And Day Are Signs**

Night and day are signs because they show power, wisdom, and mercy: power to create and alternate, wisdom to make one dark for rest and one bright for work, mercy to allow seeking bounty and knowing time. Previous chapter said night and day subjected for your benefit, signs for those who understand. This verse adds that they are two signs, one erased, one bright, for seeking bounty and knowing calculation, and everything explained in detail. The sūrah's theme of signs appears: shadows, living creatures, angels, night and day, all signs that should lead to submission and gratitude.

**Connection To Haste**

Previous verse said human prays for evil as for good, is ever hasty. This verse says We made night and day as two signs, made night devoid and day bright so you may seek bounty and know years and calculation, and explained everything in detail. The connection is that haste leads to praying for evil and to not considering signs; reflection on night and day as signs with detailed explanation cures haste by showing that time is measured, bounty is to be sought in day, rest at night, and everything explained, so there is no need to hasten. The person who knows number of years and calculation understands that everything has appointed term, as previous chapter said He delays for appointed term and when time arrives cannot be delayed or advanced, so he will not hasten.

**Linguistic Note On Maḥw And Mubṣir**

Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make. Layl from l-y-l, night. Nahār from n-h-r, day. Āyatayn dual of āyah, sign. Maḥawnā from m-ḥ-w, to erase, blot out. Āyata al-layl, sign of night. Mubṣiratan from b-ṣ-r, to see, bright, giving sight, meaning day makes you see, or day is visible. Li-tabtaghū from b-gh-y, to seek. Faḍlan from f-ḍ-l, bounty. ʿAdada from ʿ-d-d, number. Sinīna from s-n-w, years. Ḥisāb from ḥ-s-b, calculation. Kulla shay'in, everything, from k-l-l and sh-y-ʾ. Faṣṣalnāhu from f-ṣ-l, to detail. Tafṣīlan, in detail, from same root. The verse's structure — We made night and day two signs, We erased night sign and made day sign bright so you may seek bounty and know years and calculation, everything We explained in detail — with creation, purpose, and explanation, shows that signs are made for human benefit and are explained in detail to guide to most upright.

**How This Verse Teaches Gratitude And Time Management**

This verse teaches gratitude for night and day as signs and for detailed explanation, and teaches time management: night for rest, day for seeking bounty, and knowing years and calculation for organizing affairs. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should use night for rest and worship, day for seeking bounty with gratitude, and should know times for religious duties via calculation. The verse also teaches that everything explained in detail in Qur'an, so follow its guidance to most upright to receive mighty reward and avoid painful punishment. The practical lesson is to reflect on night and day as signs, to seek bounty in day with trust, to know calculation of time for duties, and to follow detailed explanation that guides to most upright.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:13

> **We have bound every human’s destiny to their neck. And on the Day of Judgment We will bring forth to each ˹person˺ a record which they will find laid open.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Bound Destiny To Neck**

Wa-kulla insānin alzamnāhu ṭā'irahu fī ʿunuqihi — and every human We bound his bird to his neck. Kulla insānin, every human, each person. Alzamnāhu from l-z-m, to bind, make incumbent, attach inseparably, from lāzim, that which is binding. Ṭā'irahu from ṭ-y-r, bird, flight, omen, destiny, from same root as ṭā'ir, bird, and taṭayyur, taking omens from birds, because Arabs would release birds and take omens from direction of flight, calling fortune ṭā'ir. Fī ʿunuqihi, in his neck, from ʿ-n-q, neck. Al-Ṭabarī explains that ṭā'ir here means destiny, deeds, fortune, what flies from him of good or evil, bound to neck meaning made incumbent upon him, attached to him inseparably like necklace that does not leave neck. Arabs used to see omens in birds flight, calling good or bad luck ṭā'ir, so Qur'an uses same word to mean destiny, what is decreed via own deeds. Al-Qurṭubī says alzamnāhu ṭā'irahu fī ʿunuqihi means We made his deeds, good and evil, and his destiny, bound to his neck, meaning he cannot escape, it stays with him as necklace stays on neck, always with him, visible.

**Record Laid Open On Day Of Judgment**

Wa-nukhriju lahu yawma al-qiyāmati kitāban yalqāhu manshūran — and We bring forth for him on Day of Resurrection a book he finds laid open, spread open. Nukhriju from kh-r-j, to bring forth, extract, produce. Lahu, for him. Yawma al-qiyāmati, Day of Resurrection, Day of Standing, from q-w-m. Kitāban, book, record, from k-t-b, to write. Yalqāhu from l-q-y, to find, meet, encounter. Manshūran from n-sh-r, to spread open, publish, lay open, from same root as nashr, spreading, publication. Al-Ṭabarī says on Day of Judgment We bring forth for each person a book, record of deeds, which he finds laid open, manshūran, meaning open for him to read, not sealed, wide open so he can see all deeds, small and great. Al-Rāzī says manshūran indicates that record is spread open, not folded, so that person sees all, nothing hidden, and that laid open indicates ease of reading. The phrase yalqāhu manshūran, he finds it laid open, emphasizes that it is presented open, not that he must open it.

**Why Neck And Bird Mentioned**

Why neck and bird? Neck is what holds necklace, closest to head, inseparable, always with person, and bird indicates what flies, what is taken as omen. The metaphor that destiny bound to neck means it is attached, cannot be removed, follows him wherever he goes, as necklace follows. Al-Zamakhsharī notes that ʿunuq, neck, is mentioned because it is where ornaments and yokes are placed, and because what is bound to neck is always with person, visible, cannot be hidden, and because Arabs said this is in your neck, meaning incumbent upon you. Ṭā'ir as bird also alludes to Arab practice of taking omens from bird flight, which Qur'an refutes: destiny not from birds but from own deeds bound to neck. The verse therefore corrects superstition and affirms responsibility: your destiny is your deeds bound to neck, not random bird omen. This links to previous verse that no bearer bears burden of another: each has own ṭā'ir bound to own neck.

**Connection To Detailed Explanation And Seeking Bounty**

Verse 12 said We explained everything in detail, made night and day signs for seeking bounty and knowing calculation. Verse 13 says every human's destiny bound to neck, record laid open on Day. The connection is that after explaining signs and that everything detailed, the sūrah moves to individual responsibility: everything explained so you can choose, and your choice, your ṭā'ir, is bound to neck, recorded in book laid open. Seeking bounty and knowing calculation are part of deeds that will be in book. The person who uses night and day as signs, seeks bounty with gratitude, knows calculation for duties, will have good record; who prays for evil out of haste will have evil record. The detailed explanation is so that person knows what binds to neck as good.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Record**

Previous chapter said on Day Resurrection you will be presented to Lord in rows, told you came as created first time but claimed never appointed meeting, and book placed and you see criminals fearful of what in it saying woe this book does not omit small nor great but has counted, and they find what they did present, and Lord wrongs not. This verse says similar: record laid open he finds spread open. The theme of record is consistent across both chapters: deeds recorded, book laid open, person reads, sufficient self as reckoner. Next verse says read your book, sufficient yourself today as reckoner, completing picture.

**Linguistic Note On Alzamna And Manshur**

Alzamnāhu from l-z-m, to bind, make incumbent, from lāzim, incumbent, from lazima, to be necessary, to stick. Ṭā'irahu from ṭ-y-r, to fly, bird, omen, from ṭāra, to fly. ʿUnuq from ʿ-n-q, neck. Nukhriju from kh-r-j, to bring forth. Yawm from y-w-m, day. Qiyāmah from q-w-m, to stand, resurrection. Kitāb from k-t-b, book, record. Yalqāhu from l-q-y, to meet, find. Manshūr from n-sh-r, to spread, publish, open, from nashara, to spread. The verse's structure — every human We bound his bird in his neck, and We bring forth for him on Day of Resurrection a book he finds laid open — with binding in world and bringing forth in Hereafter, shows that destiny in world is deeds that become record laid open in Hereafter, inseparable then readable.

**How This Verse Teaches Responsibility And Anti-Superstition**

This verse teaches responsibility and rejection of superstition: your destiny is not random omen from birds but your deeds bound to neck, and record laid open will show all. Al-Rāzī says believer should be mindful that every deed is bound to neck and will be in book laid open, so he should do good and avoid evil, follow most upright guidance that Qur'an guides to. The sūrah's moral code that follows is what should be bound to neck as good deeds: honoring parents, giving relatives due, not killing children, not approaching adultery, etc. The verse also teaches against taṭayyur: do not seek omens in birds, your ṭā'ir is your deeds. The practical lesson is to be aware that every action is recorded, to strive for good record laid open that you will be pleased to read, and to remember that on Day record will be laid open with no hiding, so prepare now.

**A Reflection On Book Laid Open**

Manshūran — laid open, spread open — evokes image of book opened fully so that nothing hidden, all visible, laid open before eyes. Unlike sealed book, laid open book is readable at glance. On Day, each person finds his book laid open, meaning he can read all, small and great, without barrier. This is justice: he sees own deeds, not told by others, and sufficient self as reckoner. Next verse invites him to read himself. The believer who reflects that book will be laid open will be careful what he writes in it via deeds bound to neck, seeking that when laid open it shows good, seeking bounty lawfully, knowing calculation, following most upright guidance, so that reading is easy and joyful.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:14

> **˹And it will be said,˺ “Read your record. You ˹alone˺ are sufficient this Day to take account of yourself.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Read Your Record**

Iqra' kitābaka — read your book. Iqra' from q-r-ʾ, to read, recite, same root as Qur'an, recitation, and iqra' first revealed to Prophet Muhammad in cave, command to read. Kitābaka, your book, your record, from k-t-b, to write, book. The command iqra' kitābaka is said to each person on Day of Judgment, as if it will be said, read your record. Al-Ṭabarī explains that on Day We say to person read your book, your record of deeds that was bound to neck and brought forth laid open. Al-Qurṭubī says this is address to each person to read own book, meaning to examine deeds, to confront own actions. The imperative iqra' indicates that person is able to read, even if illiterate in worldly life, on Day he reads his record, because record laid open is made readable by Allah. The fact that first revelation iqra' and Day command iqra' kitābaka both about reading links worldly reading of Qur'an to Hereafter reading of record.

**Sufficient Yourself As Reckoner**

Kafā bi-nafsika al-yawma ʿalayka ḥasīban — sufficient yourself today against you as reckoner. Kafā from k-f-y, to suffice, be enough, as in verse 17 sufficient Lord as Aware Seeing. Bi-nafsika, with yourself, yourself, from n-f-s, self, soul. Al-yawma, today, this Day, Day of Judgment. ʿAlayka, against you, upon you, indicating reckoning against self. Ḥasīban from ḥ-s-b, to reckon, calculate, account, same root as ḥisāb, calculation, and ḥasīb, reckoner, as verse 12 about knowing number years and calculation. Al-Ṭabarī says sufficient yourself today as reckoner against yourself, meaning you are sufficient to reckon yourself, you do not need other reckoner, your own self knows what you did when you read book laid open. Al-Rāzī explains that kafā bi-nafsika ʿalayka ḥasīban indicates justice and self-conviction: you yourself reckon, you cannot claim you did not know, because you read your own book laid open, spread open, and you know deeds.

**Why Self Reckoning Is Most Just**

Why self reckoning? Because it is most just and most convincing: when person reads own record laid open and reckons himself, he cannot deny, cannot claim injustice. Al-Zamakhsharī notes that phrase kafā bi-nafsika indicates that self is sufficient as reckoner, no need for witnesses beyond self, because book is laid open and self knows deeds intimately. This is also mercy: person sees own deeds clearly and understands justice of judgment. The verse also implies that in worldly life, person should reckon self before being reckoned, as ʿUmar ibn al-Khaṭṭāb said reckon yourselves before you are reckoned, weigh yourselves before you are weighed, because on Day you will be sufficient as reckoner, so prepare by self-accountability now. This practice of muḥāsabah, self-reckoning, is central to spiritual training.

**Connection To Destiny Bound To Neck**

Verse 13 said every person's destiny bound to neck, record laid open brought forth on Day. Verse 14 says read your book, sufficient yourself today as reckoner. The connection is that record bound to neck in world becomes book laid open in Hereafter that person reads and reckons himself. The destiny that was bound, ṭā'ir, becomes readable book. The sequence shows responsibility and justice: your deeds bound to neck, recorded, laid open, you read, you reckon. No injustice: you see own book, you are sufficient as reckoner. This also links to principle no bearer bears burden of another: each reads own book, reckons own self.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Book Not Omitting**

Previous chapter said book placed and criminals fearful saying woe this book does not omit small nor great but has counted, they find what they did present, and Lord wrongs not. This verse says read your book, sufficient yourself today as reckoner, similar theme: book does not omit, you read, you reckon, you cannot deny. The sūrah emphasizes record and self-reckoning to encourage following most upright guidance that leads to good record and mighty reward, and avoiding evil that leads to painful punishment. The fact that book does not omit small nor great means self-reckoning will be detailed.

**Linguistic Note On Iqra And Hasib**

Iqra' from q-r-ʾ, to read, recite, from same root as Qur'an, recitation, and qirā'ah, reading, and maqra'ah, place of reading. Kitāb from k-t-b, book, record, writing, from kataba, to write. Nafs from n-f-s, self, soul, from nafasa, to breathe. Yawm from y-w-m, day. Ḥasīb from ḥ-s-b, to reckon, calculate, account, from same root as ḥisāb, calculation, as in verse 12 about knowing number years and calculation, and as in reckoning deeds. Kafā bi-, sufficient with, enough, from kafā, to suffice. The verse's brevity — read your book, sufficient yourself today against you as reckoner — with command and statement of sufficiency, conveys justice and self-awareness: you read, you reckon, you are enough as reckoner against yourself, no need for other.

**How This Verse Teaches Self-Accountability In World**

This verse teaches self-accountability in worldly life: in world, reckon yourself before you are reckoned on Day when you will be sufficient as reckoner. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should daily read his own book of deeds in worldly sense, i.e., reflect what he did, and reckon himself, repenting for evil and increasing good, so that when book laid open on Day he is pleased to read it. The sūrah's moral code that follows provides what good deeds to do that will make self-reckoning easy: honoring parents, giving relatives due, not spending wastefully, not killing children, not approaching adultery, honoring pledges, measuring fully, etc. The verse also teaches that Qur'an guides to most upright, so reading it in world helps you read your record with ease in Hereafter, because it teaches what to write in book.

**A Lesson On Reading And Reckoning**

Iqra' — read — first word revealed to Prophet, and here addressed to every person on Day to read own record. The parallel suggests that reading Qur'an in world, which guides to most upright and explains everything in detail, prepares you to read your record in Hereafter with success. The person who reads Qur'an and follows it will have book laid open showing good deeds and will reckon himself with ease, while who does not believe in Hereafter and prays for evil out of haste will find painful record and self-reckoning difficult. The command read your book therefore invites reflection: what are you writing in your book today via deeds bound to neck? Are you preparing for easy self-reckoning by following most upright guidance, seeking bounty with gratitude, knowing calculation, being patient not hasty?

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:15

> **Whoever chooses to be guided, it is only for their own good. And whoever chooses to stray, it is only to their own loss. No soul burdened with sin will bear the burden of another. And We would never punish ˹a people˺ until We have sent a messenger ˹to warn them˺.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Whoever Guided For Own Good**

Mani ihtadā fa-innamā yahtadī li-nafsihi — whoever is guided, then only he is guided for his self. Man, whoever, conditional. Ihtadā from h-d-y, to be guided, follow guidance, from same root as hudā, guidance, and hadā, to guide, and hādī, guide. Fa-innamā, then only, so only, restriction. Yahtadī from h-d-y, is guided, follows guidance. Li-nafsihi, for his self, for himself, from n-f-s, self, soul. Al-Ṭabarī explains that whoever chooses to be guided, follows most upright guidance of Qur'an that guides to most upright, it is only for own good, benefit returns to himself, not to Allah, because Allah not in need of creation. Al-Rāzī says guidance benefits self in world and Hereafter, as mighty reward mentioned in verse 9 for believers who do good. The phrase li-nafsihi emphasizes personal benefit: guided for self, not for other.

**Whoever Strays To Own Loss**

Wa-man ḍalla fa-innamā yaḍillu ʿalayhā — and whoever strays, then only he strays against it, to its loss. Man ḍalla from ḍ-l-l, to stray, go astray, from same root as ḍalāl, straying, misguidance. Fa-innamā yaḍillu, then only he strays, restriction. ʿAlayhā, against it, upon it, i.e., against his self, to its loss, from ʿ-l-w, upon, against. Al-Ṭabarī says whoever chooses to stray, not follow guidance, rejects most upright, it is only to own loss, harm returns to himself, not to Allah, because Allah not harmed by straying. Al-Qurṭubī says this is justice: guided for own good, straying to own loss, each responsible for own choice, as destiny bound to neck and record laid open shows own deeds.

**No Soul Bears Burden Of Another**

Wa-lā taziru wāziratun wizra ukhrā — and no bearer bears burden of another. Lā taziru from w-z-r, to bear, carry burden, from same root as wizr, burden, sin, heavy load, and wāzir, bearer, minister who bears burden of king. Wāziratun, bearer, female form used for soul, nafs, as bearer of burden, soul that bears. Wizra ukhrā, burden of another, from w-z-r and ā-kh-r, other, another. Al-Ṭabarī says no soul burdened with sin will bear burden of another, meaning each soul bears own burden, own sins, not others', on Day each reads own book and sufficient self as reckoner, no transferring. Al-Rāzī says this is principle of justice: no soul bears another's burden, each responsible for own deeds bound to neck. This principle appears in several places (6:164, 35:18, 39:7, 53:38) to affirm individual responsibility, and does not contradict that one who misleads others bears burden of misleading in addition to own, not instead of theirs, as hadith says whoever calls to misguidance bears its burden and burden of those who follow without diminishing theirs.

**We Never Punish Until Messenger Sent**

Wa-mā kunnā muʿadhdhibīna ḥattā nabʿatha rasūlan — and We were not punishers until We send a messenger. Mā kunnā, We were not, We would never, negation of past with emphasis of never. Muʿadhdhibīna from ʿ-dh-b, to punish, chastise, from same root as ʿadhāb, punishment, torment. Ḥattā, until. Nabʿatha from b-ʿ-th, to send, raise, dispatch, from same root as baʿth, sending, resurrection, raising. Rasūlan from r-s-l, messenger, apostle, from arsala, to send. Al-Ṭabarī explains that We would never punish a people until We have sent messenger to warn them, establish proof, as justice requires that people know guidance before being punished for rejecting, so that no one says we did not know. Al-Qurṭubī says this indicates that punishment in world or Hereafter only after messenger sent to warn, so that proof established, hujjah, as in story of Children of Isra'il who were given Book as guidance after Moses, warned, then punished when promise came due to rebellion.

**Why This Verse Follows Reading Book**

Verses 13-14 said destiny bound to neck, record laid open, read your book, sufficient yourself as reckoner. Verse 15 says whoever guided for own good, whoever strayed to own loss, no bearer bears burden of another, We never punish until messenger sent. The connection is that after describing individual record and self-reckoning, the sūrah affirms principle of individual responsibility and justice: guided for self, strayed against self, no bearing others burden, and no punishment until messenger sent to warn. This explains why record matters: because each responsible for own guidance, not others', and punishment only after warning, so record is fair. The person who reads own book cannot blame other, because no bearer bears another's burden, and cannot claim no warning, because messenger sent.

**Connection To Children Of Isra'il And Night Journey**

The sūrah's narrative of Night Journey and Children of Isra'il who were given Book as guidance illustrates sending messenger Moses with Book, then warning about corruption twice, then punishment. The principle that We never punish until messenger sent explains why Children of Isra'il were warned via Book and messengers before punishment when promise came. Similarly, Prophet Muhammad taken on Night Journey and given Qur'an that guides to most upright is messenger sent to warn current people, so that proof established before punishment. The verse therefore justifies sending messengers and establishes that current audience has been warned via Qur'an.

**Linguistic Note On Wazira And Muʿadhdhibin**

Ihtadā from h-d-y, to be guided. Nafs from n-f-s, self. Ḍalla from ḍ-l-l, to stray. Wazara from w-z-r, to bear burden, from wizr, burden, heavy load, from wazara, to carry. Wāzira, bearer, female because nafs feminine, soul. Wizra ukhrā, burden of another. Muʿadhdhibīn from ʿ-dh-b, to punish. Nabʿatha from b-ʿ-th, to send. Rasūl from r-s-l, messenger. The verse's structure — whoever guided for self, whoever strayed against self, no bearer bears another burden, We never punish until messenger sent — with four principles, establishes justice and mercy: guidance benefits self, straying harms self, no transferring burden, no punishment without warning via messenger, all foundations of divine justice.

**How This Verse Teaches Justice And Mercy**

This verse teaches justice and mercy: guidance for own good, straying to own loss, no soul bears another's burden, no punishment until messenger sent to warn. Al-Rāzī says believer should be grateful that Allah sends messengers to warn and guide to most upright, and should take guidance for own good, not delay, because burden is own and will be in book laid open. The sūrah's moral code that follows is guidance sent via messenger Prophet Muhammad who was taken on Night Journey, guiding to most upright. Following it is for own good with mighty reward; straying is to own loss with painful punishment. The principle also teaches against blaming others: each bears own burden, so focus on own deeds bound to neck and record laid open, not on others.

**A Lesson On Individual Responsibility**

No soul burdened bears burden of another — this principle of individual responsibility is central to Qur'anic justice. It means on Day, each reads own book, sufficient self as reckoner, no one bears another's sins, though one may bear burden of misleading others in addition to own, not instead. The verse also assures that Allah would never punish until messenger sent, showing mercy and justice: warning before punishment, explanation in detail before reckoning, as verse 12 said everything explained in detail. The believer who understands this will hasten to guidance for own good, not pray for evil out of haste, and will follow most upright guidance that was sent via messenger, seeking mighty reward and avoiding painful punishment.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:16

> **Whenever We intend to destroy a society, We command its elite ˹to obey Allah˺ but they act rebelliously in it. So the decree ˹of punishment˺ is justified, and We destroy it utterly.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**When Intend To Destroy Society**

Wa-idhā aradnā an nuhlika qaryatan — and when We intend to destroy a town, society. Idhā, when, whenever. Aradnā from r-w-d, to intend, will, desire, as verse 18-19 arāda. An nuhlika from h-l-k, to destroy, perish, cause to perish, from same root as halāk, destruction, and ahlaknā, We destroyed. Qaryatan from q-r-y, town, village, society, settlement, from same root as qaryah, town, gathering place, from qarā, to gather. Al-Ṭabarī explains that when We intend to destroy a society due to its sins, after messenger sent and warning rejected, We command its elite but they rebel, so decree justified and We destroy it. Al-Qurṭubī says qaryah here means society, town, any settlement, not necessarily large city, and intention to destroy is after establishing proof via messenger, consistent with previous verse that We never punish until messenger sent, so destruction only after warning.

**Command Elite But They Rebel**

Amarnā mutrafīhā fa-fasaqū fīhā — We command its elite but they act rebelliously in it. Amarnā from a-m-r, to command, order, from amara, to command. Mutrafīhā from t-r-f, to live in luxury, affluence, from same root as mutraf, affluent, luxurious, elite, one given luxury, and itraf, luxury, affluence, from atrafa, to make live in luxury. Fa-fasaqū from f-s-q, to act rebelliously, transgress, disobey, exit from obedience, from same root as fisq, rebellion, sin, transgression. Fīhā, in it, in society. Al-Ṭabarī explains that We command its affluent ones, elite, those given luxury, to obey Allah, via messenger, but they act rebelliously, disobey, commit fisq in it, in society, spreading corruption. The affluent, mutraf, are those given luxury, wealth, ease, who often are leaders, and when they rebel, society follows or is corrupted. Al-Rāzī says commanding elite is because they have means to obey and influence, and their rebellion is more serious and more corrupting, as they are followed.

**Decree Justified Then Destruction Utterly**

Fa-ḥaqqa ʿalayhā al-qawlu fa-dammarnāhā tadmīran — so decree is justified against it, then We destroy it utterly. Fa-ḥaqqa from ḥ-q-q, to become true, incumbent, realized, justified, from same root as ḥaqq, truth, right. ʿAlayhā, against it, upon it. Al-qawlu, decree, word, saying, word of punishment, from q-w-l, to say. Fa-dammarnāhā from d-m-r, to destroy utterly, annihilate, from same root as tadmir, utter destruction. Tadmīran, utterly, with emphasis, utter destruction, complete annihilation, from same root, intensive verbal noun. Al-Ṭabarī says so decree of punishment becomes true, justified, against it due to rebellion of elite, then We destroy it utterly, with complete destruction, no remainder. Al-Qurṭubī says ḥaqqa al-qawl means word of punishment becomes incumbent, justified, true, as in other verses about word becoming true against disbelievers who rebelled after warning.

**Why Elite Mentioned**

Why elite, mutraf? Because affluence often leads to arrogance, rebellion, and because elite are influencers, leaders. When they rebel, others follow, and corruption spreads, so society becomes deserving of destruction. Previous verse said no bearer bears burden of another, yet here society destroyed due to rebellion of elite — how reconciled? Al-Rāzī explains that destruction of society when elite rebel does not mean innocent bear burden of others unjustly, but that when elite rebel and society does not forbid evil, does not enjoin good, all become complicit, or that destruction is worldly punishment that may affect all as test, while in Hereafter each bears own burden and innocent not punished for others' sins. The sūrah's earlier mention of Children of Isra'il corruption twice illustrates: elite among them rebelled, so decree justified and We sent servants to destroy.

**Connection To Children Of Isra'il Corruption**

Verses 4-8 told Children of Isra'il will cause corruption twice, when first of two warnings would come to pass, We would send against you some of Our servants of great might, who would ravage your homes, this would be warning fulfilled, then after repentance We would give upper hand, aid with wealth and offspring, then if you act rightly for own good, if do wrong to own loss, when second warning would come to pass, enemies would totally disgrace you and enter Temple as entered first time and destroy utterly whatever would fall into hands. Verse 16 says whenever We intend to destroy society We command its elite but they rebel so decree justified then We destroy utterly — this is general principle illustrated by Children of Isra'il story. Their elite rebelled, decree justified, destruction utterly came via servants of great might. The sūrah therefore links general principle to specific example to warn current audience.

**Linguistic Note On Mutraf And Tadmīr**

Aradnā from r-w-d, to intend. Nuhlika from h-l-k, to destroy, from halaka, to perish. Qaryah from q-r-y, to gather, town as gathering place. Amarnā from a-m-r, to command. Mutraf from t-r-f, luxury, affluence, one given luxury, from atrafa, to make live in luxury, from tarafa, to live luxuriously. Fasaqū from f-s-q, to rebel, transgress, exit from obedience, from fasaqa, to exit, to be corrupt. Ḥaqqa from ḥ-q-q, to be true, incumbent, justified. Qawl from q-w-l, word, decree. Dammarnā from d-m-r, to destroy utterly, from dammara, to destroy. Tadmīran, utter destruction, intensive, from same root. The verse's structure — when We intend to destroy town, We command its elite but they rebel in it, so word is justified against it, then We destroy it utterly — with intention, command, rebellion, decree justified, destruction utterly, shows stages of divine justice after warning via messenger, not arbitrary.

**How This Verse Teaches Warning Against Luxury And Rebellion**

This verse teaches warning against luxury leading to rebellion: affluence is test, not excuse to rebel, elite should obey command. Al-Qurṭubī says mutraf, elite, affluent, are often cause of corruption, so believer should not be deceived by luxury but should obey command, be grateful, not rebellious. The sūrah's moral code that follows includes not spending wastefully, which is characteristic of mutraf, and being moderate, not tight-fisted nor openhanded, and giving relatives due. The verse also teaches that when elite rebel, decree justified and destruction utterly may come, so society should enjoin good and forbid evil, not follow elite in rebellion. The practical lesson is to be grateful for bounty, not rebellious, and to follow command to obey, so that decree of punishment not justified and town not destroyed and record laid open shows obedience, not rebellion.

**A Reflection On Decree Justified**

Fa-ḥaqqa ʿalayhā al-qawlu — so decree is justified against it — indicates that word of punishment, promise of punishment after messenger sent and warning rejected and elite rebellion, becomes incumbent, justified, realized, not arbitrary. It is justified due to rebellion after command. Then tadmir, utter destruction, follows as consequence. This shows justice: punishment only after command and rebellion, after messenger sent, after decree justified. The believer who reflects will obey command when elite are tested, will not be among mutraf who rebel, and will seek bounty with gratitude, not with rebellion, so that his town not destroyed and his record laid open shows obedience and gratitude.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:17

> **˹Imagine˺ how many peoples We have destroyed after Noah! And sufficient is your Lord as All-Aware and All-Seeing of the sins of His servants. This Life or the Next?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**How Many Peoples Destroyed After Noah**

Wa-kam ahlaknā mina al-qurūni min baʿdi Nūḥin — and how many generations We destroyed after Noah. Kam, how many, how much, interrogative for many, rhetorical to indicate many. Ahlaknā from h-l-k, to destroy, cause to perish, as previous verse nuhlika, destroy, and dammarnā, destroy utterly. Mina al-qurūni from q-r-n, generations, peoples, centuries, from same root as qarn, generation, century, horn, period. Min baʿdi, after, from baʿd. Nūḥin, Noah, prophet Noah, from n-w-ḥ, name. Al-Ṭabarī explains that how many generations, peoples, We destroyed after Noah due to their rebellion after messenger sent and elite rebelled, as example of previous principle that when intention to destroy society and elite rebel, decree justified and destruction utterly. Al-Qurṭubī says after Noah because Noah was first major messenger to people of earth after Adam, and after him many generations destroyed for rejecting messengers, as Qur'an mentions ʿĀd, Thamūd, Pharaoh, etc. The phrase kam ahlaknā indicates many, not few, showing pattern of destruction after warning.

**Sufficient Lord As Aware And Seeing Sins**

Wa-kafā bi-rabbika bi-dhunūbi ʿibādihi khabīran baṣīran — and sufficient is your Lord with sins of His servants as All-Aware and All-Seeing. Kafā from k-f-y, to suffice, be enough, as in verse 14 kafā bi-nafsika sufficient yourself as reckoner. Bi-rabbika, with your Lord, by your Lord, from r-b-b, Lord, master, sustainer. Bi-dhunūbi, with sins, of sins, from dh-n-b, sin, tail, from same root as dhanb, sin, offense. ʿIbādihi, His servants, slaves, from ʿ-b-d, to worship, serve, from ʿabada. Khabīran from kh-b-r, to be aware, informed, from same root as khabar, news, and khabīr, All-Aware, who knows inner. Baṣīran from b-ṣ-r, to see, seeing, from same root as baṣar, sight, and mubṣir, bright, giving sight, as verse 12 made day bright mubṣiratan. Al-Ṭabarī says sufficient is your Lord as All-Aware and All-Seeing of sins of His servants, meaning He knows and sees their sins, no need for other informer, He is enough to know and punish accordingly with justice. Al-Rāzī says khabīran baṣīran indicates complete knowledge: aware of inner sins and seeing outer deeds, nothing hidden.

**Why After Noah Specifically**

Why after Noah? Because Noah's people were first major destroyed after Adam due to rejection of messenger, and after him pattern continued, and because verse 3 said progeny of those carried with Noah, grateful servant, linking to Noah. Now verse says how many generations destroyed after Noah, indicating that being progeny of those carried with Noah does not guarantee safety; many after Noah destroyed for sins despite being progeny of survivors. The Children of Isra'il story also after Noah, as they are progeny of those carried with Noah, and they were warned about corruption twice. The sūrah therefore reminds that history shows many destroyed after Noah when they rebelled, so current people, also progeny of those carried, should not be complacent, should follow most upright guidance.

**Connection To Destroying Society When Elite Rebel**

Verse 16 said whenever We intend to destroy society We command its elite but they rebel so decree justified then We destroy utterly. Verse 17 says how many peoples We have destroyed after Noah, sufficient Lord as All-Aware and All-Seeing sins of servants. The connection is that destruction of many generations after Noah illustrates principle of verse 16: elite rebelled, decree justified, destruction utterly. The phrase kam ahlaknā indicates many examples, not just one, showing consistent divine law. And sufficient Lord as Aware Seeing indicates He knows sins that caused destruction, no injustice, destruction based on knowledge of sins, not arbitrary, and His awareness sufficient to judge.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Destroying Towns And Provision**

Previous chapter also mentioned destroying towns, and that Allah does not punish until messenger sent, and that He gives provision in due measure, Aware Seeing of servants. This verse continues theme of destruction after Noah as warning, and that Lord is Aware Seeing of sins of servants, as previous chapter said He is Aware Seeing of servants regarding provision. The sūrah's opening about Night Journey and Children of Isra'il corruption also about destruction. The verse therefore places current audience in historical context: many before destroyed, so heed warning, follow most upright guidance that gives mighty reward.

**Linguistic Note On Qurun And Khabir Basir**

Kam from k-m, how many. Ahlaknā from h-l-k, to destroy. Qurūn from q-r-n, generations, centuries, from qarn, horn, generation as period, as horn marks period. Baʿd from b-ʿ-d, after. Nūḥ, Noah, prophet name. Kafā from k-f-y, to suffice. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Dhunūb from dh-n-b, sins. ʿIbād from ʿ-b-d, servants. Khabīr from kh-b-r, All-Aware, informed, who knows hidden. Baṣīr from b-ṣ-r, All-Seeing. The verse's structure — how many generations We destroyed after Noah, sufficient your Lord with sins of His servants as All-Aware All-Seeing — with rhetorical question about many destroyed and statement of sufficiency of Lord's awareness, shows that destruction is based on knowledge of sins, not arbitrary, and that Lord's awareness sufficient to judge justly, no need for other.

**How This Verse Teaches Reflection On History**

This verse teaches reflection on history: how many generations destroyed after Noah for sins, so reflect and avoid their path. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should consider history of past generations destroyed, as Qur'an often says travel in earth and see how was end of those before, to learn lesson, and that many destroyed after Noah shows pattern. The sūrah's mention of Children of Isra'il corruption twice is part of that history, and current Makkan audience also should learn. The practical lesson is to not be among mutraf, elite, who rebel, but to follow command, seek bounty with gratitude, and remember that Lord is All-Aware and All-Seeing of sins, so hidden sins not hidden from Him, and to repent.

**A Lesson On Lord Being Sufficient As Aware Seeing**

Wa-kafā bi-rabbika bi-dhunūbi ʿibādihi khabīran baṣīran — sufficient is your Lord with sins of His servants as All-Aware, All-Seeing. This phrase reassures that Allah knows and sees sins, sufficient as watcher, no need for other witness, and that judgment based on complete knowledge. For believer, it is warning that sins seen, even hidden, and also comfort that Lord is Aware of struggles and sincere efforts. The combination khabīran baṣīran, All-Aware All-Seeing, indicates comprehensive knowledge: khabīr knows inner, hidden, baṣīr sees outer, apparent, as verse 12 said day bright mubṣiratan, giving sight, and here Lord Baṣīr sees all. The believer who knows Lord is All-Aware All-Seeing of sins will be mindful, will repent, will follow most upright guidance that leads to mighty reward and avoids painful punishment, and will not pray for evil out of haste, will not desire fleeting alone.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:18

> **Whoever desires this fleeting world ˹alone˺, We hasten in it whatever We please to whoever We will; then We destine them for Hell, where they will burn, condemned and rejected.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Whoever Desires Fleeting World**

Man kāna yurīdu al-ʿājilata — whoever is desires the hasty, fleeting. Man, whoever, conditional. Kāna, is, was, indicates state. Yurīdu from r-w-d, to desire, want, intend, same root as aradnā, We intend, in verse 16, and arāda, desires, in verse 19. Al-ʿājilata from ʿ-j-l, hasty, fleeting, immediate, from same root as ʿajūlan, hasty, ever hasty, in verse 11, and ʿajila, to hasten, and taʿjīl, hastening. Al-ʿājilah, the hasty one, meaning worldly life, immediate life, fleeting world, as opposed to al-ākhirah, Hereafter, lasting, latter. Al-Ṭabarī explains that whoever desires fleeting world alone, immediate world, not Hereafter, making world sole goal, We hasten for him in it what We will, etc. Al-Rāzī says al-ʿājilah is world because it is hasty, immediate, fleeting, passes quickly, as opposed to Hereafter which is delayed but lasting, remaining. The person who desires ʿājilah alone is hasty, as human is ever hasty.

**We Hasten Whatever We Please To Whoever We Will**

ʿAjjalnā lahu fīhā mā nashā'u li-man nurīdu — We hasten for him in it what We will to whom We will. ʿAjjalnā from ʿ-j-l, to hasten, expedite, give quickly, same root as al-ʿājilah and ʿajūlan and ʿājilah. Lahu, for him. Fīhā, in it, in fleeting world. Mā nashā'u, what We will, from sh-y-ʾ, to will, wish. Li-man nurīdu, to whom We will, for whom We intend, from r-w-d. Al-Ṭabarī says We hasten for him in fleeting world whatever We please to whoever We will, meaning not all who desire world get all they desire, but We give hastily in world what We will to whom We will, according to wisdom, not necessarily what they desire fully, limited by will. Al-Qurṭubī notes that ʿajjalnā indicates hastening, giving quickly in world, but limited to what We will and to whom We will, not all desires fulfilled, showing that worldly gain is controlled, not guaranteed.

**Then Destined For Hell Condemned Rejected**

Thumma jaʿalnā lahu jahannama yaṣlāhā madhmūman madḥūran — then We made for him Hell he burns condemned rejected, then We destine them for Hell where they will burn condemned and rejected. Thumma, then, after worldly hastening, indicates sequence. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make, as verse 12 jaʿalnā night and day signs. Lahu, for him. Jahannama, Hell, Gehenna, from Hebrew Gehinnom, valley. Yaṣlāhā from ṣ-l-y, to burn, roast, enter fire, endure fire, from same root as ṣalā, to burn, and iṣṭilā', burning. Madhmūman from dh-m-m, to blame, condemn, from same root as dhamm, blame, and madhmūm, blamed, condemned. Madḥūran from d-ḥ-r, to drive away, reject, banish, from same root as daḥr, rejection, and madḥūr, driven away, rejected, banished from mercy. Al-Ṭabarī says then We make for him Hell he will burn, condemned, blamed, and rejected, driven away from mercy, banished. Al-Rāzī says madhmūman madḥūran indicates two punishments in Hell: blame and rejection, condemned by Allah and angels and rejected from mercy, unlike appreciated striving for Hereafter.

**Why Fleeting World Called ʿĀjilah**

Why al-ʿājilah? Because world is hasty, immediate, fleeting, passes quickly, and human is ever hasty, ʿajūlan, desires hasty, prays for evil as for good out of haste. The person who desires ʿājilah is hasty, wants immediate, not patient for Hereafter, which is lasting. Previous verse said human ever hasty, and here says whoever desires hasty world, We hasten for him what We will. The connection is that haste leads to desiring fleeting world alone. The sūrah's earlier warning do not hasten command at hand and human is ever hasty frames this: desiring ʿājilah is manifestation of haste, wanting immediate reward without patience for Hereafter.

**Connection To Seeking Bounty And Provision**

Verse 12 said made day bright so you may seek bounty of Lord and know number years and calculation. Verse 20 says each We extend these and those from gift of Lord, bounty not withheld. Verse 18 says whoever desires fleeting world alone, We hasten for him what We will then destine them for Hell condemned rejected. The connection is that seeking bounty is allowed, even commanded, but desiring fleeting world alone, making it sole goal, without seeking Hereafter, leads to Hell. Seeking bounty from Lord with gratitude as means to strive for Hereafter is good, but desiring ʿājilah alone as ultimate goal, without faith and striving for Hereafter, is blameworthy. The distinction is intention and goal: seeking bounty as part of seeking Lord's bounty with gratitude vs desiring fleeting alone as ultimate.

**Link To Next Verse About Hereafter**

Next verse says whoever desires Hereafter and strives for it accordingly and is true believer, it is they whose striving will be appreciated. The pair shows two paths, two desires, two ends: desiring ʿājilah vs desiring ākhirah, hastening worldly vs striving for Hereafter, condemned rejected vs appreciated. The sūrah's theme of guidance to most upright includes choosing Hereafter while seeking bounty in world lawfully, not making fleeting sole goal. The moral code that follows details striving for Hereafter: honoring parents, giving relatives due, etc.

**Linguistic Note On ʿAjila And Madhmum Madhur**

Yurīdu from r-w-d, to desire. ʿĀjilah from ʿ-j-l, hasty, immediate, fleeting, from ʿajila, to hasten. ʿAjjalnā from ʿ-j-l, to hasten, from ʿajjala, to hasten. Mā nashā'u from sh-y-ʾ, what We will. Nurīdu from r-w-d, We will, intend. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make. Jahannam, Hell, from Gehenna. Yaṣlā from ṣ-l-y, to burn. Madhmūm from dh-m-m, condemned, blamed, from dhamma, to blame. Madḥūr from d-ḥ-r, driven away, rejected, from daḥara, to drive away, reject. The verse's structure — whoever desires fleeting, We hasten for him in it what We will to whom We will, then We make for him Hell he burns condemned rejected — with desire, hastening limited by will, then Hell with double description, shows that fleeting desire leads to limited hastened worldly gain then severe punishment with condemnation and rejection.

**How This Verse Warns Against Worldliness**

This verse warns against making fleeting world sole desire: you may get hastened portion what Allah wills, to whom He wills, but then Hell condemned rejected. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should desire Hereafter, not make world sole goal, should seek bounty in world but with Hereafter in mind, as Qur'an guides to most upright. The sūrah's moral code includes not killing children for fear of poverty, not approaching adultery, etc., which are often motivated by worldly desire and haste. The verse teaches that desiring ʿājilah alone leads to Hell, while desiring Hereafter and striving for it as believer leads to appreciated striving, mighty reward. The practical lesson is to check intention: are you seeking fleeting alone or seeking bounty as part of seeking Lord's pleasure and Hereafter? If fleeting alone, change.

**A Reflection On Condemned Rejected**

Madhmūman madḥūran — condemned, rejected — two words describing state in Hell: blamed, condemned, and driven away, rejected from mercy, banished. This contrasts with appreciated striving for Hereafter in next verse, mashkūran, appreciated, thanked. The person who desires fleeting alone is condemned and rejected, while who desires Hereafter and strives has striving appreciated. The choice is clear: fleeting hastened limited gain then condemned rejection, vs Hereafter lasting reward with appreciation. The believer who reflects on this will not pray for evil out of haste, will not desire fleeting alone, but will desire Hereafter and strive with faith, following most upright guidance, so that striving appreciated not condemned.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:19

> **But whoever desires the Hereafter and strives for it accordingly, and is a ˹true˺ believer, it is they whose striving will be appreciated.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Whoever Desires Hereafter And Strives Accordingly**

Wa-man arāda al-ākhirata wa-saʿā lahā saʿyahā — and whoever desires Hereafter and strives for it its striving, accordingly. Man, whoever, conditional. Arāda from r-w-d, to desire, want, intend, as previous verse yurīdu al-ʿājilah desires fleeting, and aradnā We intend. Al-ākhirata from ā-kh-r, last, Hereafter, latter, opposite of al-ʿājilah, hasty, immediate, fleeting. Wa-saʿā from s-ʿ-y, to strive, run, endeavor, hasten with effort, from same root as saʿy, striving, effort, as in pilgrimage saʿy between Ṣafā and Marwah, striving. Lahā, for it, for Hereafter. Saʿyahā, its striving, its due striving, its appropriate striving, from s-ʿ-y, meaning striving that is due for Hereafter, appropriate for it. Al-Ṭabarī explains that whoever desires Hereafter, not fleeting world alone, and strives for it its striving, meaning strives with striving due for Hereafter, with deeds that lead to Hereafter, appropriate effort, not mere wish. Al-Qurṭubī says saʿā lahā saʿyahā indicates striving appropriate for Hereafter, i.e., with faith and good deeds, with conditions, not mere desire without effort.

**And Is True Believer**

Wa-huwa mu'minun — and he is believer, true believer. Huwa, he. Mu'minun from a-m-n, to believe, have faith, be secure, from same root as īmān, faith, and amn, security, and mu'min, believer, faithful. Al-Ṭabarī says and he is believer, meaning condition for striving to be appreciated is faith, because without faith deeds not accepted, as faith is foundation. Al-Rāzī says wa-huwa mu'minun indicates that striving for Hereafter must be while believer, because faith is basis, without it striving not beneficial, as previous verse about desiring fleeting alone leads to Hell even if strives in worldly sense. The phrase true believer in translation adds emphasis that faith must be true, sincere, not superficial.

**Striving Appreciated**

Fa-ulā'ika kāna saʿyuhum mashkūran — then those, their striving is appreciated, thanked. Fa-ulā'ika, then those, so those, those who desire Hereafter and strive with faith. Kāna, is, was, indicates being. Saʿyuhum, their striving, their effort, from s-ʿ-y. Mashkūran from sh-k-r, to thank, appreciate, be grateful, reward abundantly, from same root as shukr, gratitude, and shakūr, grateful, Appreciative, as verse 3 grateful servant and Allah is Shakūr. Al-Ṭabarī says then those, their striving will be appreciated, meaning Allah appreciates, thanks, rewards their striving for Hereafter, accepts it and rewards abundantly. Al-Qurṭubī says mashkūran means appreciated, rewarded, thanked by Allah, meaning accepted and rewarded abundantly, thanked, not rejected.

**Contrast With Fleeting Desirer**

Verse 18 said whoever desires fleeting world alone, We hasten for him what We will to whoever We will, then destine them for Hell where they will burn condemned and rejected. Verse 19 says whoever desires Hereafter and strives for it accordingly and is true believer, it is they whose striving will be appreciated. The contrast is clear and intentional: two desires, two strivings, two ends. Fleeting desirer gets hastened limited worldly then Hell condemned rejected; Hereafter desirer who strives accordingly as believer gets appreciated striving, i.e., mighty reward mentioned in verse 9. The pair teaches that desire alone not enough; striving accordingly with faith needed for Hereafter, while fleeting desirer may get worldly hastening without true striving for Hereafter.

**Why Striving And Faith Both Mentioned**

Why both striving and faith? Because desire without striving is wishful thinking, mere wish, and striving without faith is not accepted, not appreciated. Al-Rāzī explains that arāda al-ākhirata is intention, sincere desire for Hereafter, wa-saʿā lahā saʿyahā is action, striving appropriate, due striving, wa-huwa mu'minun is foundation, faith, all three needed: sincere desire for Hereafter, striving with deeds due for it, and faith. This is most upright way that Qur'an guides to, as verse 9 says Qur'an guides to what is most upright. The sūrah's moral code that follows details what striving for Hereafter looks like: honoring parents, giving relatives due, not spending wastefully, not killing children, not approaching adultery, honoring pledges, measuring fully, not following without knowledge, not walking arrogantly.

**Connection To Qur'an Guides To Most Upright**

Verse 9 said surely this Qur'an guides to what is most upright, and gives good news to believers who do good that they will have mighty reward. Verse 19 says whoever desires Hereafter and strives for it accordingly and is true believer, it is they whose striving will be appreciated — this is example of most upright: desiring Hereafter, striving accordingly with faith, appreciated. The sūrah's moral code that follows is detailed guidance to most upright that, if followed with desire for Hereafter and faith, leads to appreciated striving. The verse therefore links back to opening promise of mighty reward for believers who do good.

**Linguistic Note On Saʿa And Mashkur**

Arāda from r-w-d, to desire, intend, from same root as murād, intended. Ākhirah from ā-kh-r, last, Hereafter, latter, opposite of ūlā, first. Saʿā from s-ʿ-y, to strive, run, endeavor, from saʿā, to strive, hasten. Saʿy from same root, striving, effort, endeavor, as pilgrimage saʿy. Mu'min from a-m-n, believer, from amina, to be secure, believe, from īmān, faith, amn, security. Kāna from k-w-n, to be. Mashkūr from sh-k-r, thanked, appreciated, from shakara, to thank, appreciate, from shukr, gratitude, and Shakūr, Appreciative. The verse's structure — whoever desires Hereafter and strives for it its striving and is believer, then those their striving is appreciated — with desire, striving appropriate, faith, then appreciation, shows conditions for appreciated striving, three conditions leading to result, and result is appreciation.

**How This Verse Teaches Sincere Striving**

This verse teaches sincere striving for Hereafter with faith: desire Hereafter, strive with appropriate striving, be believer, then striving appreciated. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should desire Hereafter, not make fleeting sole goal, should strive with deeds that Qur'an guides to most upright, with faith, so that striving appreciated and rewarded with mighty reward, not condemned rejected. The practical lesson is to examine desire: do you desire Hereafter? Do you strive for it its striving, i.e., with deeds due, not mere wish? Are you believer, true believer? If yes, glad tidings: striving appreciated, thanked by Allah who is Shakūr, Appreciative, who appreciates small deeds with great reward, multiplies. This encourages effort even if imperfect, as long as desire and faith sincere and striving appropriate.

**A Reflection On Appreciated Striving**

Mashkūran — appreciated, thanked — from sh-k-r, same root as grateful servant in verse 3, Nūḥ grateful servant, and Allah Shakūr Appreciative. Allah is Shakūr, Appreciative, who thanks servants by rewarding abundantly, appreciating small striving with great reward. The person who desires Hereafter and strives with faith, even if striving imperfect, is appreciated, thanked, rewarded, not condemned. This contrasts with madhmūman madḥūran, condemned rejected, for fleeting desirer. The believer who reflects will desire Hereafter, strive its striving with faith, following most upright guidance, seeking bounty in world but with Hereafter as goal, so that striving is mashkūr, appreciated, leading to mighty reward and avoiding painful punishment, and record laid open will show appreciated striving.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:20

> **We provide both the former and the latter from the bounty of your Lord. And the bounty of your Lord can never be withheld.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**We Provide Both From Bounty**

Kullan numiddu hā'ulā'i wa-hā'ulā'i min ʿaṭā'i rabbika — each, all, We extend these and those from gift of your Lord. Kullan, each, all, everyone, both groups, from k-l-l, all, each. Numiddu from m-d-d, to extend, supply, provide, give مدد, to extend, to مدد, مدد, مدد, to مدد, to extend, to give abundantly, from madda, to extend, to مدد. Hā'ulā'i wa-hā'ulā'i, these and those, i.e., both groups: those desiring fleeting world and those desiring Hereafter, seekers of world and seekers of Hereafter, former and latter. Min ʿaṭā'i from ʿ-ṭ-w, gift, bounty, giving, from same root as ʿaṭā', gift, bounty, from aʿṭā, to give. Rabbika, your Lord, from r-b-b, Lord, master, sustainer, who provides. Al-Ṭabarī explains that each, both those who desire fleeting and those who desire Hereafter, We extend, provide, from gift of your Lord, meaning Allah provides for all, both believers and disbelievers, desirers of world and desirers of Hereafter, from His bounty in worldly life, worldly provision. Al-Qurṭubī says numiddu indicates continuous extension, provision, مدد, مدد, not one-time, and kullān indicates all are provided, no one excluded from worldly provision, both former and latter.

**Bounty Of Lord Never Withheld**

Wa-mā kāna ʿaṭā'u rabbika maḥẓūran — and bounty of your Lord is not withheld, not restricted, not forbidden, can never be withheld. Mā kāna, is not, was not, would never be. ʿAṭā'u, gift, bounty, from ʿ-ṭ-w, giving. Rabbika, your Lord. Maḥẓūran from ḥ-ẓ-r, to withhold, forbid, restrict, enclose, prevent, from same root as ḥaẓr, withholding, prohibition, and maḥẓūr, forbidden, withheld, restricted, from ḥaẓara, to forbid, enclose, prevent. Al-Ṭabarī says bounty of your Lord is not withheld, meaning His gift, provision, in world is not restricted from anyone, both groups receive, because worldly provision is general mercy, while Hereafter reward is specific for believers who strive and whose striving appreciated. Al-Rāzī says maḥẓūran means not forbidden, not prevented, not restricted, indicating that worldly bounty extends to all, even fleeting desirers, but Hereafter bounty only for Hereafter desirers who strive with faith, as distinction between general worldly gift and specific Hereafter reward.

**Why Both Provided From Bounty**

Why both provided from bounty? To show justice and mercy and wisdom: worldly provision given to all, not withheld, but Hereafter reward only for those who desire Hereafter and strive accordingly with faith. The person who desires fleeting alone still provided in world from bounty, as verse 18 said We hasten for him in it what We will to whoever We will, but then Hell condemned rejected. The person who desires Hereafter also provided from bounty in world, as he needs provision to strive, to seek bounty, and then appreciated striving in Hereafter with mighty reward. The verse therefore clarifies that provision in world does not indicate Allah's pleasure or displeasure; both groups provided from bounty, but end differs drastically. Worldly provision is not sign of success alone.

**Connection To Seeking Bounty And Knowing Calculation**

Verse 12 said We made day bright so you may seek bounty of your Lord and know number of years and calculation, and explained everything in detail. Verse 20 says each We extend these and those from gift of your Lord, bounty never withheld. The connection is that seeking bounty is from gift of Lord, and gift not withheld, so both groups can seek bounty, but intention differs: fleeting desirer seeks bounty as ultimate goal, Hereafter desirer seeks bounty as means to strive for Hereafter. The sūrah's moral code about giving relatives due, not spending wastefully, not being tight-fisted nor openhanded, etc., is about using bounty properly, not withholding, not wasting, using gift to desire Hereafter.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Provision In Due Measure**

Previous chapter said if Allah were to extend provision for His servants they would transgress in earth but He sends down in due measure what He wills, He is All-Aware, All-Seeing of His servants, and He sends water, subjects night and day, etc. This verse says each We extend these and those from gift of your Lord, bounty never withheld, indicating that extension of provision is from gift, measured as He wills, not withheld arbitrarily, but given to all as test. The provision is from gift, not earned solely, and bounty not withheld means no one prevented from receiving worldly provision if Allah wills, but Hereafter provision withheld from those who desire fleeting alone.

**Linguistic Note On Numiddu And Mahzur**

Kull from k-l-l, each, all. Numiddu from m-d-d, to extend, supply, give مدد, مدد, from madda, to extend, مدد, مدد, مدد, مدد, to مدد. Hā'ulā' from h-'-l-ā, these, those. ʿAṭā' from ʿ-ṭ-w, gift, giving, from aʿṭā, to give. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Mā kāna from k-w-n, to be. Maḥẓūr from ḥ-ẓ-r, to withhold, forbid, from ḥaẓara, to forbid, enclose. The verse's structure — each We extend these and those from gift of your Lord, and gift of your Lord is not withheld — with provision for both groups and statement that gift not restricted, shows general mercy in worldly provision and distinguishes from specific reward in Hereafter, which is withheld from fleeting desirers who get Hell condemned rejected.

**How This Verse Teaches Gratitude And Proper Use Of Bounty**

This verse teaches gratitude for bounty that is never withheld and proper use: since both groups provided from gift, use bounty to desire Hereafter and strive accordingly with faith, not only fleeting. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should be grateful that Lord's bounty not withheld, extends to all, both former and latter, and should use provision to strive for Hereafter, following most upright guidance, not to rebel like mutraf, elite, affluent who rebel when commanded. The practical lesson is to see worldly provision as gift from Lord, not as sign of success alone, and to use it to seek Hereafter, so that striving appreciated, not condemned rejected, and record laid open shows gratitude and good use.

**A Lesson On Bounty Never Withheld**

Wa-mā kāna ʿaṭā'u rabbika maḥẓūran — bounty of your Lord can never be withheld, is never withheld — is reassurance and warning: reassurance that provision not restricted, Allah provides for all, both former and latter, worldly gift extends, and warning that provision in world does not mean safety in Hereafter; both provided, but ends differ: fleeting desirer gets hastened provision but then Hell condemned rejected, Hereafter desirer gets provision in world and appreciated striving in Hereafter with mighty reward. The believer who reflects will use bounty never withheld to strive for Hereafter, seeking bounty of Lord with gratitude, knowing number of years and calculation, following most upright guidance, honoring parents, giving relatives due, etc., so that gift used well and leads to appreciated striving and mighty reward.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:21

> **See how We have favoured some over others ˹in this life˺, but the Hereafter is certainly far greater in rank and in favour.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**See How We Favoured Some Over Others In This Life**

Unẓur kayfa faḍḍalnā baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍin — see how We favoured some over others. Unẓur from n-ẓ-r, to see, look, consider, observe, as previous chapter said see how they call you names, and as Qur'an often says unẓur, look, consider. Kayfa, how, manner. Faḍḍalnā from f-ḍ-l, to favour, prefer, give bounty, superiority, from same root as faḍl, bounty, favour, grace, as verse 12 seeking bounty of Lord and verse 20 bounty never withheld and verse 21 favour. Baʿḍahum ʿalā baʿḍin, some over others, some above others, in provision, wealth, offspring, numbers, strength, knowledge, as verse 6 aided with wealth and offspring and made more numerous, and verse 20 We provide both former and latter from bounty, former desiring fleeting and latter desiring Hereafter. Al-Ṭabarī explains that see how We favoured some over others in this life, in provision, wealth, children, etc., some have abundant, some limited, as verse 30 says Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing. The difference in worldly favour is visible, observable, test, not hidden, and should be seen with reflection.

**Hereafter Far Greater In Rank And Favour**

Wa-la-l-ākhiratu akbaru darajātin wa-akbaru tafḍīlan — and Hereafter is certainly greater in rank and greater in favour. Wa-la-, and surely, and indeed, emphasis, la for emphasis. Al-ākhiratu, Hereafter, from ā-kh-r, last, latter, opposite of al-ūlā, first, world. Akbaru, greater, bigger, from k-b-r, great, big, from kabura. Darajātin from d-r-j, ranks, degrees, levels, steps, from same root as darajah, rank, degree, step, as Paradise has darajāt, ranks, levels, as hadith says Paradise has one hundred levels. Wa-akbaru tafḍīlan, and greater in favour, preference, superiority, from f-ḍ-l, favour, and tafḍīl, preferring, favouring, superiority, from faḍḍala. Al-Ṭabarī says Hereafter is far greater in rank, degrees, levels, and in favour, preference, meaning difference in Hereafter between people is far greater than difference in worldly life, ranks in Hereafter far higher, more significant, more lasting, than worldly ranks. Al-Rāzī says darajāt refers to ranks in Paradise, levels of reward, and tafḍīlan refers to favour, bounty, reward, superiority, and Hereafter greater in both than worldly differences, worldly difference small compared to Hereafter difference which is immense, eternal.

**Why This Verse Follows Provision And Bounty**

Verse 20 said We provide both former and latter from bounty of Lord, bounty never withheld, both groups provided, former desiring fleeting and latter desiring Hereafter. Verse 21 says see how We favoured some over others in this life, but Hereafter far greater in rank and favour. The connection is that provision from bounty is not equal: some favoured over others in worldly provision, as test, some abundant, some limited, but Hereafter difference far greater, so do not be deceived by worldly favouring, do not envy worldly favouring, focus on Hereafter where ranks and favour far greater, more important. Verse 18-19 contrasted fleeting desirer who gets hastened limited worldly then Hell condemned rejected vs Hereafter desirer whose striving appreciated, mighty reward. Verse 21 explains that worldly favouring is minor, temporary, Hereafter favouring major, lasting, so desire Hereafter, strive accordingly.

**Link To Abundant Or Limited Provision And Wisdom**

Verse 30 will say Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing of servants. Verse 21 previews that: We favoured some over others in this life, some abundant, some limited, but Hereafter greater in rank and favour. The difference in provision is by will and wisdom, not random, and is test, as previous chapter said if Allah were to extend provision for servants they would transgress but He sends down in due measure what He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing. The person who is favoured in world with abundant provision should be grateful, not arrogant like mutraf elite who rebelled when commanded, and who is not favoured, limited provision, should be patient, not despair, both should desire Hereafter where ranks far greater. The favouring some over others in world is part of divine wisdom to test gratitude and patience.

**Linguistic Note On Faḍḍala And Darajat And Tafdil**

Unẓur from n-ẓ-r, to see, look, consider, from naẓara. Kayfa from k-y-f, how. Faḍḍalnā from f-ḍ-l, to favour, prefer, from faḍala, to be superior, to favour, from faḍl, bounty, favour, superiority. Baʿḍ from b-ʿ-ḍ, some, part. Ākhirah from ā-kh-r, Hereafter, last, latter. Akbar from k-b-r, greater, from kabura, to be great. Darajāt from d-r-j, ranks, degrees, levels, from darajah, step, rank, from daraja, to step, to rank. Tafḍīlan from f-ḍ-l, favouring, preference, superiority, from faḍḍala, to prefer, favour. The verse's structure — see how We favoured some over others in this life, but Hereafter greater in rank and greater in favour — with command to see worldly difference and statement that Hereafter greater in two aspects, rank and favour, shows that worldly difference visible but minor compared to Hereafter which is far greater in both rank and favour, eternal.

**How This Verse Teaches Perspective And Not Envy**

This verse teaches perspective and avoidance of envy: see worldly favouring, some over others, but remember Hereafter far greater in rank and favour, so do not envy worldly favouring, do not be arrogant if favoured, do not despair if not favoured. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should not envy worldly favouring, because Hereafter ranks far greater, and should strive for Hereafter ranks, not only worldly, should use worldly favouring, whether abundant or limited, to desire Hereafter and strive accordingly with faith, so that striving appreciated and ranks in Hereafter far greater, mighty reward. The practical lesson is to use worldly favouring as test, to be grateful if abundant, patient if limited, and to focus on Hereafter where rank and favour far greater, eternal.

**A Reflection On Ranks And Favour In Hereafter**

Akbaru darajātin wa-akbaru tafḍīlan — greater in rank and greater in favour — indicates that Hereafter has ranks, degrees, levels, as Paradise has many levels, and favour, bounty, reward, superiority, far greater than worldly, which is limited, temporary. Worldly favouring some over others is limited, temporary, test, while Hereafter favouring is lasting, far greater, based on desire for Hereafter and striving with faith, appreciated striving. The person who desires fleeting alone may be favoured in worldly provision hastened, but Hereafter rank low, Hell condemned rejected; who desires Hereafter and strives has high rank in Hereafter, appreciated striving, mighty reward, far greater rank and favour. The believer who reflects will not be deceived by worldly favouring, will seek Hereafter where rank and favour far greater, and will follow most upright guidance to achieve it.

**Connection To Seeking Bounty And Using It**

Verse 12 said seek bounty of Lord and know number years and calculation, and verse 20 bounty never withheld, but some favoured over others in bounty, provision. The verse reminds that seeking bounty is allowed, even commanded, to seek bounty of Lord, but Hereafter far greater, so seek bounty as means to Hereafter, not as ultimate goal alone. The person who seeks bounty with gratitude and uses it to honor parents, give relatives due, poor, traveller, avoid waste, moderate, will have greater rank in Hereafter, far greater favour. The person who seeks bounty only for fleeting alone, without Hereafter, will have hastened limited worldly then Hell condemned rejected, losing far greater rank and favour in Hereafter.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:22

> **Do not set up any other god with Allah, or you will end up condemned, abandoned.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Set Up Other God With Allah**

Lā tajʿal maʿa Allāhi ilāhan ākhara — do not make with Allah another god. Lā tajʿal from j-ʿ-l, to make, set up, as verse 12 jaʿalnā night and day signs and verse 18 jaʿalnā for him Hell. Maʿa Allāhi, with Allah, alongside Allah. Ilāhan, god, deity, from a-l-h, to worship, god. Ākhara from ā-kh-r, other, another, from same root as ākhirah, Hereafter, other. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not set up with Allah another god, meaning do not associate partner with Allah in worship, do not worship other god besides Allah, as polytheists did with idols, angels as daughters, etc. This is first command in moral code that follows Qur'an guides to most upright: monotheism is most upright, foundation. Al-Qurṭubī says this is prohibition of shirk, associating partners, which is greatest sin, as previous chapter said those who set up partners have no knowledge.

**Condemned Abandoned**

Fa-taqʿuda madhmūman makhdhūlan — then you will sit condemned, abandoned, end up condemned, abandoned. Fa-taqʿuda from q-ʿ-d, to sit, to end up, to become, to remain, as sitting indicates ending, remaining. Madhmūman from dh-m-m, condemned, blamed, as verse 18 madhmūman, condemned, and verse 29 blameworthy. Makhdhūlan from kh-dh-l, to abandon, forsake, fail to support, from same root as khadhlān, abandonment, failure to help. Al-Ṭabarī says then you will end up condemned, blamed by Allah and people, and abandoned, forsaken, with no helper, no support, because other god you set up cannot help, cannot provide bounty that never withheld. Al-Rāzī says madhmūman refers to blame in world and Hereafter, makhdhūlan refers to being abandoned, forsaken, without support from Allah or from false gods.

**Why This Is First In Moral Code**

Why first? Because monotheism is foundation of most upright guidance. Qur'an guides to what is most upright, and most upright is to worship none but Allah, as verse 23 will say Lord decreed you worship none but Him. Setting up other god is most crooked, leads to condemned abandoned. The moral code that follows — honoring parents, giving relatives due, not spending wastefully, not killing children, not approaching adultery, etc. — all built on monotheism. Without monotheism, good deeds not accepted, as faith condition for appreciated striving. Verse 22 therefore opens moral code with prohibition of shirk, warning that if you set up other god, you end up condemned abandoned, as opposed to appreciated striving for Hereafter.

**Connection To Previous Verses About Bounty And Ranks**

Verses 20-21 said bounty never withheld, some favoured over others in world but Hereafter greater in rank and favour. Verse 22 says do not set up other god with Allah or you will end up condemned abandoned. The connection is that bounty from Lord, favouring, ranks, all from Allah alone, not from other gods, so do not set up other god, because other gods do not provide bounty, do not favour, do not give ranks. Setting up other god leads to condemned abandoned, because false gods cannot provide bounty that never withheld and cannot give Hereafter rank far greater. The person who desires Hereafter and strives should worship Allah alone to get appreciated striving.

**Link To Later Verse 39**

Verse 39 will say this is part of wisdom revealed to you, and do not set up other god with Allah or you will be cast into Hell blameworthy rejected, similar to verse 22 but more severe: here condemned abandoned, there blameworthy rejected cast into Hell. The pair frames moral code: begins with do not set up other god or condemned abandoned, ends with do not set up other god or cast into Hell blameworthy rejected, showing that shirk is at both ends, foundation and conclusion, most important. The moral code between is wisdom that is part of what Lord revealed.

**Linguistic Note On Tajʿal And Makhdhul**

Lā tajʿal from j-ʿ-l, to make, set up, from jaʿala. Maʿa, with. Allāh, Allah, God. Ilāh from a-l-h, god, deity, from aliha, to worship. Ākhar from ā-kh-r, other. Taqʿuda from q-ʿ-d, to sit, to end up, remain, from qaʿada. Madhmūm from dh-m-m, condemned, blamed, from dhamma, to blame. Makhdhūl from kh-dh-l, abandoned, forsaken, from khadhala, to abandon, fail to support. The verse's structure — do not make with Allah another god, then you will sit condemned abandoned — with prohibition and consequence, shows that shirk leads to condemnation and abandonment, sitting, remaining in that state, not fleeting.

**How This Verse Teaches Monotheism**

This verse teaches monotheism as foundation: worship Allah alone, do not set up other god, or you will end up condemned abandoned. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should worship Allah alone, not set up other god with Him, whether idol, angel, jinn, etc., because only Allah provides bounty never withheld, favours some over others, gives ranks far greater in Hereafter. The practical lesson is to check worship: is it for Allah alone? If you set up other god, you will be condemned by Allah and people and abandoned without helper, as false gods cannot help, as verse 56 will say invoke those you claim besides Him they do not have power to undo harm or transfer it. The believer who worships Allah alone will have appreciated striving and mighty reward, not condemned abandoned.

**A Reflection On Condemned Abandoned**

Madhmūman makhdhūlan — condemned, abandoned — two words describing state of polytheist: blamed, condemned, and forsaken, abandoned, without support. This contrasts with appreciated, mashkūran, for Hereafter desirer, and with supported by law for heir of unjustly killed, etc. The polytheist who sets up other god sits condemned abandoned, sitting indicates remaining, not moving, stuck in condemnation and abandonment. The monotheist who worships Allah alone is appreciated, supported, given bounty never withheld, favoured in Hereafter with far greater rank and favour. The choice is clear: monotheism leads to appreciation and support, shirk leads to condemnation and abandonment.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:23

> **For your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him. And honour your parents. If one or both of them reach old age in your care, never say to them ˹even˺ ‘ugh,’ nor yell at them. Rather, address them respectfully.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Lord Decreed Worship None But Him**

Wa-qaḍā rabbuka allā taʿbudū illā iyyāhu — and your Lord decreed that you worship none but Him. Wa-qaḍā from q-ḍ-y, to decree, judge, ordain, decide, from same root as qaḍā', decree, judgment. Rabbuka, your Lord, from r-b-b. Allā taʿbudū, that you not worship, from ʿ-b-d, to worship, serve, as ʿibād, servants. Illā iyyāhu, except Him, only Him, from a-l-l and pronoun. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Lord decreed, ordained, commanded decisively, that you worship none but Him alone, no other god with Allah, as previous verse prohibited setting up other god. Qaḍā indicates decisive decree, not mere suggestion, as judge decrees. Al-Qurṭubī says qaḍā here means commanded, ordained, made incumbent, that you worship none but Him, which is foundation of most upright guidance, as Qur'an guides to most upright.

**Honour Your Parents**

Wa-bi-l-wālidayni iḥsānan — and with parents good, excellence, honour your parents. Wa-, and. Bi-l-wālidayni, with parents, to parents, from w-l-d, to beget, parents who beget, dual. Iḥsānan from ḥ-s-n, good, excellence, beautiful doing, from same root as aḥsantum, you do good, in verse 7, and muḥsin, doer of good. Al-Ṭabarī says and honour your parents, do good to parents with excellence, as parents are cause of existence after Allah, and honouring them is most upright after worshipping Allah alone. Al-Rāzī says iḥsānan indicates doing good with excellence, not merely not harming, but doing good excellently. This is second command after monotheism in moral code, showing importance of parents.

**If Parents Reach Old Age In Your Care**
