Immā yablughanna ʿindaka al-kibara aḥaduhumā aw kilāhumā — if one or both reach old age with you, in your care. Immā, if, either. Yablughanna from b-l-gh, to reach, attain, from balagha, to reach, with emphasis, indeed reach. ʿIndaka, with you, in your care, near you, from ʿ-n-d. Al-kibara from k-b-r, old age, greatness, from kabura, to be great, old, from same root as akbar, greater. Aḥaduhumā, one of them, from a-ḥ-d, one. Aw kilāhumā, or both of them, from k-l-l, both. Al-Ṭabarī explains that if one or both parents reach old age with you, in your care, under your protection, you must honour them especially in old age when they are weak, needy. Old age is mentioned because need greatest then, and because parents in old age may be difficult, so command to be patient.

**Never Say Ugh Nor Yell**

Fa-lā taqul lahumā uffin wa-lā tanharhumā — then do not say to them even ugh, nor yell at them, scold. Fa-lā taqul from q-w-l, to say. Lahumā, to them, dual. Uffin, ugh, expression of annoyance, disgust, from a-f-f, to be annoyed, ugh. Wa-lā tanharhumā from n-h-r, to yell, scold, rebuke harshly, from nahara, to scold, to drive away. Al-Ṭabarī says do not say to them even ugh, smallest expression of annoyance, nor yell at them, scold them harshly. Uff is minimal word of annoyance, so if even that prohibited, greater annoyance more prohibited. Al-Qurṭubī says uff indicates any expression of disrespect, even slight, and tanharhumā indicates yelling, harsh rebuke, both prohibited, showing that parents in old age must be treated with utmost respect, patience.

**Address Them Respectfully**

Wa-qul lahumā qawlan karīman — and say to them noble word, respectful word. Wa-qul from q-w-l, to say. Lahumā, to them. Qawlan karīman, noble word, respectful, generous word, from q-w-l and k-r-m, noble, generous, honourable, from karuma, to be noble. Al-Ṭabarī says say to them noble, respectful, generous word, meaning gentle, kind, honourable speech, not harsh. Al-Rāzī says qawlan karīman includes respectful address, kind words, honouring, not demeaning. This is positive command after negative prohibitions: not only avoid ugh and yelling, but say respectful word.

**Why Parents After Monotheism**

Why parents after monotheism? Because parents are cause of existence after Allah, and gratitude to parents after gratitude to Allah, as Qur'an often pairs worship Allah and honour parents. Also because human is ever hasty and may be annoyed with old parents, so command to be patient and respectful, especially when they reach old age in your care. The sūrah's theme of gratitude appears: grateful servant Noah, gratitude for bounty never withheld, now gratitude to parents who raised when young, as next verse says be merciful to them as they raised me when young.

**Linguistic Note On Qada And Uff And Karim**

Qaḍā from q-ḍ-y, to decree, judge, ordain. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Taʿbudū from ʿ-b-d, to worship. Iyyāhu, Him, pronoun. Wālidayn from w-l-d, parents, dual of wālid, begetter. Iḥsān from ḥ-s-n, excellence, good. Yablughanna from b-l-gh, to reach, with emphasis. ʿIndaka from ʿ-n-d, with you. Kibar from k-b-r, old age. Aḥad from a-ḥ-d, one. Kilāhumā from k-l-l, both. Taqul from q-w-l, to say. Uff from a-f-f, ugh, annoyance. Tanhar from n-h-r, to yell, scold. Qawl from q-w-l, word. Karīm from k-r-m, noble, generous, honourable. The verse's structure — Lord decreed worship none but Him and with parents excellence, if one or both reach old age with you do not say ugh nor yell, but say noble word — with decree of monotheism, command of parents excellence, specific case of old age, prohibitions, positive command, shows comprehensive guidance for most upright dealing with parents.

**How This Verse Teaches Honouring Parents**

This verse teaches honouring parents with excellence, especially in old age in your care, never saying even ugh nor yelling, but addressing respectfully. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should honour parents with excellence, do good to them, be patient when they reach old age, avoid even smallest annoyance expression, avoid yelling, and say noble, respectful words. The practical lesson is to be mindful that parents in old age are test, as they raised you when young with mercy, so be merciful as next verse says pray My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me when young. The verse also teaches that monotheism and honouring parents are foundation of most upright, as Qur'an guides to most upright.

**A Reflection On Uff And Noble Word**

Uffin — ugh — smallest word of annoyance, prohibited, showing how sensitive honouring parents is. If even ugh prohibited, greater disrespect more prohibited. Qawlan karīman — noble, respectful word — positive, showing that speech to parents should be noble, generous, honourable, not demeaning. The believer who reflects will avoid ugh and yelling even when parents old and difficult, and will say noble word, as gratitude for raising when young, and as part of striving for Hereafter whose striving appreciated and ranks far greater.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:24

> **And be humble with them out of mercy, and pray, “My Lord! Be merciful to them as they raised me when I was young.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Be Humble With Them Out Of Mercy**

Wa-ikhfiḍ lahumā janāḥa al-dhulli mina al-raḥmati — and lower for them wing of humility out of mercy. Wa-ikhfiḍ from kh-f-ḍ, to lower, humble, from khafaḍa, to lower, to be humble. Lahumā, for them, to them, dual parents. Janāḥa from j-n-ḥ, wing, side, from janāḥ, wing, as bird lowers wing. Al-dhulli from dh-l-l, humility, lowness, from dhalla, to be humble, lowly, from dhull, humility, not humiliation. Mina al-raḥmati, out of mercy, from raḥmah, mercy, from r-ḥ-m, mercy, womb. Al-Ṭabarī explains that lower for them wing of humility out of mercy, meaning be humble with them, lower yourself, as bird lowers wing for its chicks, out of mercy, compassion. Al-Qurṭubī says janāḥ al-dhull metaphor for humility, lowering wing indicates softness, humility, not arrogance, as bird lowers wing to protect and show humility. Mina al-raḥmati indicates motivation: out of mercy, compassion, not out of fear or show.

**Pray My Lord Be Merciful To Them As They Raised Me When Young**

Wa-qul rabbi irḥamhumā kamā rabbayānī ṣaghīran — and say My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me small, young. Wa-qul from q-w-l, to say, pray. Rabbi from r-b-b, My Lord. Irḥamhumā from r-ḥ-m, to be merciful, mercy, dual parents, from raḥima, to have mercy. Kamā, as, like, as. Rabbayānī from r-b-b, to raise, nurture, educate, from rabbā, to raise, same root as rabb, Lord who raises. Ṣaghīran from ṣ-gh-r, small, young, from ṣaghura, to be small. Al-Ṭabarī says pray My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me when young, meaning ask Allah to have mercy on parents as they had mercy raising you small, young, weak, needing care. Al-Rāzī says this supplication teaches gratitude and reciprocity: as they raised you with mercy when young, ask Allah to be merciful to them when old, in your care.

**Why Wing Of Humility**

Why wing? Because wing is used for protection, softness, humility. Bird lowers wing for chicks out of mercy, to protect, to show humility. Lowering wing of humility for parents indicates being humble, soft, protective, not arrogant, not raising self above them. The phrase janāḥa al-dhulli mina al-raḥmati, wing of humility out of mercy, combines humility and mercy: humility is attitude, mercy is motivation. The believer should be humble with parents out of mercy, compassion, remembering they raised him when young with mercy.

**Connection To Previous Verse About Ugh And Noble Word**

Verse 23 said if parents reach old age in your care, never say even ugh nor yell, but address respectfully. Verse 24 says be humble with them out of mercy and pray My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me when young. The connection is that previous verse prohibited disrespectful speech and commanded respectful speech, this verse commands humble attitude and supplication. Together they cover speech and attitude and prayer: do not say ugh, do not yell, say noble word, lower wing of humility out of mercy, pray for mercy for them as they raised you young. Comprehensive honouring parents.

**Link To Gratitude And Raising**

Rabbayānī ṣaghīran — they raised me when young — indicates parents' raising, nurturing, with mercy, when child small, weak, needy. This is reason for mercy prayer: as they raised with mercy when young, be merciful to them when old. The root r-b-b, to raise, same as rabb, Lord, indicates parents' raising is like Lord's raising, nurturing. Gratitude to parents for raising is part of gratitude to Lord who decreed worship none but Him and honour parents. The sūrah's theme of gratitude appears again.

**Linguistic Note On Ikhfid And Janah And Rabbayani**

Ikhfiḍ from kh-f-ḍ, to lower, humble, from khafaḍa. Lahumā, to them dual. Janāḥ from j-n-ḥ, wing, side. Dhull from dh-l-l, humility, lowness, from dhalla. Raḥmah from r-ḥ-m, mercy, from raḥima. Qul from q-w-l, to say. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord, My Lord. Irḥam from r-ḥ-m, be merciful. Kama from k-m, as. Rabbayānī from r-b-b, to raise, nurture, from rabbā. Ṣaghīr from ṣ-gh-r, small, young. The verse's structure — lower for them wing of humility out of mercy and say My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me young — with attitude command and supplication, shows that honouring parents includes humility and prayer for mercy, not only respectful speech.

**How This Verse Teaches Humility And Supplication For Parents**

This verse teaches humility with parents out of mercy and supplication for them: be humble, lower wing of humility, out of mercy, compassion, not arrogance, and pray My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me when young. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should be humble with parents, lower self, be soft, out of mercy, remembering they raised him when young with mercy, and should pray for them for mercy, both in life and after death. The practical lesson is to show humility, not arrogance, with old parents, to be merciful, and to pray for them regularly: My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me when young, as gratitude and reciprocity.

**A Reflection On Mercy As They Raised**

Kamā rabbayānī ṣaghīran — as they raised me when young — is powerful reminder that parents raised you when you were small, weak, needy, with mercy, patience, care. Now when they are old in your care, you should be merciful as they were merciful. The supplication My Lord be merciful to them as they raised me young asks Allah to be merciful to them with same mercy they showed raising you. This teaches gratitude, humility, mercy, and supplication. The believer who reflects will lower wing of humility out of mercy, not be arrogant, not say ugh, not yell, say noble word, and pray for parents' mercy as they raised him young, as part of most upright guidance that leads to mighty reward and appreciated striving.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:25

> **Your Lord knows best what is within yourselves. If you are righteous, He is certainly All-Forgiving to those who ˹constantly˺ turn to Him.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Lord Knows Best What Is Within Yourselves**

Rabbukum aʿlamu bi-mā fī nufūsikum — your Lord knows best what is in your selves, within yourselves. Rabbukum, your Lord, from r-b-b. Aʿlamu, knows best, more knowing, from ʿ-l-m, to know. Bi-mā, with what, about what. Fī nufūsikum, in your selves, within yourselves, in your souls, from n-f-s, self, soul, plural nufūs. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Lord knows best what is within yourselves, what is in your hearts, intentions, regarding parents, whether you intend good, honour, humility, mercy, or otherwise. He knows inner, not only outer speech and action. Al-Rāzī says this indicates that Allah knows inner thoughts about parents, whether sincere honour or grudging, and that He knows best what is within selves generally, not only about parents, but about all intentions.

**If You Are Righteous He Is All-Forgiving To Those Who Constantly Turn**

In takūnū ṣāliḥīna fa-innahu kāna li-l-awwābīna ghafūran — if you are righteous, then He is for those who constantly turn, ever-turning, All-Forgiving. In, if. Takūnū from k-w-n, to be. Ṣāliḥīna from ṣ-l-ḥ, righteous, good, from ṣalaḥa, to be righteous, good, from same root as ṣāliḥ, righteous, and iṣlāḥ, reform. Fa-innahu, then He, then indeed He. Kāna, is, was, indicates He is. Li-l-awwābīna from a-w-b, to turn, return, from āba, to return, and awwāb, one who constantly turns, returns to Allah, ever-returning, from same root as tawbah, repentance, turning. Ghafūran from gh-f-r, All-Forgiving, forgiving, from ghafara, to forgive. Al-Ṭabarī explains that if you are righteous, intending good to parents, honouring them, even if you fall short or have bad thoughts due to human weakness, He is All-Forgiving to those who constantly turn to Him, return to Him in repentance, asking forgiveness. Al-Qurṭubī says awwāb is one who constantly turns to Allah, returns, repents, and that Allah is All-Forgiving to such.

**Why This Verse Follows Honouring Parents**

Verses 23-24 commanded honouring parents with excellence, not saying ugh nor yelling, saying noble word, lowering wing of humility out of mercy, praying for mercy as they raised you young. Verse 25 says Lord knows best what is within yourselves, if you are righteous He is All-Forgiving to those who constantly turn to Him. The connection is that honouring parents, especially old parents in your care, is difficult, may lead to annoyance, ugh, etc., due to human weakness, haste, as human is ever hasty. Allah knows what is within yourselves, knows if you intend righteousness even if you slip, and He is All-Forgiving to those who constantly turn, meaning if you are righteous in intention and you slip with ugh or annoyance due to haste, then turn back, repent, He forgives. This is mercy and encouragement: strive to be righteous with parents, and if you fail due to weakness, turn back, He is All-Forgiving to awwābīn.

**Link To Human Hasty And Self-Reckoning**

Verse 11 said human prays for evil as for good, is ever hasty, and verse 14 said sufficient yourself as reckoner. Verse 25 says Lord knows best what is within yourselves, if righteous He is All-Forgiving to those who constantly turn. The link is that human hasty may say ugh or yell out of haste, but Lord knows inner intention, whether righteous or not, and forgives those who constantly turn, return. This encourages self-reckoning and turning: if you are righteous, even if you have bad thoughts, turn, He forgives. It also links to destiny bound to neck and record laid open: Lord knows what is within selves that will be in record, and forgives awwābīn.

**Linguistic Note On Nufus And Awwab And Ghafur**

Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Aʿlam from ʿ-l-m, more knowing, knows best. Mā fī, what in. Nufūs from n-f-s, selves, souls, plural of nafs. Takūnū from k-w-n, to be. Ṣāliḥīn from ṣ-l-ḥ, righteous, from ṣalaḥa. Awwābīn from a-w-b, constantly turning, ever-returning, from āba, to return, intensive form awwāb, one who returns often. Ghafūr from gh-f-r, All-Forgiving, intensive, from ghafara, to forgive. The verse's structure — your Lord knows best what is in your selves, if you are righteous then He is for awwābīn All-Forgiving — with knowledge of inner and condition of righteousness and forgiveness for returners, shows that Allah knows inner, judges by righteousness, and forgives those who constantly turn.

**How This Verse Teaches Sincerity And Repentance**

This verse teaches sincerity and repentance: Lord knows best what is within yourselves, so be sincere, righteous in intention, especially regarding parents, and if you slip, be among awwābīn, those who constantly turn, return to Him, repent, He is All-Forgiving. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should be righteous, intend good, honour parents sincerely, and if he has bad thoughts or says ugh out of human weakness, he should turn, repent, as awwāb, and Allah is All-Forgiving to awwābīn. The practical lesson is to be mindful that Allah knows inner, so purify intention, be righteous, and constantly turn, repent, as human is ever hasty and may err.

**A Reflection On Awwab**

Awwāb — one who constantly turns, returns, ever-returning — from a-w-b, to return, is beautiful attribute: one who returns to Allah often, after slipping, after sin, after annoyance, after haste. Allah is Ghafūr, All-Forgiving, to awwābīn, those who constantly turn. This is mercy: even if you are not perfect in honouring parents, if you are righteous in intention and constantly turn, return, repent, He forgives. The believer who reflects will strive to be ṣāliḥ, righteous, honour parents with excellence, avoid ugh and yelling, say noble word, lower wing of humility out of mercy, pray for parents, and if he slips due to haste, he will be awwāb, constantly turning, returning to Allah, seeking forgiveness, knowing Lord knows best what is within self and is All-Forgiving to awwābīn.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:26

> **Give to close relatives their due, as well as the poor and ˹needy˺ travellers. And do not spend wastefully.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Give To Close Relatives Their Due**

Wa-āti dhā al-qurbā ḥaqqahu — and give possessor of closeness his due, his right. Wa-āti from a-t-y, to give, bring, from ātā, to give, to bring, from same root as ītā', giving. Dhā al-qurbā from q-r-b, closeness, nearness, relatives, kinship, from qaruba, to be near, close, and qurbā, closeness, kinship, nearness. Ḥaqqahu from ḥ-q-q, due, right, truth, incumbent, from ḥaqq, right, due, truth, from ḥaqqa, to be true, incumbent. Al-Ṭabarī explains that give to close relatives their due, their right, meaning give relatives what is due to them of kindness, support, maintaining ties, financial help if needed, as part of honouring ties after honouring parents, which was previous command. Close relatives, dhū al-qurbā, includes parents, children, siblings, uncles, aunts, cousins, extended family, those near in kinship. Ḥaqq, due, indicates they have right, not merely optional charity, but right, incumbent, as in wealth there is right for asker and deprived. Al-Qurṭubī says this includes both financial due and moral due: visiting, kindness, support, maintaining kinship, ṣilat al-raḥim, which is emphasized in Islam, as severing kinship is major sin.

**Poor And Needy Travellers**

Wa-al-miskīna wa-ibna al-sabīli — and the poor and son of way, traveller, needy traveller. Wa-al-miskīna from m-s-k, poor, needy, destitute, from maskana, poverty, stillness, from miskīn, poor, one who is still due to poverty, unable to move, needy. Wa-ibna al-sabīli from b-n-y and s-b-l, son of way, traveller, wayfarer, one whose way, journey, cut off, needy traveller, from sabīl, way, path, road. Al-Ṭabarī says give to poor and needy travellers their due as well, as part of using bounty that never withheld to help those in need, deserving. Poor, miskīn, is one who is poor, needy, unable to meet needs, destitute, even if not asking. Ibn al-sabīl, son of way, is traveller cut off from wealth, stranded, even if rich at home, needy on way, traveller who lost provision, as wayfarer. Al-Rāzī says giving to relatives, poor, traveller are three categories of giving that cover kinship and general need and temporary need, comprehensive, covering near and far, permanent and temporary, showing most upright spending.

**Do Not Spend Wastefully**

Wa-lā tubadh-dhir tabdhīran — and do not spend wastefully, squander with wasteful spending. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition. Tubadh-dhir from b-dh-r, to spend wastefully, squander, scatter, waste, from badhdhara, to waste, squander, scatter, as scattering seeds wastefully. Tabdhīran, wastefully, with emphasis, wasteful spending, squandering, scattering, from same root, intensive verbal noun, mafʿūl muṭlaq for emphasis. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not spend wastefully, squander wealth in disobedience or in unnecessary extravagance, as wasteful spending is prohibited, is spending without right, without benefit, in sin or excess. Al-Qurṭubī says tabdhīr is spending in falsehood, disobedience, sin, or in extravagance beyond need, scattering wealth without benefit, which is characteristic of mutraf, affluent elite who rebelled when commanded, as previous verse 16 said elite rebelled. The prohibition follows command to give to relatives, poor, traveller, indicating give due but do not waste beyond due, be moderate, not wasteful.

**Why Relatives, Poor, Traveller Together**

Why these three together? Because they represent most deserving of giving, most entitled: relatives have right of kinship, womb, raḥim, poor have right of need, deprivation, traveller has right of temporary need, being cut off, stranger. Giving to them is using bounty never withheld properly, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to. The sūrah's theme of bounty appears: bounty never withheld, some favoured over others, provision abundant or limited by will, All-Aware All-Seeing, so use bounty to give due to those deserving, not to waste. The command to give due to relatives, poor, traveller is part of moral code that is wisdom, most upright, that leads to appreciated striving and mighty reward, and avoids painful punishment and being brother to devils.

**Connection To Not Being Tight-Fisted Nor Openhanded And Kind Word**

Verse 28 will say if you must turn down because you lack means while hoping for Lord's bounty, then give kind word, gentle. Verse 29 will say do not be tight-fisted, chained hand to neck, blameworthy, nor openhanded, fully extended, depleted in poverty. Verse 26 says give to relatives their due, poor, traveller, and do not spend wastefully. The connection is that giving due is commanded, wasteful spending prohibited, and being tight-fisted also not allowed, as withholding due, and being openhanded to poverty also not allowed, as wasteful. Moderation is required: give due to deserving, do not waste, do not be tight-fisted, do not be openhanded to poverty, and if lack means, give kind word while hoping for bounty. The balance is most upright, middle path, wasat.

**Link To Wasteful Are Brothers To Devils**

Next verse says wasteful are like brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful to his Lord. So tabdhīr, wasteful spending, makes one brother to devils, companion of devils, because devils are ungrateful, wasteful, spend in falsehood. The prohibition of wasteful spending is serious: makes one like brother to devils, companion in ungratefulness, disobedience. Therefore give due but do not waste, be moderate, grateful, not ungrateful like Devil.

**Linguistic Note On Qurbā And Miskīn And Tabdhīr**

Āti from a-t-y, to give, from ātā. Dhā from dh-w, possessor. Qurbā from q-r-b, closeness, kinship, nearness, from qaruba. Ḥaqq from ḥ-q-q, due, right, truth. Miskīn from m-s-k, poor, from sakana, to be still, poor still, unable. Ibn from b-n-y, son. Sabīl from s-b-l, way, path. Tubadh-dhir from b-dh-r, to waste, squander, from badhdhara, to scatter wastefully. Tabdhīran, wasteful spending, from same root, intensive verbal noun for emphasis. The verse's structure — give possessor of closeness his due and poor and son of way, and do not waste wastefully — with positive command to give three categories and negative prohibition of waste with emphasis, shows balance: give due to deserving, avoid waste, moderation, most upright.

**How This Verse Teaches Giving And Moderation And Gratitude**

This verse teaches giving due to relatives, poor, traveller, and avoiding wasteful spending, with gratitude. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should give to close relatives their due, maintaining kinship, helping financially if needed, visiting, kindness, and to poor and needy travellers, using bounty never withheld, provision abundant or limited, and should not spend wastefully, squander, as wasteful are brothers to devils, ever ungrateful. The practical lesson is to use provision, whether abundant or limited, All-Aware All-Seeing distribution, to give due to deserving, not to waste in disobedience or extravagance, and to be moderate, as most upright guidance teaches, wisdom. The verse also teaches that relatives have due, right, not optional charity, so maintain ties, give due, as gratitude to Lord who gave bounty.

**A Reflection On Due And Waste And Brotherhood To Devils**

Ḥaqqahu — his due, right — indicates relatives, poor, traveller have right in your wealth, as Qur'an says in wealth there is right for asker and deprived, and as zakat, charity, due. Giving due is justice, gratitude, not favour, not waste. Tabdhīran — wastefully — indicates spending without right, without benefit, in disobedience, extravagance, which makes one brother to devils, companion, as next verse says, because Devil ever ungrateful. The believer who reflects will give due to relatives, poor, traveller as right, using bounty never withheld, provision abundant or limited with awareness, and will avoid wasteful spending that makes one brother to devils, being moderate, grateful, not ungrateful, as part of most upright guidance that leads to appreciated striving, mighty reward, and avoids painful punishment and condemnation.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:27

> **Surely the wasteful are ˹like˺ brothers to the devils. And the Devil is ever ungrateful to his Lord.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Wasteful Are Brothers To Devils**

Inna al-mubadh-dhirīna kānū ikhwāna al-shayāṭīni — indeed wasteful are brothers of devils. Inna, indeed, surely, emphasis. Al-mubadh-dhirīna from b-dh-r, wasteful, squanderers, those who spend wastefully, extravagantly, from badhdhara, to waste, squander, scatter wastefully, intensive form mubadh-dhir, one who wastes much, squanderer. Kānū, are, were, indicates they are, were, state. Ikhwāna from a-kh-w, brothers, companions, close friends, like brothers, from akh, brother, plural ikhwān, brothers, companions. Al-shayāṭīni from sh-ṭ-n, devils, satans, from shaṭana, to be far, distant, devil far from good, mercy, truth, plural of shayṭān, devil, Satan. Al-Ṭabarī explains that wasteful, those who spend wastefully, squander wealth in disobedience, sin, or extravagance beyond need, without benefit, are like brothers to devils, companions of devils, friends of devils, because devils waste, spend in falsehood, disobey, are ungrateful, and command wasteful spending. Being brothers indicates similarity in character, companionship, following same path, sharing traits. Al-Qurṭubī says ikhwān al-shayāṭīn means they are like brothers to devils, companions, friends, followers, because they obey devils in wasteful spending, as devils command waste, as Satan incites with voice, cavalry, infantry, wealth, children, promises delusion.

**Devil Ever Ungrateful To Lord**

Wa-kāna al-shayṭānu li-rabbihi kafūran — and Devil is to his Lord ever ungrateful, very ungrateful, intensely ungrateful. Wa-kāna, and is, was, indicates state, he is. Al-shayṭānu, Devil, Satan, Iblīs, from sh-ṭ-n, devil. Li-rabbihi, to his Lord, towards his Lord, from r-b-b, Lord, master, sustainer who gave bounty. Kafūran from k-f-r, ungrateful, disbelieving, very ungrateful, intensive, from kafara, to disbelieve, be ungrateful, deny blessings, from same root as kufr, disbelief, ungratefulness, ingratitude, and kāfir, disbeliever, ungrateful, and kafūr, very ungrateful. Al-Ṭabarī says Devil is ever ungrateful to his Lord, very ungrateful, denying blessings, disobeying, refusing to be grateful, as he refused to prostrate to Adam out of arrogance, was ungrateful for honour, for creation, for command. Al-Rāzī says kafūran indicates intensive, ever, very ungrateful, characteristic of Devil, who is ungrateful despite bounty, and wasteful who wastes bounty is also ungrateful, like brother.

**Why Wasteful Brothers To Devils And Ungrateful**

Why brothers? Because both wasteful and devils share characteristics: spending in falsehood, disobedience, sin, extravagance, ungratefulness, leading astray, commanding evil. Wasteful spends bounty never withheld, provision abundant or limited, in disobedience, extravagance, without benefit, which is ungrateful to Lord who gave bounty, who favoured some over others, who gives abundant or limited with awareness. Devil is ever ungrateful to Lord, kafūran, very ungrateful, so wasteful who wastes bounty is like brother to Devil, companion in ungratefulness, sharing trait of ingratitude. The phrase ikhwāna al-shayāṭīn indicates that wasteful are companions, friends, followers of devils, as devils command wasteful spending, incite to waste, as verse 64 will say Satan promises delusion and incites with voice, cavalry, infantry, manipulates wealth and children.

**Connection To Giving Due And Not Spending Wastefully And Moderation**

Verse 26 said give to close relatives their due, poor and needy travellers, and do not spend wastefully, tabdhīr prohibited. Verse 27 says wasteful are like brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful to Lord. The connection is that prohibition of wasteful spending is emphasized by stating that wasteful are brothers to devils, companions, and devils ungrateful, so wasteful spending is ungratefulness, characteristic of devils, serious sin, not minor. Giving due to relatives, poor, traveller is grateful use of bounty, not wasteful, gratitude, while wasteful spending is ungrateful, like Devil, ingratitude. The sūrah's theme of gratitude appears: grateful servant Noah, verse 3, gratitude for bounty never withheld, now ungrateful Devil as opposite of grateful servant, showing two paths: grateful vs ungrateful, wasteful brother to devils vs giving due grateful.

**Link To Satan Seeks Discord And No Authority Over Faithful Servants**

Later verses 53 and 65 will say Satan seeks to sow discord among servants, tell believing servants to say only best, Satan sworn enemy, and you will have no authority over My faithful servants, sufficient Lord as Guardian. Wasteful being brothers to devils indicates that wasteful follows Satan who seeks discord, who commands waste, but faithful servants who give due and avoid waste have no authority for Satan over them, Lord is Guardian, sufficient. The verse therefore warns against following Satan via wasteful spending, tabdhīr, which makes one brother to devils, and encourages being faithful servant who gives due, avoids waste, says best, so Satan has no authority.

**Linguistic Note On Mubadhdhir And Ikhwan And Kafur**

Inna from a-n-n, indeed, surely. Mubadh-dhirīn from b-dh-r, wasteful, squanderers, from badhdhara, to waste, squander, intensive form mubadh-dhir, one who wastes much, from tabdhīr, wasteful spending. Kānū from k-w-n, to be. Ikhwān from a-kh-w, brothers, companions, plural of akh, brother, from akhawa, to be brother. Shayāṭīn from sh-ṭ-n, devils, plural of shayṭān, from shaṭana, to be far, distant, far from good. Shayṭān from same root, devil far from mercy. Kafūr from k-f-r, very ungrateful, intensive, from kafara, to be ungrateful, disbelieve, from kufr, ingratitude, disbelief, intensive form kafūr, ever ungrateful. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. The verse's structure — indeed wasteful are brothers of devils, and Devil is to his Lord ever ungrateful — with statement of brotherhood and reason of ungratefulness, shows that wasteful spending is ungratefulness, characteristic of Devil, serious, so avoid, be grateful.

**How This Verse Teaches Gratitude Vs Waste And Ingratitude**

This verse teaches gratitude vs waste and ingratitude: wasteful are brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful to Lord, so wasteful spending is ungratefulness, characteristic of devils, avoid. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should avoid wasteful spending, tabdhīr, which is spending in disobedience, sin, or extravagance beyond need, without benefit, because it makes one like brother to devils, companion, friend of devils, and is ungratefulness to Lord who gave bounty never withheld, who favoured some over others, who gives abundant or limited with awareness, All-Aware All-Seeing. Instead, give due to relatives, poor, traveller, which is grateful use of bounty, gratitude, not waste. The practical lesson is to use bounty with gratitude, not waste, to be moderate, not tight-fisted nor openhanded, as most upright guidance teaches, wisdom, and to remember Devil ever ungrateful, so do not be like him, be grateful like Noah grateful servant.

**A Reflection On Brotherhood To Devils And Ever Ungrateful**

Ikhwāna al-shayāṭīn — brothers to devils — strong phrase indicating close companionship, similarity, following, sharing traits, will be with devils in Hereafter, as companions. Wasteful are like brothers to devils, meaning they follow devils, obey devils, share characteristics of waste, disobedience, ungratefulness, will be with devils. Devil ever ungrateful to Lord, kafūran, intensive, ever, very ungrateful, despite bounty, despite honour, despite command, ungrateful. The believer who reflects will avoid wasteful spending that makes one brother to devils, will be grateful for bounty never withheld, give due to deserving, be moderate, not tight-fisted nor openhanded, give kind word when lack while hoping for bounty, and will seek protection from devils who have no authority over faithful servants, Lord sufficient as Guardian, as most upright guidance.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:28

> **But if you must turn them down ˹because you lack the means to give˺—while hoping to receive your Lord’s bounty—then ˹at least˺ give them a kind word.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**If You Must Turn Them Down While Hoping For Bounty**

Wa-immā tuʿriḍanna ʿanhum ibtighā'a raḥmatin min rabbika tarjūhā — and if you turn away from them seeking mercy from your Lord you hope. Wa-immā, and if, but if, conditional with emphasis. Tuʿriḍanna from ʿ-r-ḍ, to turn away, avoid, from aʿraḍa, to turn away, with emphasis, indeed turn away. ʿAnhum, from them, from relatives, poor, traveller mentioned previous, those asking. Ibtighā'a from b-gh-y, seeking, desire, from ibtaghā, to seek, as verse 12 li-tabtaghū faḍlan, to seek bounty. Raḥmatin from r-ḥ-m, mercy, bounty, from raḥmah, mercy. Min rabbika, from your Lord, from r-b-b. Tarjūhā from r-j-w, to hope, expect, from rajā, hope. Al-Ṭabarī explains that if you must turn away from them, relatives, poor, traveller, because you lack means to give, while hoping to receive mercy, bounty, provision from Lord, so you can give later, then give kind word. This indicates situation where believer wants to give but lacks means, hopes for Lord's bounty to give, so turns away temporarily, not out of stinginess but lack.

**Give Them Kind Word**

Fa-qul lahum qawlan maysūran — then say to them gentle, kind, easy word. Fa-qul from q-w-l, to say. Lahum, to them. Qawlan maysūran from y-s-r, gentle, kind, easy, soft, from maysūr, easy, gentle, from yasura, to be easy, from same root as yusr, ease, and maysarah, ease. Al-Ṭabarī says then say to them gentle, kind word, easy, soft, promising good, not harsh, as at least give kind word if cannot give wealth. Al-Qurṭubī says qawlan maysūran means gentle, kind, easy word, like saying Allah will provide for us and you, or I will give when I have, or kind promise, not harsh rejection. This is minimum when cannot give due to lack of means while hoping for bounty.

**Why Kind Word When Cannot Give**

Why kind word? Because giving due to relatives, poor, traveller is commanded, but if you lack means, you cannot give, but you can give kind word, which is also good, maintains ties, avoids hurting. The sūrah's moral code emphasizes both financial giving and good speech: honouring parents with noble word, now kind word to those asking when cannot give. The person who lacks means but hopes for Lord's bounty and gives kind word is not wasteful nor tight-fisted, but moderate, seeking bounty to give later. This is most upright: give if you can, if cannot, give kind word while hoping for bounty.

**Connection To Bounty Never Withheld And Provision Abundant Or Limited**

Verse 20 said bounty never withheld, verse 21 some favoured over others, verse 30 Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills. Verse 28 says if you must turn them down because you lack means while hoping to receive Lord's bounty, then give kind word. The connection is that lack of means is because Lord gives limited provisions to whoever He wills, as test, but bounty never withheld in general, so hope for bounty, for mercy, provision, to give later. The person who lacks means should not be ashamed, but hope for Lord's bounty and give kind word.

**Link To Not Being Tight-Fisted Nor Openhanded**

Verse 29 will say do not be tight-fisted nor openhanded. Verse 28 says if you must turn down because lack means while hoping for bounty, give kind word. The link is that turning down due to lack is not tight-fisted, tight-fisted is withholding when you have means out of stinginess, while turning down due to lack while hoping for bounty is excused, with kind word as replacement. The balance is: give due when you have, avoid waste, avoid tight-fisted, avoid openhanded to poverty, and if lack means, give kind word while hoping.

**Linguistic Note On Tuʿriḍanna And Maysur**

Immā from a-m-m, if, conditional with emphasis. Tuʿriḍanna from ʿ-r-ḍ, to turn away, from aʿraḍa, to turn away, with nun emphasis, indeed turn away. ʿAnhum, from them. Ibtighā' from b-gh-y, seeking, from ibtaghā, to seek. Raḥmah from r-ḥ-m, mercy, bounty. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Tarjūhā from r-j-w, to hope. Qul from q-w-l, to say. Qawl from q-w-l, word. Maysūr from y-s-r, easy, gentle, kind, from yasura, to be easy. The verse's structure — if you turn away from them seeking mercy from your Lord you hope, then say to them gentle word — with condition of turning away due to seeking mercy and hoping, and command to say gentle word, shows mercy and kindness even when cannot give, while hoping for bounty to give later.

**How This Verse Teaches Kindness When Unable**

This verse teaches kindness when unable to give: if you must turn down relatives, poor, traveller because you lack means while hoping to receive Lord's bounty, then at least give kind word, gentle, easy. Al-Qurṭubī says believer who lacks means to give due should not be harsh, should not yell, should give kind word, gentle, promising, while hoping for Lord's bounty, mercy, provision, to give later. This is part of most upright guidance: honouring parents with noble word, giving due, and when cannot, kind word. The practical lesson is to be kind even when unable to give financially, to hope for Lord's bounty, not despair, and to maintain ties with kind speech.

**A Reflection On Hoping For Bounty**

Ibtighā'a raḥmatin min rabbika tarjūhā — seeking mercy from your Lord you hope — indicates that believer who lacks means seeks mercy, bounty, provision from Lord, hopes for it, expects it, to give to deserving. This hope is worship, trust, as Lord gives abundant or limited as He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing. The person who hopes for bounty while giving kind word is not ungrateful like Devil, but grateful, hopeful, trusting. The believer who reflects will give due when able, avoid waste, avoid tight-fisted, avoid openhanded, and when unable, give kind word while hoping for Lord's bounty, maintaining most upright character.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:29

> **Do not be so tight-fisted, for you will be blameworthy; nor so openhanded, for you will end up in poverty.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Be Tight-Fisted**

Wa-lā tajʿal yadaka maghlūlatan ilā ʿunuqika — and do not make your hand chained to your neck. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition. Tajʿal from j-ʿ-l, to make, set up, as verse 22 lā tajʿal maʿa Allāhi ilāhan, do not make with Allah other god, and verse 12 jaʿalnā night and day signs. Yadaka, your hand, from y-d, hand, limb. Maghlūlatan from gh-l-l, chained, bound, shackled, fettered, from ghalla, to chain, bind, fetter, from ghull, chain, fetter, shackle, collar. Ilā ʿunuqika, to your neck, to your own neck, from ʿ-n-q, neck, as verse 13 bound destiny to neck, ṭā'ir bound to neck, and verse 29 tight-fisted metaphor chained hand to neck. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not make hand chained to neck means do not be tight-fisted, stingy, miserly, withholding, as if hand bound, chained to neck cannot give, cannot extend to give due to relatives, poor, traveller, metaphor for stinginess, tight-fistedness, withholding. Al-Qurṭubī says this is metaphor for withholding, not giving due, being stingy, miserly, tight-fisted, which is blameworthy.

**Blameworthy If Tight-Fisted**

Fa-taqʿuda malūman — then you will sit blameworthy, end up blameworthy. Fa-, then, so, consequence. Taqʿuda from q-ʿ-d, to sit, end up, remain, become, to sit, as sitting indicates ending, remaining in state, as verse 22 fa-taqʿuda madhmūman makhdhūlan, you will sit condemned abandoned, and verse 29 similar. Malūman from l-w-m, blameworthy, blamed, from lāma, to blame, from malām, blame, and malūm, blamed, blameworthy, one blamed. Al-Ṭabarī says if you are tight-fisted, withholding when you should give due to relatives, poor, traveller, you will end up blameworthy, blamed by people and by Allah, because you withheld due, right, and people blame miser. Al-Rāzī says malūman indicates blame for stinginess, miserliness, as people blame tight-fisted who does not give due, and Allah blames, as withholding due is disobedience, ungrateful.

**Nor Openhanded To Poverty**

Wa-lā tabsuṭhā kulla al-basṭi fa-taqʿuda malūman maḥsūran — and do not extend it all extension, then you will sit blameworthy, in poverty, regretful, depleted. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition second extreme. Tabsuṭhā from b-s-ṭ, to extend, open, spread, from basaṭa, to extend, open, spread, as hand extended for giving, as yabsuṭu al-rizqa, extends provision. Kulla al-basṭi, all extension, complete openness, total extension, from k-l-l and b-s-ṭ, all, every, extension, openness, meaning fully openhanded, extravagant, spending all, wasteful. Fa-taqʿuda, then you will sit, end up, remain. Malūman maḥsūran, blameworthy, depleted, in poverty, regretful, from l-w-m and ḥ-s-r, blameworthy and maḥsūr, depleted, in poverty, regretful, unable, from ḥasara, to be depleted, regretful, to be in poverty, from ḥasrah, regret, poverty, from maḥsūr, depleted, regretful, in poverty, unable to give. Al-Ṭabarī says do not be so openhanded, extend hand fully, spending all wastefully, extravagantly, beyond due, beyond moderation, so that you end up in poverty, depleted, regretful, unable to give, blameworthy for waste. Al-Qurṭubī says this is prohibition of being openhanded to point of poverty, spending all, wasteful, tabdhīr, which makes one brother to devils, and leads to maḥsūr, depleted, regretful, in poverty.

**Why Moderation Commanded Between Extremes**

Why moderation commanded? Because both extremes blameworthy and harmful: tight-fisted leads to blameworthy, malūman, as withholding due, right, miserly, and openhanded to poverty leads to blameworthy and maḥsūr, depleted, regretful, in poverty, unable, wasteful, brother to devils. The most upright is moderation, middle path, wasat, between tight-fisted and openhanded, giving due to relatives, poor, traveller, not spending wastefully, not withholding, not spending all to poverty, but balanced. This is part of wisdom, most upright guidance, that Qur'an guides to, as verse 9 says guides to most upright. Previous chapter said wealth and children adornment, but Hereafter better, and provision in due measure, not all extended, so moderation in spending is wisdom, most upright, balanced.

**Connection To Giving Due And Wasteful Brothers To Devils And Kind Word**

Verse 26 said give to close relatives their due, poor, traveller, and do not spend wastefully, tabdhīr. Verse 27 wasteful brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful. Verse 28 if you must turn down due to lack while hoping for bounty, give kind word, gentle. Verse 29 do not be tight-fisted nor openhanded. The connection is that giving due is commanded, wasteful prohibited, tight-fisted prohibited, openhanded to poverty prohibited, kind word when lack, all together teach moderation, most upright spending, balanced. Tight-fisted is like not giving due, withholding right, openhanded is like wasteful, tabdhīr, brother to devils, extravagance. Moderation is gratitude, not ungratefulness, middle path.

**Link To Abundant Or Limited Provision And All-Aware All-Seeing**

Verse 30 will say Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing of servants. Verse 29 teaches moderation regardless of abundant or limited: if abundant provision, do not be openhanded to poverty, wasteful, extravagant; if limited provision, do not be tight-fisted withholding due, but give due moderately and give kind word if lack means while hoping for bounty. The provision abundant or limited is test, by will, with awareness and sight, what is best, as test of gratitude and patience, so moderation required in both states, abundant and limited, to be most upright.

**Linguistic Note On Maghlulah And Basṭ And Mahsur And Malum**

Tajʿal from j-ʿ-l, to make. Yad from y-d, hand. Maghlūlah from gh-l-l, chained, bound, from ghalla, to chain, bind, from ghull, chain. ʿUnuq from ʿ-n-q, neck. Taqʿuda from q-ʿ-d, to sit, end up, remain, from qaʿada. Malūm from l-w-m, blamed, blameworthy, from lāma, to blame. Tabsuṭ from b-s-ṭ, to extend, open, from basaṭa. Basṭ from b-s-ṭ, extension, openness. Kull from k-l-l, all, every. Maḥsūr from ḥ-s-r, depleted, in poverty, regretful, from ḥasara, to deplete, regret, from ḥasrah, regret. The verse's structure — do not make hand chained to neck then you sit blameworthy, and do not extend it all extension then you sit blameworthy depleted — with two prohibitions and two consequences, both sitting, teaches moderation, avoiding both extremes, tight-fisted and openhanded, which both lead to blameworthy, one also to poverty, depleted, regretful.

**How This Verse Teaches Moderation In Spending As Most Upright**

This verse teaches moderation in spending as most upright, wisdom: do not be tight-fisted, chained hand to neck, withholding due, for you will be blameworthy, nor openhanded, fully extended, wasteful, extravagant, spending all, for you will end up in poverty, depleted, regretful, blameworthy. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should be moderate in spending, give due to relatives, poor, traveller, not waste, not withhold, not spend all to poverty, but middle path, balanced, as most upright, wisdom. The practical lesson is to check spending: are you tight-fisted withholding due, right, when you have means? Then you will be blameworthy. Are you openhanded spending all wastefully, extravagantly, beyond due? Then you will end up in poverty, depleted, regretful, blameworthy, brother to devils. Be moderate, give due, avoid waste, give kind word when lack, hope for bounty.

**A Reflection On Sitting Blameworthy And Depleted**

Fa-taqʿuda malūman, you will sit blameworthy, and fa-taqʿuda malūman maḥsūran, you will sit blameworthy depleted — sitting indicates remaining in state, not fleeting, stuck, as verse 22 condemned abandoned also sitting, fa-taqʿuda madhmūman makhdhūlan. Tight-fisted sits blameworthy, openhanded sits blameworthy depleted, in poverty, regretful, unable. Moderation avoids both sitting states, leads to appreciated striving, mighty reward, not blame, not poverty, not regret. The believer who reflects will avoid tight-fisted and openhanded, be moderate, give due, avoid waste, give kind word when lack while hoping for Lord's bounty, as part of most upright guidance that Qur'an guides to, wisdom, leading to mighty reward and avoiding painful punishment.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:30

> **Surely your Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills. He is certainly All-Aware, AllSeeing of His servants.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Lord Gives Abundant Or Limited Provisions**

Inna rabbaka yabsuṭu al-rizqa li-man yashā'u wa-yaqdiru — indeed your Lord extends provision for whom He wills and restricts, measures. Inna, indeed, surely. Rabbaka, your Lord, from r-b-b. Yabsuṭu from b-s-ṭ, to extend, expand, give abundant, from basaṭa, to extend, as verse 29 tabsuṭhā, extend hand. Al-rizqa from r-z-q, provision, sustenance, from razaqa, to provide. Li-man yashā'u, for whom He wills, to whoever He wills, from sh-y-ʾ, to will. Wa-yaqdiru from q-d-r, to restrict, measure, give limited, from qadara, to measure, decree, restrict, from same root as qadar, measure, decree, and qadir, able. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Lord extends provision, gives abundant, plentiful, for whom He wills, and restricts, gives limited, measured, for whom He wills, according to wisdom, knowledge, not random. Al-Qurṭubī says yabsuṭu al-rizqa means gives abundant provision, wa-yaqdiru means restricts, gives limited, measured, as test, as previous chapter said if Allah were to extend provision for servants they would transgress but He sends down in due measure what He wills.

**All-Aware All-Seeing Of Servants**

Innahu kāna bi-ʿibādihi khabīran baṣīran — indeed He is with His servants All-Aware, All-Seeing. Innahu, indeed He. Kāna, is, was. Bi-ʿibādihi, with His servants, about His servants, from ʿ-b-d, servants. Khabīran from kh-b-r, All-Aware, Aware, informed, from same root as khabar, news, and khabīr, Aware, as verse 17 sufficient Lord as All-Aware All-Seeing of sins. Baṣīran from b-ṣ-r, All-Seeing, Seeing, from baṣar, sight, as verse 12 mubṣiratan bright and verse 17 Baṣīran. Al-Ṭabarī says He is All-Aware, All-Seeing of His servants, knows their states, needs, what is best for them, whether abundant or limited provision is better, so He gives accordingly with knowledge and sight. Al-Rāzī says khabīran baṣīran indicates comprehensive knowledge of servants, inner and outer, so provision abundant or limited based on complete knowledge, wisdom, not arbitrary, as test.

**Why This Verse Follows Moderation Command**

Verses 26-29 commanded giving due to relatives, poor, traveller, not spending wastefully, wasteful brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful, if must turn down due to lack while hoping for bounty give kind word, do not be tight-fisted nor openhanded. Verse 30 says Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing of servants. The connection is that moderation command is based on reality that provision abundant or limited by will, with knowledge, so whether you have abundant or limited, be moderate, give due, avoid waste, avoid tight-fisted, avoid openhanded, give kind word when lack, hope for bounty. The verse explains why some have abundant and some limited: by will, with awareness and sight, as test, not as sign of pleasure or displeasure alone.

**Link To Bounty Never Withheld And Favoured Some Over Others**

Verse 20 said bounty never withheld, We provide both former and latter from bounty. Verse 21 said see how We favoured some over others in this life but Hereafter far greater in rank and favour. Verse 30 says Lord extends provision for whom He wills and restricts, All-Aware All-Seeing. The link is that bounty never withheld in general, but distribution abundant or limited by will, favouring some over others in worldly provision, but Hereafter greater. The provision abundant or limited is part of favouring some over others, and is based on knowledge and sight of servants, what is best for them, as test.

**Connection To Previous Chapter About Provision In Due Measure**

Previous chapter said if Allah were to extend provision for His servants they would transgress in earth but He sends down in due measure what He wills, He is All-Aware All-Seeing of His servants. This verse says similar: Lord extends provision for whom He wills and restricts, He is All-Aware All-Seeing of servants. The theme is consistent: provision measured by will, with awareness and sight, to prevent transgression, as test, not random. The sūrah's moral code about spending is based on this: provision measured, so be moderate.

**Linguistic Note On Yabsutu And Yaqdiru And Khabir Basir**

Inna from a-n-n, indeed. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Yabsuṭu from b-s-ṭ, to extend, expand, from basaṭa. Rizq from r-z-q, provision. Man from m-n, whom. Yashā'u from sh-y-ʾ, He wills. Yaqdiru from q-d-r, to restrict, measure, from qadara. Innahu from a-n-n, indeed He. Kāna from k-w-n, to be. ʿIbād from ʿ-b-d, servants. Khabīr from kh-b-r, All-Aware. Baṣīr from b-ṣ-r, All-Seeing. The verse's structure — indeed your Lord extends provision for whom He wills and restricts, indeed He is with His servants All-Aware All-Seeing — with statement of extending and restricting by will and statement of awareness and sight, shows that provision distribution based on knowledge and sight, wisdom, not arbitrary.

**How This Verse Teaches Trust And Moderation**

This verse teaches trust and moderation: Lord gives abundant or limited provisions to whoever He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing of servants, so trust His distribution, be moderate in spending, give due, avoid waste, avoid tight-fisted, avoid openhanded, give kind word when lack, hope for bounty. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should trust that provision abundant or limited is by will with awareness and sight, what is best, so should not envy, should not be arrogant when abundant, should not despair when limited, but be moderate, grateful, patient. The practical lesson is to see provision as test, from All-Aware All-Seeing, and to use it moderately, most upright, to achieve greater ranks in Hereafter.

**A Reflection On All-Aware All-Seeing**

Khabīran baṣīran — All-Aware, All-Seeing — as in verse 17 sufficient Lord as All-Aware All-Seeing of sins, and here All-Aware All-Seeing of servants regarding provision. This indicates comprehensive knowledge: Aware of inner, Seeing outer, knows what is best for each servant, whether abundant or limited provision better to prevent transgression, to test gratitude and patience. The believer who knows Lord is All-Aware All-Seeing of servants will trust provision distribution, be grateful when abundant, patient when limited, moderate in spending, give due to relatives, poor, traveller, avoid wasteful brotherhood to devils, avoid tight-fisted blameworthy and openhanded depleted, give kind word when lack while hoping for bounty, as part of most upright guidance that leads to appreciated striving and mighty reward.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:31

> **Do not kill your children for fear of poverty. We provide for them and for you. Surely killing them is a heinous sin.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Kill Children For Fear Of Poverty**

Wa-lā taqtulū awlādakum khashyata imlāqin — and do not kill your children fearing poverty, destitution. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition, strong. Taqtulū from q-t-l, to kill, slay, from qatala, to kill. Awlādakum from w-l-d, children, offspring, plural of walad, child, from walada, to beget, to bear. Khashyata from kh-sh-y, fear, apprehension, dread, from khashiya, to fear, dread. Imlāqin from m-l-q, poverty, destitution, need, from amlaqa, to be poor, destitute, from imlāq, poverty, destitution. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not kill your children for fear of poverty, as Arabs in jāhiliyyah killed children, especially daughters, for fear of poverty, destitution, not being able to provide, as they buried daughters alive, killed sons fearing they would eat with them. The prohibition includes killing children for fear of poverty, whether already born or unborn, male or female, via infanticide, abortion, neglect, as fear of poverty leads to killing, which is heinous sin, great sin. Al-Qurṭubī says this includes abortion for fear of poverty, infanticide, as Arabs killed children fearing poverty, and Qur'an prohibits, as provision from Allah, not from parents alone.

**We Provide For Them And For You**

Naḥnu narzuquhum wa-iyyākum — We provide for them and for you. Naḥnu, We, Allah, majestic We. Narzuquhum from r-z-q, to provide, provision, sustenance, to give rizq, from razaqa, to provide, as verse 30 yabsuṭu al-rizqa, extends provision, gives abundant or limited. Hum, them, children, offspring. Wa-iyyākum, and you, parents, you, from iyyā, you, emphatic you. Al-Ṭabarī says We provide for them and for you, meaning Allah provides for children and for parents, provision not from parents alone but from Allah who gives abundant or limited as He wills, All-Aware All-Seeing, bounty never withheld, some favoured over others, but provision from Allah. Therefore fear of poverty is unfounded, as provision from Allah, not from parents, and Allah provides for children and parents, both, independent. Al-Rāzī says naḥnu narzuquhum wa-iyyākum indicates that provision for children is from Allah, not parents, so do not kill them fearing poverty, as Allah provides for them and for you, both, and fearing poverty indicates lack of trust in Allah's provision, lack of tawakkul, distrust in bounty never withheld, that is from bounty of Lord.

**Surely Killing Them Is Heinous Sin**

Inna qatlahum kāna khiṭ'an kabīran — indeed killing them is great sin, heinous sin, grave error, tremendous sin. Inna, indeed, surely, emphasis. Qatlahum from q-t-l, killing them, from qatala, killing. Kāna, is, was, indicates it is. Khiṭ'an from kh-ṭ-ʾ, sin, error, grave sin, great error, from khaṭi'a, to sin, err, from khiṭ', sin, error, grave, from same root as khaṭa', sin, error. Kabīran from k-b-r, great, big, heinous, grave, tremendous, from kabura, to be great, as akbar greater, far greater. Al-Ṭabarī says killing children is heinous sin, great sin, grave error, not small, minor, but great, heinous, because it is killing innocent life made sacred by Allah except with legal right, and because it is due to fear of poverty, distrust in Allah's provision, and because child has right to life, innocent. Al-Qurṭubī says khiṭ'an kabīran means great sin, heinous, grave, tremendous, as killing children is among greatest sins, major sins, as hadith says greatest sins include shirk, killing child for fear he will eat with you, disobeying parents, etc., indicating gravity.

**Why Fear Of Poverty Leads To Killing And Distrust**

Why fear of poverty leads to killing? Because provision abundant or limited by will, and some favoured over others in this life, and bounty never withheld, but human ever hasty, ʿajūlan, ever hasty, prays for evil as for good, desires fleeting, fears poverty, so kills children to avoid poverty, to avoid burden, to avoid sharing provision. The prohibition addresses root cause: fear of poverty, imlāq, which is due to lack of trust in Allah who provides for them and for you, who gives abundant or limited with awareness, All-Aware All-Seeing, who provides both former and latter from bounty, bounty never withheld. The verse corrects by saying We provide for them and for you, so do not fear poverty, trust provision, do not kill, be grateful, not ungrateful like Devil ever ungrateful. Fear of poverty is from Satan who promises poverty, as Qur'an says Satan promises you poverty and commands shameful deeds.

**Connection To Moderation And Provision And Giving Due**

Verses 26-30 taught giving due to relatives, poor, traveller, not spending wastefully, wasteful brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful, if must turn down due to lack while hoping for bounty give kind word, do not be tight-fisted nor openhanded, Lord gives abundant or limited provisions, All-Aware All-Seeing. Verse 31 says do not kill children for fear of poverty, We provide for them and for you, killing is heinous sin. The connection is that fear of poverty that leads to withholding due, tight-fisted, miserly, or to killing children, is addressed by reminding that provision from Allah, abundant or limited by will, with awareness and sight, and that He provides for children and parents, so do not kill for fear of poverty, do not be tight-fisted, give due, be moderate, trust bounty never withheld. The verse links provision to protection of children.

**Link To Protecting Life And Orphan Wealth And Measuring**

Next verses will say do not kill human life made sacred except with legal right, if killed unjustly heir given authority but do not exceed limits for already supported, and do not come near wealth of orphan unless intending to enhance it until they attain maturity, honour pledges accountable, give full when measure and weigh with even balance fairest best in end, do not follow without sure knowledge hearing sight intellect accountable, do not walk arrogantly you cannot crack earth nor reach mountains height, violation detestable to Lord, this is part of wisdom revealed, do not set up other god or cast into Hell blameworthy rejected. Verse 31 about not killing children is part of series of prohibitions protecting life and wealth: children, adultery, life sacred, orphan wealth, measuring, etc., all most upright, wisdom, detestable if violated.

**Linguistic Note On Imlaq And Khit' And Rizq**

Taqtulū from q-t-l, to kill. Awlād from w-l-d, children. Khashyah from kh-sh-y, fear. Imlāq from m-l-q, poverty, destitution, from amlaqa, to be poor. Narzuqu from r-z-q, to provide, from razaqa. Qatlahum from q-t-l, killing them. Khiṭ' from kh-ṭ-ʾ, sin, error, from khaṭi'a, to sin. Kabīr from k-b-r, great, heinous, grave. The verse's structure — do not kill children fearing poverty, We provide for them and for you, indeed killing them is great sin — with prohibition, reason of provision for them and you, and statement of gravity great sin, shows that killing children for fear of poverty is prohibited, provision from Allah for both, and sin great, heinous, grave, not minor, so avoid.

**How This Verse Teaches Trust And Protection**

This verse teaches trust in Allah's provision and protection of children: do not kill children for fear of poverty, because We provide for them and for you, provision from Allah who gives abundant or limited with awareness, All-Aware All-Seeing, bounty never withheld, some favoured over others but Hereafter far greater, so trust, do not fear poverty to point of killing, withholding, being tight-fisted. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should trust Allah provides for children and parents, should not kill children for fear of poverty, whether via infanticide, abortion, neglect, etc., and should know killing them is heinous sin, great sin, grave error, major sin. The practical lesson is to provide for children, trust provision, give due to relatives, poor, traveller, be moderate, not tight-fisted nor openhanded, and protect children's life, as most upright guidance, wisdom, part of appreciated striving, mighty reward, avoiding painful punishment.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:32

> **Do not go near adultery. It is truly a shameful deed and an evil way.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Go Near Adultery**

Wa-lā taqrabū al-zinā — and do not approach, go near adultery, fornication, unlawful sexual intercourse. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition, strong. Taqrabū from q-r-b, to approach, go near, come close, be near, from qaruba, to be near, close, as qurbā closeness, kinship, and taqrabū approaching. Al-zinā from z-n-y, adultery, fornication, unlawful sexual intercourse, from zanā, to commit adultery, fornication, from zinā, adultery. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not go near adultery, not only do not commit adultery, but do not approach, go near, come close to it, meaning avoid all means, steps, causes, that lead to adultery, as approaching, going near leads to committing, falling into. The prohibition of approaching is stronger, more comprehensive than prohibition of committing, as it blocks means, prevents, as wisdom, most upright, to protect lineage, honour, family, children. Al-Qurṭubī says this includes avoiding looking with desire, touching, seclusion with non-mahram, flirting, etc., that lead to zinā, as all are approaching, going near, steps to adultery, and should be avoided.

**Shameful Deed And Evil Way**

Innahu kāna fāḥishatan wa-sā'a sabīlan — indeed it is shameful deed and evil way, bad path. Innahu, indeed it, adultery, zinā. Kāna, is, was, indicates it is. Fāḥishatan from f-ḥ-sh, shameful, obscene, indecent, lewd, from faḥusha, to be shameful, obscene, indecent, from fāḥishah, shameful deed, obscenity, indecency, lewdness. Wa-sā'a from s-w-ʾ, evil, bad, worse, from sā'a, to be evil, bad, from sū', evil, bad. Sabīlan from s-b-l, way, path, road, method, from sabīl, way, path, as ibn al-sabīl son of way, traveller. Al-Ṭabarī says adultery is shameful deed, fāḥishah, obscene, indecent, shameful, lewd, and evil way, bad path, evil way to follow, evil method, leading to evil consequences, as it leads to mixing lineage, confusion of parentage, harm to children, family, society, etc., and leads to Hell, not most upright. Al-Rāzī says fāḥishatan indicates indecency, shamefulness, obscenity in itself, and sā'a sabīlan indicates evil way, path, method, leading to further evil, not most upright, not wisdom, but evil.

**Why Approaching Prohibited Not Only Committing**

Why approaching prohibited, not only committing? Because human is ever hasty, ʿajūlan, ever hasty, prays for evil as for good, desires fleeting world, so approaching adultery, going near, leads to committing, as steps lead, as hasty desire leads. The most upright guidance blocks means, not only act, to protect, as wisdom, part of wisdom revealed, as verse 39 says this is part of wisdom revealed. The sūrah's moral code includes protecting lineage, honour, family, children, as previous verse protected children from killing for fear of poverty, provision for them and you, killing heinous sin, now protects from adultery which harms children, lineage, family, honour, and leads to killing children for fear of poverty or shame, as unwanted children killed, as Arabs killed children. So prohibition of adultery prevents killing children, protects lineage.

**Connection To Killing Children And Killing Life Sacred And Orphan Wealth**

Verse 31 said do not kill children for fear of poverty, We provide for them and for you, killing heinous sin, great sin. Verse 32 says do not go near adultery, shameful deed evil way. Verse 33 says do not kill human life made sacred except with legal right, if killed unjustly heir given authority but do not exceed limits for already supported. Verse 34 says do not approach orphan wealth except best until maturity, honour pledges accountable. The connection is that killing children, adultery, killing sacred life, consuming orphan wealth are all prohibitions protecting life and lineage and wealth and honour, all most upright, wisdom, most upright guidance. Adultery often leads to killing children for fear of poverty or shame, as unwanted children killed, abortion, infanticide, so prohibition of adultery prevents killing children, protects children. The series shows comprehensive protection: children, chastity, life sacred, orphan wealth, pledges, measuring, knowledge, humility, all wisdom.

**Link To Moral Code And Most Upright And Wisdom**

The moral code in this sūrah from verse 22 to 39 is detailed: monotheism, parents, relatives, poor, traveller, moderation, provision, children, adultery, life sacred, orphan wealth, pledges, measuring, knowledge, arrogance, all wisdom, most upright, that Qur'an guides to, as verse 9 says Qur'an guides to what is most upright. Adultery is included as shameful deed and evil way, not most upright, opposite of most upright guidance that Qur'an guides to, opposite of wisdom, detestable to Lord as verse 38 says violation detestable. The verse teaches that most upright includes chastity, avoiding approaching adultery, protecting lineage, honour, family, children.

**Linguistic Note On Taqrabu And Fahishah And Sabila**

Taqrabū from q-r-b, to approach, go near, come close, from qaruba, to be near, close. Zinā from z-n-y, adultery, fornication, from zanā, to commit adultery. Fāḥishah from f-ḥ-sh, shameful, obscene, indecent, from faḥusha, to be shameful, obscene. Sā'a from s-w-ʾ, evil, bad, from sā'a, to be evil. Sabīl from s-b-l, way, path, road, method. The verse's structure — do not approach adultery, indeed it is shameful deed and evil way — with prohibition of approaching, not only committing, and two descriptions, shameful deed and evil way, shows gravity: not only act shameful, obscene, but way evil, path bad, method evil, leading to further evil, not most upright, not wisdom, detestable.

**How This Verse Teaches Chastity And Avoiding Means To Adultery**

This verse teaches chastity and avoiding means to adultery: do not go near adultery, not only do not commit, but avoid approaching, means, steps, causes, that lead to it, because it is shameful deed, fāḥishah, obscene, indecent, and evil way, bad path, evil method. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should avoid adultery and avoid approaching it, such as looking with desire, touching, seclusion with non-mahram, flirting, chatting that leads, etc., that lead to zinā, as approaching leads to committing, as steps. The practical lesson is to guard chastity, lower gaze, avoid seclusion, avoid means, follow most upright guidance that protects lineage, honour, family, children, and avoids shameful deed and evil way that leads to painful punishment and Hell confinement, as Qur'an guides to most upright.

**A Reflection On Shameful Deed And Evil Way And Most Upright**

Fāḥishatan wa-sā'a sabīlan — shameful deed and evil way — two descriptions: shameful, obscene, indecent in itself, fāḥishah, and evil way, bad path, method, sā'a sabīlan, leading to further evil, not most upright, not wisdom, but evil, detestable to Lord. Adultery is shameful deed, fāḥishah, indecency, obscenity, and evil way, path that leads to mixing lineage, confusion, harming children, family, society, leading to killing children, to Hell, not most upright. The most upright guidance that Qur'an guides to, wisdom revealed, is chastity, avoiding approaching adultery, protecting children, life, honour, family, as wisdom, most upright. The believer who reflects will avoid approaching adultery, will see it as shameful deed and evil way, will follow most upright guidance of chastity, protecting lineage, family, children, honour, and will seek mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour in Hereafter, and avoid painful punishment, Hell, detestable violation.

**Additional Note On Protection Of Lineage**

Protection of lineage, nasab, is one of five essentials in Islamic law: religion, life, lineage, intellect, wealth. Adultery violates lineage, so prohibition of approaching adultery protects lineage, as most upright, wisdom. The sūrah's moral code protects all five: religion via monotheism, life via not killing children and sacred life, lineage via not approaching adultery, intellect via not following without knowledge, wealth via orphan wealth, measuring, giving due, moderation, etc., comprehensive most upright guidance, wisdom.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:33

> **Do not take a ˹human˺ life—made sacred by Allah—except with ˹legal˺ right. If anyone is killed unjustly, We have given their heirs the authority, but do not let them exceed limits in retaliation, for they are already supported ˹by law˺.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Take Human Life Made Sacred Except With Legal Right**

Wa-lā taqtulū al-nafsa allatī ḥarrama Allāhu illā bi-l-ḥaqqi — and do not kill soul which Allah made sacred except with right, legal right. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition. Taqtulū from q-t-l, to kill, as verse 31 taqtulū awlādakum, kill children. Al-nafsa from n-f-s, soul, person, life, human life, from nafs. Allatī ḥarrama Allāhu, which Allah made sacred, forbade killing, from ḥ-r-m, sacred, forbidden, from ḥarrama, to make sacred, forbid. Illā bi-l-ḥaqqi, except with right, legal right, truth, justice, from ḥ-q-q, right, truth, legal right, as capital punishment for murder, etc., with justice. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not kill human life made sacred by Allah except with legal right, meaning do not kill innocent person whose life Allah made sacred, forbidden to kill, except with legal right, such as qisas, retaliation for murder, apostasy, adultery of married, etc., with justice, legal. Al-Qurṭubī says nafs made sacred includes Muslim, dhimmī, muʿāhad, etc., whose life sacred, not to be killed except with legal right, justice.

**If Anyone Killed Unjustly, Heirs Given Authority**

Wa-man qutila maẓlūman fa-qad jaʿalnā li-waliyyihi sulṭānan — and whoever is killed unjustly, We gave his heir authority. Wa-man, and whoever, who. Qutila from q-t-l, killed, passive, was killed. Maẓlūman from ẓ-l-m, unjustly, wronged, oppressed, from ẓalama, to wrong, oppress, from ẓulm, injustice, wrong. Fa-qad jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make, give, as verse 12 jaʿalnā signs and verse 18 jaʿalnā Hell. Li-waliyyihi, for his heir, guardian, protector, from w-l-y, to be near, guardian, heir, protector, from walī, guardian, heir, close. Sulṭānan from s-l-ṭ, authority, power, proof, from sulṭān, authority, power. Al-Ṭabarī says if anyone killed unjustly, wrongfully, not with legal right, We gave his heir, guardian, family, authority, power, to demand justice, qisas or compensation, via law. Al-Rāzī says sulṭān here means authority to demand retaliation or pardon, via ruler, law, not personal vengeance without law.

**Do Not Exceed Limits In Retaliation, Already Supported By Law**

Fa-lā yusrif fī al-qatli innahu kāna manṣūran — so do not exceed in killing, indeed he is supported. Fa-lā yusrif from s-r-f, to exceed, transgress, waste, from asrafa, to exceed, transgress, waste, from same root as isrāf, excess, waste. Fī al-qatli, in killing, in retaliation, from q-t-l. Innahu kāna manṣūran, indeed he is supported, helped, from n-ṣ-r, to support, help, from naṣara, to help, support, and manṣūr, supported, helped. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not exceed limits in retaliation, killing, meaning heir should not exceed legal limits, not kill more than killer, not mutilate, not kill other than killer, etc., for he is already supported by law, by Allah, by ruler, so no need to exceed. Al-Qurṭubī says lā yusrif fī al-qatli means do not transgress in killing, retaliation, by killing more than one for one, or killing innocent, etc., because heir already supported by law, by Allah, so justice will be done without excess.

**Why Prohibition Of Killing Sacred Life**

Why prohibition? Because life made sacred by Allah, not to be taken except with legal right, justice, as part of most upright guidance protecting life. Previous verses protected children from killing for fear of poverty, protected chastity from approaching adultery, now protects all sacred life from killing except legal right. The series shows comprehensive protection: children, lineage, life, orphan wealth, etc., all wisdom, most upright. Killing sacred life unjustly is heinous sin, like killing children, as both are killing innocent.

**Connection To Killing Children And Provision**

Verse 31 said do not kill children for fear of poverty, We provide for them and you, killing heinous sin. Verse 33 says do not kill sacred life except with legal right, if killed unjustly heir given authority but do not exceed limits for already supported. The connection is that killing children is example of killing sacred life unjustly, heinous sin, and general prohibition of killing sacred life includes children, all innocent. The provision reminder that We provide for them and you also applies: do not kill for fear of poverty or other reason, except legal right, justice.

**Link To Authority And Support By Law**

The phrase We gave his heir authority but do not exceed limits for already supported by law indicates that Islam gives authority to heir to demand justice but limits excess, as supported by law, by Allah, by ruler, so no need for personal vengeance beyond law. This is most upright: justice, not excess, support by law, not anarchy. The sūrah's theme of justice appears: no bearer bears burden of another, record laid open, sufficient self as reckoner, now justice in retaliation, not exceeding.

**Linguistic Note On Nafs And Mazluman And Sultan And Israf**

Taqtulū from q-t-l, to kill. Nafs from n-f-s, soul, life, person. Ḥarrama from ḥ-r-m, to make sacred, forbid, from ḥaruma. Ḥaqq from ḥ-q-q, right, truth, legal right. Qutila from q-t-l, killed. Maẓlūman from ẓ-l-m, unjustly, wronged, from ẓalama, to wrong. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make, give. Walī from w-l-y, heir, guardian, from waliya. Sulṭān from s-l-ṭ, authority, power. Yusrif from s-r-f, to exceed, transgress, waste, from asrafa. Qatl from q-t-l, killing. Manṣūr from n-ṣ-r, supported, helped, from naṣara. The verse's structure — do not kill sacred soul except with right, whoever killed unjustly We gave his heir authority, so do not exceed in killing, indeed he is supported — with prohibition, exception, authority given, limit not to exceed, reason supported, shows balanced justice: protection of life, legal right exception, authority for heir, limit not to exceed, support by law.

**How This Verse Teaches Protection Of Life And Justice**

This verse teaches protection of life made sacred by Allah, prohibition of killing except with legal right, justice, and teaches justice in retaliation: heir given authority but not to exceed limits, for already supported by law. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should not kill sacred life unjustly, should know life sacred, not to be taken except with legal right, justice, via law, and if someone killed unjustly, heir has authority to demand qisas or pardon, but should not exceed limits, transgress, as supported by law, by Allah, so justice will be done without excess, vengeance. The practical lesson is to protect life, avoid killing unjustly, seek justice via law, not personal excess, as most upright guidance, wisdom.

**A Reflection On Sacred Life And Support**

Al-nafs allatī ḥarrama Allāhu — soul which Allah made sacred — indicates sanctity of human life, made sacred by Allah, not to be violated except with right, justice, legal. This sanctity is foundation of most upright, wisdom. Manṣūran — supported — indicates that heir of unjustly killed is supported by law, by Allah, by ruler, so no need to exceed, transgress, take law into own hands beyond limits. The believer who reflects will protect sacred life, not kill except with legal right, will seek justice via authority given, not exceed, trusting support by law, as part of most upright guidance that leads to mighty reward and avoids painful punishment and Hell.
