## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:34

> **Do not come near the wealth of the orphan—unless intending to enhance it—until they attain maturity. And honour ˹your˺ pledges, for you will surely be accountable for them.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Come Near Wealth Of Orphan Unless Enhancing**

Wa-lā taqrabū māla al-yatīmi illā bi-allatī hiya aḥsanu ḥattā yablugha ashuddahu — and do not approach wealth of orphan except with what is best until he reaches maturity. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition. Taqrabū from q-r-b, to approach, go near, as verse 32 lā taqrabū al-zinā, do not approach adultery. Māla from m-w-l, wealth, property, from māl. Al-yatīmi from y-t-m, orphan, one whose father died while young, from yatama, to be orphan. Illā bi-allatī hiya aḥsanu, except with what is best, better, most excellent, from ḥ-s-n, good, best, as aḥsantum do good, iḥsānan excellence, aḥsanu best. Ḥattā, until. Yablugha from b-l-gh, to reach, attain, as verse 23 yablughanna old age. Ashuddahu from sh-d-d, maturity, strength, full strength, from shadda, to be strong, from ashudd, strength, maturity, full strength, adulthood. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not come near wealth of orphan, do not touch, use, except with what is best, intending to enhance it, improve it, invest, protect, until he attains maturity, full strength, adulthood, able to manage wealth. Al-Qurṭubī says approaching wealth of orphan prohibited except with best intention, enhancing, protecting, not consuming, until maturity, when orphan can manage, then return wealth.

**Honour Pledges Accountable**

Wa-awfū bi-l-ʿahdi inna al-ʿahda kāna mas'ūlan — and fulfill pledge, covenant, indeed pledge is questioned, accountable. Wa-awfū from w-f-y, to fulfill, complete, keep, from wafā, to fulfill, keep promise. Bi-l-ʿahdi from ʿ-h-d, pledge, covenant, promise, commitment, from ʿahida, to pledge. Inna al-ʿahda, indeed pledge, covenant. Kāna mas'ūlan from s-ʾ-l, questioned, asked about, accountable, responsible, from sa'ala, to ask, question, and mas'ūl, questioned, accountable. Al-Ṭabarī says honour pledges, fulfill covenants, promises, commitments, for you will be accountable for them, questioned about them on Day, record laid open, sufficient self as reckoner. Al-Rāzī says ʿahd includes covenants with Allah, with people, promises, oaths, all must be fulfilled, and you will be accountable, questioned, as part of justice, no bearer bears burden of another but each accountable for own pledges.

**Why Orphan Wealth Protected**

Why orphan wealth protected? Because orphan is weak, fatherless, vulnerable, as parents honoured, children protected from killing, now orphan wealth protected, as part of most upright guidance protecting weak, vulnerable. The sūrah's moral code protects weak: parents old, children, orphan, poor, traveller, etc. Orphan wealth is trust, not to be approached except to enhance, improve, protect, until maturity, when orphan can manage. This is justice, not consuming orphan wealth unjustly, which is heinous sin, as Qur'an says those who consume orphan wealth unjustly consume fire in bellies.

**Connection To Killing Children And Life Sacred And Measuring**

Verses 31-33 protected life: children, chastity, sacred life. Verse 34 protects wealth of vulnerable orphan and pledges, accountability. Verse 35 will say give full when measure and weigh with even balance, fairest best in end. The connection is protection of wealth: orphan wealth not to be approached except enhancing, and measuring and weighing fairly, not cheating, both protecting wealth, property, justice in financial dealings, most upright. The series shows comprehensive protection: life, lineage, wealth, pledges, measure, knowledge, humility.

**Link To Accountability And Record Laid Open**

Honour pledges for you will be accountable for them, mas'ūlan, questioned, accountable, links to record laid open, read your book, sufficient yourself as reckoner, no bearer bears burden, etc. Pledges will be in record laid open, you will be questioned, accountable. The sūrah's theme of accountability appears: destiny bound to neck, record laid open, read book, sufficient self as reckoner, no bearing other's burden, messenger sent, etc., now pledges accountable.

**Linguistic Note On Yatim And Ashudd And Ahd And Mas'ul**

Taqrabū from q-r-b, to approach. Māl from m-w-l, wealth. Yatīm from y-t-m, orphan, from yatama. Aḥsan from ḥ-s-n, best, better, from ḥasuna, to be good. Yablugha from b-l-gh, to reach. Ashudd from sh-d-d, maturity, strength, from shadda, to be strong. Awfū from w-f-y, to fulfill, from wafā. ʿAhd from ʿ-h-d, pledge, covenant. Mas'ūl from s-ʾ-l, questioned, accountable, from sa'ala. The verse's structure — do not approach wealth of orphan except with best until he reaches maturity, and fulfill pledge, indeed pledge is accountable — with prohibition of approaching orphan wealth except best until maturity and command to fulfill pledge with reason accountable, shows protection of vulnerable wealth and importance of fulfilling commitments, both accountable.

**How This Verse Teaches Protection Of Orphan Wealth And Fulfilling Pledges**

This verse teaches protection of orphan wealth and fulfilling pledges: do not come near wealth of orphan unless intending to enhance it, improve, protect, invest, until they attain maturity, full strength, adulthood, able to manage, then return, and honour pledges, fulfill covenants, promises, for you will be accountable for them, questioned on Day, record laid open. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should not consume orphan wealth unjustly, should protect, enhance, until maturity, and should fulfill pledges, covenants, promises, with Allah and people, as accountable, questioned. The practical lesson is to protect orphan wealth as trust, enhance, not consume, and to fulfill pledges, as most upright guidance, wisdom, part of appreciated striving, mighty reward.

**A Reflection On Best And Maturity And Accountability**

Bi-allatī hiya aḥsanu — with what is best, better — indicates that approaching orphan wealth allowed only if intention best, enhancing, improving, protecting, not consuming, not harming. Ḥattā yablugha ashuddahu — until he attains maturity, strength — indicates limit: until orphan mature, strong, able to manage wealth, then return. Awfū bi-l-ʿahdi — fulfill pledge — indicates importance of keeping commitments. Inna al-ʿahda kāna mas'ūlan — pledge is accountable, questioned — indicates you will be questioned about pledges, accountable, record laid open will show. The believer who reflects will protect orphan wealth with best intention until maturity, fulfill pledges, knowing accountable, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to, leading to appreciated striving, mighty reward, and avoiding painful punishment.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:35

> **Give in full when you measure, and weigh with an even balance. That is fairest and best in the end.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Give Full When Measure And Weigh With Even Balance**

Wa-awfū al-kayla idhā kiltum wa-zinū bi-l-qisṭāsi al-mustaqīmi — and fulfill measure when you measure and weigh with straight, even balance. Wa-awfū from w-f-y, to fulfill, complete, give full, as previous verse awfū bi-l-ʿahdi, fulfill pledge. Al-kayla from k-y-l, measure, volume measure, from kāla, to measure. Idhā kiltum from k-y-l, when you measure, you measure for others. Wa-zinū from w-z-n, to weigh, from wazana, to weigh, as wizr burden from w-z-r, different root, but wazn weight. Bi-l-qisṭāsi from q-s-ṭ, balance, scale, even, just, from qisṭās, balance, scale, from q-s-ṭ, justice, evenness, from qist, justice, fairness. Al-mustaqīmi from q-w-m, straight, even, upright, from istaqāma, to be straight, upright, from same root as mustaqīm, straight, as ṣirāṭ mustaqīm, straight path, and qiyāmah standing. Al-Ṭabarī explains that give in full when you measure, fulfill measure, do not cheat, give full volume, and weigh with even, straight, just balance, scale, not crooked, cheating. Al-Qurṭubī says this is command to give full measure and weigh with even balance, just, fair, not cheating, as part of justice in transactions, protecting wealth, as previous verse protected orphan wealth.

**Fairest And Best In End**

Dhālika khayrun wa-aḥsanu ta'wīlan — that is best, fairest, and best in end, interpretation, result. Dhālika, that, that practice, giving full measure and even balance. Khayrun from kh-y-r, good, better, best, fairest, from khayr, good. Wa-aḥsanu from ḥ-s-n, better, best, more excellent, as aḥsanu best, as previous verse aḥsanu best for orphan wealth. Ta'wīlan from a-w-l, interpretation, result, end, outcome, from awwala, to interpret, result, from ta'wīl, interpretation, result, end. Al-Ṭabarī says that is fairest, better, and best in end, result, outcome, meaning giving full measure and even balance is fairest, most just, and best in end, result, outcome, in world and Hereafter, as it leads to trust, blessing, and avoids punishment. Al-Rāzī says khayrun indicates good, fairest in world, and aḥsanu ta'wīlan indicates best in end, Hereafter, result, as justice leads to good outcome.

**Why Measuring And Weighing Justice Commanded**

Why measuring and weighing justice? Because wealth protection requires justice in transactions, as orphan wealth protected, pledges honoured, now measuring and weighing fairly, not cheating, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, protecting wealth, property, rights. Cheating in measure and weight is common injustice, especially in markets, so Qur'an commands giving full, even balance, as most upright, fairest, best in end. The sūrah's theme of bounty never withheld, provision abundant or limited, favouring some over others, but justice in measuring ensures fair distribution, not cheating.

**Connection To Orphan Wealth And Pledges And Knowledge**

Verses 34-36: do not approach orphan wealth except best until maturity, honour pledges accountable, give full when measure and weigh with even balance fairest best in end, do not follow what you have no sure knowledge of, hearing, sight, intellect accountable. The connection is protection of wealth and commitments and knowledge: orphan wealth protected, pledges honoured, measuring fair, knowledge not followed without sure knowledge, all accountable, all part of most upright, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to. The series shows comprehensive justice: wealth, pledges, measure, knowledge, humility.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Measuring? And Commercial Justice**

The moral code in this sūrah includes commercial justice: give full measure, even balance, as part of wisdom. Previous communities like Shuʿayb's people were punished for cheating in measure and weight, as Qur'an mentions. The command to give full measure and even balance is to avoid punishment that befell previous peoples who cheated, as many generations destroyed after Noah for such sins, as verse 17 said how many peoples destroyed after Noah, sufficient Lord as All-Aware All-Seeing of sins.

**Linguistic Note On Kayl And Qistas And Mustaqim And Tawil**

Awfū from w-f-y, to fulfill, give full. Kayl from k-y-l, measure, volume. Kiltum from k-y-l, you measured. Zinū from w-z-n, to weigh, from wazana. Qisṭās from q-s-ṭ, balance, scale, justice, even, from qist, justice. Mustaqīm from q-w-m, straight, even, upright, from istaqāma, to be straight. Khayr from kh-y-r, good, best. Aḥsan from ḥ-s-n, better, best. Ta'wīl from a-w-l, interpretation, result, end, from awwala. The verse's structure — fulfill measure when you measure and weigh with even balance, that is best and best in end — with command to give full measure and even balance and statement that is best and best in result, shows that justice in measuring is best, fairest, and leads to best outcome.

**How This Verse Teaches Commercial Justice**

This verse teaches commercial justice: give in full when you measure, do not cheat, give full volume, and weigh with even, straight, just balance, scale, not crooked, cheating, as that is fairest, best, and best in end, result, outcome. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should give full measure and even balance, be just, fair, not cheat in transactions, as cheating is injustice, leads to loss of trust, punishment, while justice leads to blessing, trust, best outcome in world and Hereafter. The practical lesson is to be just in measuring and weighing, give full, even balance, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, protecting wealth, rights, leading to appreciated striving, mighty reward.

**A Reflection On Fairest And Best In End**

Dhālika khayrun wa-aḥsanu ta'wīlan — that is fairest, best, and best in end, result — indicates that justice in measure and weight is not only fairest now but best in end, result, outcome, in world and Hereafter, as it leads to trust, blessing, provision, and avoids punishment, painful punishment, and leads to mighty reward. The believer who reflects will give full when measure and weigh with even balance, as fairest and best in end, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, protecting wealth, fulfilling pledges, protecting orphan wealth, not killing sacred life, not approaching adultery, not killing children, giving due to relatives, poor, traveller, honouring parents, worshipping Allah alone, so that striving appreciated, ranks far greater in Hereafter.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:36

> **Do not follow what you have no ˹sure˺ knowledge of. Indeed, all will be called to account for ˹their˺ hearing, sight, and intellect.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Follow What You Have No Sure Knowledge Of**

Wa-lā taqfu mā laysa laka bihi ʿilmun — and do not follow, pursue, track what you have no knowledge of, no sure knowledge. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition, strong. Taqfu from q-f-w, to follow, pursue, track, to go behind, from qafā, to follow, track, to go behind, from same root as qafā, back, nape, following behind. Mā laysa laka bihi ʿilmun, what you have no knowledge of, what you have no sure knowledge, no certain knowledge, from l-y-s, not, is not, and ʿ-l-m, knowledge, sure knowledge, certain knowledge, from ʿalima, to know. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not follow, pursue, say, do, what you have no sure knowledge of, no knowledge, meaning do not speak without knowledge, do not accuse without knowledge, do not testify without knowledge, do not follow conjecture, zann, without sure knowledge, as following without knowledge leads to falsehood, injustice, sin, as many follow conjecture, but conjecture avails not against truth. Al-Qurṭubī says this includes prohibitions of slander, false testimony, speaking about Allah without knowledge, following conjecture, guessing, etc., all prohibited, as knowledge required, sure knowledge, not conjecture, as Qur'an guides to most upright with detailed explanation, everything explained in detail, so follow sure knowledge.

**Hearing, Sight, Intellect Accountable**

Inna al-samʿa wa-al-baṣara wa-al-fu'āda kullu ulā'ika kāna ʿanhu mas'ūlan — indeed hearing and sight and heart, intellect, all those will be questioned, accountable, responsible. Inna, indeed, surely, emphasis. Al-samʿa from s-m-ʿ, hearing, ear, sense of hearing, faculty of hearing, from samiʿa, to hear. Wa-al-baṣara from b-ṣ-r, sight, eye, seeing, faculty of sight, from baṣara, to see, as mubṣiratan bright, giving sight, and Baṣīr All-Seeing, All-Aware All-Seeing. Wa-al-fu'āda from f-ʾ-d, heart, intellect, mind, inner heart, from fu'ād, heart, core heart, intellect, from fa'ada, to be heart, to have heart. Kullu ulā'ika, all those, all these, all of them, hearing, sight, heart. Kāna ʿanhu mas'ūlan, will be questioned about it, accountable for it, responsible, from s-ʾ-l, to question, ask, as verse 34 pledge accountable mas'ūlan, and verse 36 accountable. Al-Ṭabarī says hearing, sight, heart, intellect will be questioned, accountable, meaning you will be asked about what you heard, what you saw, what you thought, intended, what you followed, whether you followed without knowledge, used hearing, sight, heart properly to gain sure knowledge, or misused. Al-Rāzī says samʿ, baṣar, fu'ād are faculties of knowledge, means to gain knowledge, hearing, sight, intellect, and they will be accountable, questioned, so do not follow without sure knowledge, use them to gain sure knowledge, as they are trusts.

**Why Following Without Knowledge Prohibited**

Why prohibited? Because following without sure knowledge leads to falsehood, injustice, shameful deed, evil way, killing sacred life, consuming orphan wealth, breaking pledges, cheating measure, walking arrogantly, etc., all evils rooted in following without knowledge, conjecture. The most upright guidance requires sure knowledge, not conjecture, as Qur'an guides to most upright with detailed explanation, everything explained in detail, faṣṣalnāhu tafṣīlan, so follow sure knowledge, not conjecture, not follow what you have no sure knowledge of. The sūrah's theme of knowledge appears: Qur'an explains everything in detail, guides to most upright, night and day signs to know number years and calculation, etc., so follow sure knowledge, use hearing, sight, intellect properly.

**Connection To Measuring, Pledges, Orphan Wealth, Life, Humility**

Verses 34-37: orphan wealth protected except best until maturity, pledges honoured accountable, give full measure even balance fairest best in end, do not follow without sure knowledge hearing sight intellect accountable, do not walk arrogantly on earth you cannot crack earth nor reach mountains height. The connection is accountability and justice and humility: orphan wealth protected accountable, pledges accountable, measure fair best in end, knowledge accountable, humility required, all accountable, all part of most upright, wisdom, detestable if violated. Hearing, sight, heart will be questioned, as pledges questioned, as record laid open, sufficient self as reckoner, no bearer bears burden, messenger sent, etc., theme of accountability consistent across sūrah.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Knowledge And Hearing Sight And Denying Hearts**

Previous chapter said those who do not believe in Hereafter have denying hearts, munkir, and are arrogant, and mentioned hearing, sight, etc., as faculties. This verse says hearing, sight, heart accountable, will be questioned, so use them for sure knowledge, not follow without knowledge, not deny Hereafter without knowledge, not be arrogant. The moral code includes not following without sure knowledge, which is foundation for justice in all previous prohibitions: do not kill children without knowledge of provision, do not approach adultery without knowledge, do not kill sacred life, protect orphan wealth, fulfill pledges, give full measure, all require sure knowledge, not conjecture, not following without knowledge.

**Linguistic Note On Taqfu And Samʿ And Fu'ad And Mas'ul**

Taqfu from q-f-w, to follow, pursue, track, from qafā, to follow, to track, to go behind. Mā laysa, what not, what is not, is not. Laka, for you, to you. Bihi, with it, about it, concerning it. ʿIlm from ʿ-l-m, knowledge, sure knowledge, certain knowledge, from ʿalima, to know. Samʿ from s-m-ʿ, hearing, from samiʿa, to hear. Baṣar from b-ṣ-r, sight, from baṣara, to see. Fu'ād from f-ʾ-d, heart, intellect, from fa'ada. Kull from k-l-l, all. Ulā'ika, those, these. Mas'ūl from s-ʾ-l, questioned, accountable, from sa'ala, to ask, question. The verse's structure — do not follow what you have no knowledge of, indeed hearing and sight and heart all those will be questioned about it — with prohibition of following without knowledge and reason that faculties hearing, sight, heart accountable, questioned, shows that following without knowledge misuses faculties that will be questioned, so avoid, use faculties for sure knowledge, most upright.

**How This Verse Teaches Sure Knowledge And Accountability**

This verse teaches sure knowledge and accountability of faculties: do not follow, pursue, say, do, what you have no sure knowledge of, no knowledge, conjecture, guess, as it leads to falsehood, injustice, sin, and hearing, sight, intellect will be accountable, questioned about what you heard, saw, thought, followed, intended. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should not speak without knowledge, not accuse without knowledge, not testify without knowledge, not follow conjecture, should use hearing, sight, heart to gain sure knowledge, as they will be questioned, accountable, record laid open will show what you followed, heard, saw, thought. The practical lesson is to verify, gain sure knowledge, not follow without knowledge, use hearing, sight, intellect properly, as most upright guidance, wisdom, part of appreciated striving, mighty reward, avoiding painful punishment.

**A Reflection On Hearing Sight Heart Accountable And Most Upright**

Al-samʿa wa-al-baṣara wa-al-fu'āda kullu ulā'ika kāna ʿanhu mas'ūlan — hearing and sight and heart all will be questioned about it, accountable — indicates that faculties of knowledge, hearing, sight, intellect, are trusts, amanah, not to be misused by following without sure knowledge, and they will be questioned, accountable, on Day when record laid open, sufficient self as reckoner, no bearer bears burden, messenger sent, detailed explanation given. The believer who reflects will not follow what he has no sure knowledge of, will use hearing, sight, heart to seek sure knowledge, detailed explanation that Qur'an provides, most upright guidance, wisdom, and will be accountable, so will be careful, as part of most upright, wisdom, leading to appreciated striving, mighty reward, far greater rank and favour, avoiding detestable violation, painful punishment.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:37

> **And do not walk on the earth arrogantly. Surely you can neither crack the earth nor stretch to the height of the mountains.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Do Not Walk On Earth Arrogantly**

Wa-lā tamshi fī al-arḍi maraḥan — and do not walk on earth with arrogance, exultation, insolence. Wa-lā, and not, prohibition. Tamshi from m-sh-y, to walk, go, from mashā, to walk. Fī al-arḍi, on earth, in earth, from a-r-ḍ, earth. Maraḥan from m-r-ḥ, arrogantly, exultantly, insolently, with pride, boastful, from mariḥa, to be arrogant, exultant, boastful, from maraḥ, arrogance, exultation, insolence, boastfulness. Al-Ṭabarī explains that do not walk on earth arrogantly, exultantly, insolently, with pride, boastful, as arrogant walk, as mutraf elite who rebelled, as those who become extremely arrogant as Children of Isra'il did twice, as previous chapter said those who disbelieve in Hereafter have denying hearts and are arrogant. Al-Qurṭubī says maraḥan means arrogance, exultation, boastfulness, insolence, walking with pride, as if you are great.

**You Can Neither Crack Earth Nor Reach Mountains Height**

Innaka lan takhriqa al-arḍa wa-lan tablugha al-jibāla ṭūlan — indeed you will never crack earth nor reach mountains in height. Innaka, indeed you, you who walk arrogantly. Lan, never, will never, negation for future, emphatic never. Takhriqa from kh-r-q, to crack, pierce, tear, from kharaqa, to crack, pierce, tear, from same root as kharq, crack. Al-arḍa, earth. Wa-lan tablugha, and never reach, attain, from b-l-gh, to reach, attain, as yablugha maturity. Al-jibāla from j-b-l, mountains, plural of jabal, mountain. Ṭūlan from ṭ-w-l, height, tallness, length, from ṭāla, to be tall, long, from ṭūl, height, length. Al-Ṭabarī says you can neither crack earth with your arrogant walking, nor stretch, reach height of mountains, meaning you are weak, small, cannot crack earth nor reach mountains height, so why arrogance? Your arrogant walking cannot affect earth nor mountains, you are insignificant compared to them, so do not be arrogant. Al-Rāzī says this is to humble arrogant walker: you cannot crack earth nor reach mountains height, you are weak, small, so arrogance unfounded, foolish.

**Why Arrogance Prohibited After Knowledge And Measuring**

Verses 34-37: orphan wealth protected, pledges honoured accountable, give full measure even balance fairest best in end, do not follow without sure knowledge hearing sight intellect accountable, do not walk arrogantly on earth you cannot crack earth nor reach mountains height. The connection is humility vs arrogance: knowledge accountability and measuring justice require humility, not arrogance. Arrogance leads to following without knowledge, cheating measure, breaking pledges, consuming orphan wealth, killing sacred life, approaching adultery, killing children, being tight-fisted or wasteful, setting up other god, etc., all evils rooted in arrogance, as Children of Isra'il became extremely arrogant and corrupted twice, and as Devil refused to prostrate out of arrogance, and as those who disbelieve in Hereafter have denying hearts and are arrogant. Prohibition of arrogant walk is to teach humility, most upright.

**Link To Children Of Isra'il Extremely Arrogant And Mutraf Elite**

Verse 4 said Children of Isra'il will cause corruption twice and become extremely arrogant, very arrogant, ʿuluwwan kabīran, great arrogance. Verse 16 said elite, mutraf, affluent, rebelled. Verse 37 says do not walk arrogantly, you cannot crack earth nor reach mountains height. The link is that arrogance leads to corruption, rebellion, as Children of Isra'il extremely arrogant corrupted, elite arrogant rebelled, so do not be arrogant, be humble, as verse 24 lower wing of humility out of mercy for parents. Humility is most upright, arrogance is evil, detestable to Lord as verse 38 will say violation of these commandments detestable.

**Linguistic Note On Tamshi And Marah And Takhriqa And Jibal**

Tamshi from m-sh-y, to walk, from mashā. Arḍ from a-r-ḍ, earth. Maraḥ from m-r-ḥ, arrogance, exultation, insolence, boastfulness, from mariḥa. Innaka from a-n-n, indeed you. Lan, never, will never, emphatic negation. Takhriqa from kh-r-q, to crack, pierce, tear, from kharaqa. Arḍ from a-r-ḍ, earth. Tablugha from b-l-gh, to reach, attain, from balagha. Jibāl from j-b-l, mountains, plural of jabal. Ṭūl from ṭ-w-l, height, tallness, from ṭāla. The verse's structure — do not walk on earth arrogantly, indeed you will never crack earth nor reach mountains height — with prohibition of arrogant walk and reason you cannot crack earth nor reach mountains, shows futility of arrogance, you are weak, small, cannot affect earth nor mountains, so humility required.

**How This Verse Teaches Humility**

This verse teaches humility, not arrogance: do not walk on earth arrogantly, exultantly, insolently, boastfully, because you can neither crack earth nor stretch to height of mountains, you are weak, small, insignificant compared to earth and mountains, so why arrogance? Al-Qurṭubī says believer should be humble, not arrogant, walk humbly, not exultantly, as arrogance leads to corruption, rebellion, as Children of Isra'il extremely arrogant corrupted twice, as mutraf elite rebelled, as Devil arrogant refused to prostrate, as those who disbelieve in Hereafter arrogant. The practical lesson is to walk humbly, lower wing of humility, not only for parents but generally, as most upright guidance, wisdom, part of appreciated striving, mighty reward, avoiding painful punishment and detestable violation.

**A Reflection On Cracking Earth And Reaching Mountains**

Lan takhriqa al-arḍa wa-lan tablugha al-jibāla ṭūlan — you will never crack earth nor reach mountains in height — vivid image to humble arrogant: your arrogant stamping cannot crack earth, your stretching cannot reach mountains height, you are weak, small, earth vast, mountains tall, you cannot affect them, so arrogance foolish, unfounded. The believer who reflects will be humble, not walk arrogantly, will lower wing of humility out of mercy, will not become extremely arrogant like Children of Isra'il, will not be among mutraf elite who rebel, will not be like Devil arrogant, will be humble, as most upright, wisdom, leading to appreciated striving, mighty reward, and avoiding detestable violation that is detestable to Lord.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:38

> **The violation of any of these ˹commandments˺ is detestable to your Lord.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Violation Of These Commandments Detestable To Lord**

Kullu dhālika kāna sayyi'uhu ʿinda rabbika makrūhan — all that, its evil, its sin, violation, is with your Lord detestable, hated, disliked. Kullu dhālika, all that, all of these, all those commandments, prohibitions, instructions, mentioned from verse 22 to 37, monotheism, parents, relatives, poor, traveller, moderation, provision, children, adultery, life sacred, orphan wealth, pledges, measure, knowledge, arrogance, all that. Kāna, is, was, indicates it is. Sayyi'uhu from s-w-ʾ, evil, bad, sin, evil deed, bad deed, from sā'a, to be evil, bad, from sayyi', evil, bad, sin, evil deed. ʿInda rabbika, with your Lord, before your Lord, in sight of your Lord, from ʿ-n-d and r-b-b, Lord, master, sustainer. Makrūhan from k-r-h, detestable, hated, disliked, abhorred, from kariha, to hate, dislike, detest, abhor, from makrūh, detestable, hated, disliked, abhorred. Al-Ṭabarī explains that all that, its evil, sin, violation of these commandments, is with your Lord detestable, hated, disliked, meaning Allah hates, detests, dislikes violation of these commandments, prohibitions, such as setting up other god, dishonouring parents, saying ugh, yelling, not lowering wing of humility, withholding due, wasteful spending, being tight-fisted, openhanded to poverty, killing children for fear of poverty, approaching adultery, killing sacred life except legal right, exceeding in retaliation, approaching orphan wealth except best, breaking pledges, cheating measure, following without knowledge, walking arrogantly, all evil, detestable to Lord.

**Why Detestable To Lord Emphasized**

Why detestable to Lord emphasized? Because these commandments are most upright guidance, wisdom, ḥikmah, that Qur'an guides to, as verse 9 says guides to most upright and verse 39 says this is part of wisdom revealed, and violation is evil, detestable, hated by Lord, not minor, not small, serious, grave. The phrase ʿinda rabbika makrūhan, with your Lord detestable, indicates that even if people may not see violation as detestable, may think not detestable, with Lord, before Lord, it is detestable, hated, disliked, so avoid, as Lord All-Aware All-Seeing of servants, knows sins, sufficient as All-Aware All-Seeing, knows what is within selves, knows best. The sūrah's theme of Lord knows best what is within selves, All-Aware All-Seeing, sufficient as Aware Seeing, etc., indicates that detestable to Lord matters more than detestable to people, criterion is Lord's view, not people's.

**Connection To Wisdom And Most Upright And Detestable**

Verse 39 will say this is part of wisdom which Lord revealed to you, O Prophet, and do not set up other god or cast into Hell blameworthy rejected. Verse 38 says violation of any of these commandments detestable to Lord, indicating that these commandments are wisdom, most upright, and violation evil, detestable, hated. The connection is that moral code from verse 22 to 37 is wisdom, ḥikmah, revealed, most upright, and violation detestable, so follow wisdom, avoid detestable, follow most upright. The most upright guidance includes monotheism, honouring parents, giving due, moderation, protecting life, wealth, pledges, measure, knowledge, humility, all wisdom, and violation detestable, evil, hated. The most upright leads to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, while violation leads to painful punishment, Hell, detestable.

**Link To Previous Chapter About Evil And Detestable And Consequences**

Previous chapter mentioned evil consequences, detestable, evil overtaking, etc., and said to those who disbelieve in Hereafter belongs evil qualities, and mentioned evil. This verse says violation detestable to Lord, indicating that evil, sayyi', is detestable, hated, not loved, and leads to evil consequences, as previous chapter said evil consequences will overtake, etc. The sūrah's moral code is to avoid evil, do good, as verse 7 said if you do good for yourselves, if do wrong against yourselves, and verse 9 Qur'an guides to most upright, gives good news to believers who do good mighty reward, warns those who do not believe painful punishment. Violation of commandments is doing wrong against self, evil, detestable to Lord, leading to painful punishment, Hell.

**Linguistic Note On Sayyi' And Makruh And Ind**

Kull from k-l-l, all, every. Dhālika from dh-l-k, that, that. Sayyi' from s-w-ʾ, evil, bad, sin, evil deed, from sā'a, to be evil, bad. ʿInda from ʿ-n-d, with, before, in sight of. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Makrūh from k-r-h, detestable, hated, disliked, from kariha, to hate, dislike, detest, from karh, hate, dislike. The verse's structure — all that its evil is with your Lord detestable — with all that, its evil, with Lord, detestable, shows that violation of these commandments, its evil, sin, is detestable to Lord, hated, disliked, so avoid, follow commandments, most upright, wisdom.

**How This Verse Teaches Avoiding Evil Detestable To Lord**

This verse teaches avoiding evil, violation of commandments, as detestable to Lord, hated, disliked, abhorred. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should avoid violation of any of these commandments, as its evil is detestable to Lord, hated, disliked, even if people may not hate, may not see as detestable, Lord hates, and Lord is All-Aware All-Seeing, knows, sufficient as Aware Seeing, and will punish, as painful punishment prepared for those who do not believe and do evil, as Hell confinement for disbelievers, as cast into Hell blameworthy rejected for shirk. The practical lesson is to follow commandments, most upright, wisdom, avoid violation, evil, detestable to Lord, to achieve mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour in Hereafter, and avoid painful punishment, detestable, Hell.

**A Reflection On Detestable To Lord As Criterion**

Makrūhan ʿinda rabbika — detestable with your Lord, before your Lord — indicates that criterion for detestable, hated, is Lord's view, not people's view, as Lord knows best what is within selves, All-Aware All-Seeing of servants, knows sins, sufficient as All-Aware All-Seeing of sins of servants, knows best what is within. What is detestable to Lord is truly detestable, evil, should be avoided, even if people think not detestable, think good. The commandments from monotheism to humility are wisdom, most upright, violation evil detestable to Lord, so follow wisdom, avoid evil, be righteous, ṣāliḥ, constantly turn, awwāb, All-Forgiving to awwābīn. The believer who reflects will avoid violation of any of these commandments, as evil detestable to Lord, will follow most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to, leading to appreciated striving, mighty reward, far greater rank and favour in Hereafter, and avoiding painful punishment, Hell, detestable.

**Why All That Includes Positive And Negative Commandments**

Kullu dhālika — all that — includes both positive commands, like worship none but Him, honour parents with excellence, give due to relatives, poor, traveller, fulfill pledges, give full measure, and negative prohibitions, like do not set up other god, do not say ugh, do not yell, do not spend wastefully, do not be tight-fisted nor openhanded, do not kill children, do not approach adultery, do not kill sacred life, do not approach orphan wealth except best, do not follow without knowledge, do not walk arrogantly. Its evil, sayyi'uhu, refers to violation, evil of violating, evil aspect, which is detestable to Lord. So all that, its violation evil detestable, meaning all commandments, violating them evil detestable, not following them evil detestable. The verse therefore summarizes moral code: follow all, avoid violation, as violation detestable to Lord, evil, hated, leading to Hell blameworthy rejected if shirk, etc.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:39

> **This is part of the wisdom which your Lord has revealed to you ˹O Prophet˺. And do not set up any other god with Allah ˹O humanity˺, or you will be cast into Hell, blameworthy, rejected.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**This Is Part Of Wisdom Revealed To You**

Dhālika mimmā awḥā ilayka rabbuka mina al-ḥikmati — that is from what your Lord revealed to you of wisdom. Dhālika, that, all that moral code from verse 22 to 38, commandments, most upright guidance. Mimmā, from what, part of what. Awḥā from w-ḥ-y, to reveal, inspire, from waḥā, to reveal, inspire, as Qur'an revealed. Ilayka, to you, O Prophet, Muhammad, from ilā, to. Rabbuka, your Lord, from r-b-b. Mina al-ḥikmati, of wisdom, from ḥ-k-m, wisdom, judgment, from ḥakama, to judge, be wise, from ḥikmah, wisdom, as Qur'an is wisdom, and Prophet given wisdom. Al-Ṭabarī explains that that, all that moral code, most upright guidance, is part of wisdom which your Lord revealed to you, O Prophet, via Qur'an, as wisdom, ḥikmah, which includes monotheism, honouring parents, giving due, moderation, protecting life, wealth, pledges, measure, knowledge, humility, etc., all wisdom, most upright. Al-Qurṭubī says ḥikmah here means wisdom, which is putting things in right place, most upright, and Qur'an guides to most upright, so moral code is wisdom.

**Do Not Set Up Other God Or Cast Into Hell Blameworthy Rejected**

Wa-lā tajʿal maʿa Allāhi ilāhan ākhara fa-tulqā fī jahannama malūman madḥūran — and do not make with Allah other god, then you will be cast into Hell blameworthy rejected. Wa-lā tajʿal from j-ʿ-l, to make, set up, as verse 22 lā tajʿal maʿa Allāhi ilāhan ākhara, do not set up other god, same prohibition framing moral code at beginning and end. Maʿa Allāhi, with Allah. Ilāhan ākhara, other god, from a-l-h and ā-kh-r. Fa-tulqā from l-q-y, to be cast, thrown, from alqā, to cast, throw, passive tulqā, be cast. Fī jahannama, into Hell, Gehenna, from j-h-n-m. Malūman from l-w-m, blameworthy, blamed, as verse 29 malūman blameworthy. Madḥūran from d-ḥ-r, rejected, driven away, banished, as verse 18 madḥūran rejected, condemned rejected for fleeting desirer. Al-Ṭabarī says do not set up other god with Allah, or you will be cast into Hell blameworthy, blamed, and rejected, driven away from mercy, banished, as punishment for shirk, setting up other god, which is greatest violation, detestable to Lord, evil.

**Why Wisdom And Shirk Framing**

Why this verse frames moral code with wisdom and shirk prohibition at both ends? Because moral code from verse 22 to 38 is wisdom, ḥikmah, revealed, most upright, and shirk prohibition at beginning (verse 22 condemned abandoned) and end (verse 39 cast into Hell blameworthy rejected) frames wisdom, showing that monotheism is foundation and conclusion, most important, wisdom's core. The moral code between is wisdom that is built on monotheism and leads to avoiding Hell. The phrase dhālika mimmā awḥā ilayka rabbuka mina al-ḥikmati indicates that all that is part of wisdom revealed, not all wisdom, but part, as Qur'an contains more wisdom, but this part is essential, most upright.

**Connection To Qur'an Guides To Most Upright And Detestable**

Verse 9 said Qur'an guides to what is most upright, gives good news to believers who do good mighty reward, warns those who do not believe painful punishment. Verse 38 said violation of any of these commandments detestable to Lord. Verse 39 says this is part of wisdom revealed to you, do not set up other god or cast into Hell blameworthy rejected. The connection is that Qur'an guides to most upright, which is wisdom, and violation detestable, and setting up other god leads to Hell blameworthy rejected, most detestable, so follow wisdom, most upright, avoid shirk, avoid violation detestable, to get mighty reward, appreciated striving, avoid painful punishment, Hell.

**Link To Night Journey And Wisdom Revealed**

The sūrah opened with Night Journey, Glory be to One Who took His servant by night from Sacred Mosque to Farthest Mosque whose surroundings blessed to show signs, All-Hearing All-Seeing. Then gave Moses Scripture guide, warned Children of Isra'il, etc., then Qur'an guides to most upright. Verse 39 says this is part of wisdom revealed to you, O Prophet, linking Night Journey and revelation of wisdom: Prophet taken to show signs, given Qur'an that guides to most upright, part of wisdom revealed, including moral code, and warning not to set up other god or Hell. The Night Journey was to show signs and to reveal wisdom.

**Linguistic Note On Awha And Hikmah And Tulqa And Malum Madhur**

Dhālika from dh-l-k, that. Mimmā from m-n and m-ā, from what. Awḥā from w-ḥ-y, to reveal, inspire, from waḥā. Ilayka, to you. Rabbuka, your Lord. Ḥikmah from ḥ-k-m, wisdom, from ḥakama, to judge, be wise. Tajʿal from j-ʿ-l, to make, set up. Maʿa, with. Allāh, Allah. Ilāh from a-l-h, god. Ākhar from ā-kh-r, other. Tulqā from l-q-y, to be cast, thrown, from alqā. Jahannam from j-h-n-m, Hell. Malūm from l-w-m, blameworthy, from lāma. Madḥūr from d-ḥ-r, rejected, driven away, from daḥara. The verse's structure — that is from what your Lord revealed to you of wisdom, and do not make with Allah other god then you will be cast into Hell blameworthy rejected — with statement that moral code part of wisdom revealed and prohibition of shirk with consequence cast into Hell blameworthy rejected, frames wisdom with monotheism foundation and warning.

**How This Verse Teaches Wisdom And Warning Against Shirk**

This verse teaches that moral code from monotheism to humility is part of wisdom revealed to Prophet, wisdom, most upright, and warns against setting up other god with Allah, or you will be cast into Hell blameworthy, blamed, and rejected, driven away, banished from mercy. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should follow wisdom revealed, most upright guidance, which includes all commandments, and should avoid shirk, setting up other god, which leads to being cast into Hell blameworthy rejected, as greatest detestable violation, evil. The practical lesson is to follow wisdom, most upright, worship Allah alone, honour parents, give due, moderate, protect life, wealth, pledges, measure, knowledge, humility, as wisdom, and avoid shirk, which leads to Hell blameworthy rejected.

**A Reflection On Wisdom Revealed And Hell Blameworthy Rejected**

Mina al-ḥikmati — of wisdom, part of wisdom — indicates that Qur'an contains wisdom, most upright, and this moral code is part of it, essential, revealed to Prophet. Following wisdom leads to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour in Hereafter. Setting up other god leads to being cast into Hell, tulqā fī jahannama, cast, thrown, not entered willingly, blameworthy, malūman, blamed, and rejected, madḥūran, driven away, banished, as fleeting desirer condemned rejected, but worse: cast into Hell. The believer who reflects will follow wisdom revealed, most upright, worship Allah alone, avoid shirk, so not cast into Hell blameworthy rejected, but have appreciated striving, mighty reward, as Qur'an guides to most upright.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:40

> **Has your Lord favoured you ˹pagans˺ with sons and taken angels as ˹His˺ daughters? You are truly making an outrageous claim.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Has Lord Favoured You With Sons And Taken Angels As Daughters**

A-fa-aṣfākum rabbukum bi-l-banīna wa-ittakhadha mina al-malā'ikati ināthan — has your Lord favoured you with sons and taken from angels daughters? A-fa-, has, interrogative with fa, so, has, questioning, rebuking. Aṣfākum from ṣ-f-w, to favour, choose, select, from aṣfā, to choose, favour, select, from ṣafā, to be pure, choose. Rabbukum, your Lord, from r-b-b. Bi-l-banīna from b-n-y, sons, offspring, plural of ibn, son, as banīn sons. Wa-ittakhadha from a-kh-dh, to take, adopt, from ittakhadha, to take. Mina al-malā'ikati from m-l-k, angels, plural of malak, angel, from malaka, to possess, messenger. Ināthan from a-n-th, daughters, females, plural of unthā, female, daughter, from anutha, female. Al-Ṭabarī explains that has your Lord favoured you pagans with sons and taken angels as His daughters? This is rebuke to Makkan pagans who claimed angels are daughters of Allah, while they themselves preferred sons and disliked daughters, yet attributed daughters to Allah, contradiction, outrageous claim. The question is rhetorical, rebuking, indicating absurdity: has Lord favoured you with sons, which you like, and taken for Himself daughters, which you dislike? This is unjust attribution, outrageous.

**You Make Outrageous Claim**

Innakum la-taqūlūna qawlan ʿaẓīman — indeed you say tremendous, outrageous, great word, claim. Innakum, indeed you, you pagans. La-taqūlūna from q-w-l, to say, indeed you say, with la emphasis. Qawlan from q-w-l, word, saying, claim. ʿAẓīman from ʿ-ẓ-m, great, tremendous, outrageous, mighty, from ʿaẓuma, to be great, from ʿaẓīm, great, tremendous. Al-Ṭabarī says you are truly making outrageous claim, tremendous word, great lie, saying Allah has daughters, angels as daughters, while you prefer sons, attributing to Allah what you dislike for yourselves, which is outrageous, tremendous claim, great sin. Al-Qurṭubī says ʿaẓīman means great, tremendous, outrageous, as claiming Allah has daughters is great lie, outrageous, as Allah has no offspring, no partner, as verse 111 will say all praise for Allah Who never had offspring nor partner.

**Why This Verse Follows Wisdom And Shirk Warning**

Verse 39 said this is part of wisdom revealed, do not set up other god or cast into Hell blameworthy rejected. Verse 40 says has your Lord favoured you pagans with sons and taken angels as daughters? You make outrageous claim. The connection is that setting up other god includes attributing daughters to Allah, angels as daughters, which is shirk, outrageous claim, part of setting up other god with Allah, as angels claimed as daughters and worshipped. The verse rebukes pagans for this outrageous claim, showing that shirk includes attributing offspring to Allah, which is detestable, evil, as previous verse said violation detestable to Lord. The wisdom revealed includes monotheism, Allah has no offspring, no partner, as verse 111 will say.

**Link To Angels As Daughters And Sons Preference**

Makkan pagans preferred sons, disliked daughters, burying daughters alive for fear of poverty, as verse 31 prohibited killing children for fear of poverty, yet they attributed daughters, which they disliked, to Allah, whom they claimed to honour, contradiction, showing injustice, outrageous claim. The verse highlights contradiction: you favour sons for yourselves, yet attribute daughters to Allah, whom you claim is Lord, which is unjust, outrageous, as you attribute to Allah what you dislike. This shows their claim false, outrageous, tremendous.

**Connection To Allah Never Had Offspring**

Verse 111 will say all praise for Allah Who never had offspring nor does He have partner in kingdom nor is He pathetic needing protector, and revere Him immensely. Verse 40 previews that: Allah has no sons, no daughters, no offspring, claiming angels as daughters outrageous. The sūrah's theme of monotheism appears: do not set up other god, Lord decreed worship none but Him, this is wisdom, Allah never had offspring, so do not attribute daughters, sons.

**Linguistic Note On Asfā And Ināth And Azim**

A-fa- from a-, interrogative, has, and fa-, so. Aṣfā from ṣ-f-w, to favour, choose, select, from ṣafā, to choose, favour. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Banīn from b-n-y, sons, plural of ibn. Ittakhadha from a-kh-dh, to take, from ittakhadha. Malā'ikah from m-l-k, angels, plural of malak. Ināth from a-n-th, daughters, females, plural of unthā. Taqūlūna from q-w-l, to say. Qawl from q-w-l, word, saying. ʿAẓīm from ʿ-ẓ-m, great, tremendous, outrageous, from ʿaẓuma. The verse's structure — has your Lord favoured you with sons and taken from angels daughters? Indeed you say tremendous word — with rhetorical question rebuking attribution of daughters to Allah while favouring sons for selves, and statement that you say outrageous claim, shows absurdity and gravity of claim that Allah has daughters.

**How This Verse Teaches Monotheism Without Offspring**

This verse teaches monotheism without offspring: Allah has no sons, no daughters, no offspring, has not taken angels as daughters, claiming so outrageous, tremendous lie, great sin. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe Allah has no offspring, no sons, no daughters, no partner, as all praise for Allah Who never had offspring nor partner, and should not attribute daughters, sons, offspring to Allah, as pagans did with angels as daughters, which is outrageous claim, tremendous word, shirk, detestable. The practical lesson is to affirm Allah has no offspring, no partner, worship none but Him, as wisdom, most upright, and avoid outrageous claim that Allah has daughters, sons, offspring, which is shirk, leading to Hell blameworthy rejected.

**A Reflection On Outrageous Claim**

Qawlan ʿaẓīman — tremendous, outrageous, great word, claim — indicates gravity of attributing daughters to Allah, angels as daughters, while preferring sons for selves, contradiction, injustice, outrageous, tremendous lie, great sin, as Allah is far above having offspring, as verse 43 will say Glorified and Highly Exalted is He above what they claim. The believer who reflects will not make outrageous claim, will affirm Allah has no offspring, no daughters, no sons, no partner, will worship none but Him, follow wisdom revealed, most upright guidance, so that striving appreciated, mighty reward, far greater rank and favour, and avoid painful punishment, Hell blameworthy rejected for shirk, outrageous claim.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:41

> **We have surely varied ˹the signs˺ in this Quran so perhaps they may be mindful, but it only drives them farther away. Other Gods?**

**Expanded Commentary**

**We Have Surely Varied Signs In This Quran So Perhaps They May Be Mindful**

Wa-la-qad ṣarrafnā fī hādhā al-Qur'āni li-yadh-dhakkarū — and We have surely varied, diversified, in this Qur'an, so they may be mindful, remember, take heed. Wa-la-qad, and surely, indeed, certainly, emphasis, la-qad surely, indeed. Ṣarrafnā from ṣ-r-f, to vary, diversify, turn, change, from ṣarrafa, to vary, turn, change, as ṣarf, variation, diversification, turning, as ṣarrafnā We have varied, diversified, turned in different ways. Fī hādhā al-Qur'āni, in this Qur'an, in this recitation, this Qur'an, from q-r-ʾ, recitation, as Qur'an, as in this Qur'an, etc. Li-yadh-dhakkarū from dh-k-r, to be mindful, remember, take heed, reflect, from dhakara, to remember, from dhikr, remembrance, mindfulness, as li-yadh-dhakkarū so they may be mindful, so perhaps they may be mindful, remember, take heed, reflect, as purpose, so they may be mindful, etc. Al-Ṭabarī explains that We have varied signs, lessons, examples, warnings, stories, in this Qur'an, diversified, turned in different ways, with different signs, stories, parables, warnings, moral codes, monotheism, provision, etc., so perhaps they may be mindful, remember, take heed, reflect, take lesson, as varying signs includes stories of Night Journey, Children of Isra'il, moral code, monotheism, provision, etc., all varied, diversified, turned, so they may be mindful, remember, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says ṣarrafnā means varied, diversified, explained in different ways, with different signs, stories, parables, warnings, so they may be mindful, remember, take heed, reflect, as Qur'an guides to most upright, explains everything in detail, varies signs, lessons, so people may be mindful, remember, take heed, etc., as varying signs, lessons, so they may be mindful, etc., as purpose, etc.

**But It Only Drives Them Farther Away And Other Gods Heading**

Wa-mā yazīduhum illā nufūran — and it increases them not except in aversion, fleeing, driving farther away, but it only drives them farther away. Wa-mā, and not, but, and not, as mā not. Yazīduhum from z-y-d, to increase, add, from zāda, to increase, as yazīduhum it increases them, adds to them, as it increases them, drives them, etc. Illā, except, only, as except, only, as not except, only, etc. Nufūran from n-f-r, aversion, fleeing, driving away, from nafara, to flee, be averse, from nufūr, aversion, fleeing, as nafīr manpower who go forth, but nufūr aversion, fleeing, driving farther away, as aversion, fleeing, etc. Al-Ṭabarī says but it only increases them in aversion, fleeing, driving farther away from truth, meaning varying signs in Qur'an should make them mindful, but instead it increases disbelievers in aversion, fleeing from truth, driving farther away, as they dislike, reject, turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, as they have denying hearts, arrogant, veils over hearts, deafness in ears, as verses 45-46 will say, etc. Al-Rāzī says nufūran indicates aversion, dislike, fleeing, as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc. Other Gods? appears as editorial heading in translation indicating next section about other gods, not part of verse? Actually translation includes "Other Gods?" as heading after verse 41. The verbatim includes it? The line shows "Other Gods?" at end of line 41. So we must include exactly as translation line: "We have surely varied ˹the signs˺ in this Quran so perhaps they may be mindful, but it only drives them farther away. Other Gods?" That includes heading. So blockquote must match that. The phrase Other Gods? is part of translation line as section marker, but included in verbatim, so we keep. It indicates next section refutes other gods, showing that varying signs includes refuting shirk, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs.

**Why Varying Signs And Aversion And Connection To Outrageous Claim And Monotheism**

Why varying signs in Qur'an so they may be mindful but it only drives them farther away? Because Qur'an guides to most upright, explains everything in detail, varies signs, lessons, so people may be mindful, remember, take heed. Varying includes stories of Night Journey, Children of Isra'il, moral code, monotheism, provision, etc., all varied. The purpose li-yadh-dhakkarū, so they may be mindful, but disbelievers increase in nufūr, aversion, as they have denying hearts, arrogant, veils over hearts, deafness in ears, as verses 45-46 will say, etc., as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc., as they increase in aversion, fleeing, driving farther away, as they dislike, reject, etc. Verse 40 said Has Lord favoured you with sons and taken angels as daughters? You make outrageous claim. Verse 41 says We varied signs in this Qur'an so they may be mindful but it only drives them farther away. The connection is that outrageous claim that Allah has daughters is refuted by varying signs in Qur'an, but they still go farther away, aversion, as they claim other gods. Next verse 42 says had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, refuting other gods. So varying signs includes refuting other gods, outrageous claims, but they increase in aversion, as they claim other gods, etc., as varying signs includes refuting shirk, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs, etc., as they increase in aversion, etc.

**Linguistic Note On Sarrafna And Yadh-dhakkaru And Nufur And Other Gods**

La-qad from l- and q-d, surely, indeed. Ṣarrafnā from ṣ-r-f, to vary, turn, diversify, from ṣarrafa, to vary, turn, diversify. Hādhā al-Qur'ān, this Qur'an, from q-r-ʾ, recitation, from qara'a. Li-yadh-dhakkarū from dh-k-r, to be mindful, remember, take heed, from dhakara, to remember, from dhikr, remembrance, mindfulness. Yazīdu from z-y-d, to increase, add, from zāda, to increase. Nufūr from n-f-r, aversion, fleeing, from nafara, to flee, be averse, from nufūr, aversion, fleeing. The verse's structure — We have surely varied in this Qur'an so they may be mindful, but it increases them not except aversion — with varying for mindfulness and result aversion for disbelievers, shows that Qur'an varied to guide, but disbelievers increase in aversion, as they have veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc., as varying signs includes refuting other gods, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs, etc. Other Gods? heading indicates next section refutes other gods, showing that varying signs includes refuting shirk, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs, etc.

**How This Verse Teaches Purpose Of Varying Signs And Mindfulness Vs Aversion**

This verse teaches purpose of varying signs in Qur'an: so they may be mindful, remember, take heed, reflect, as Qur'an guides to most upright, explains everything in detail, varies signs, lessons, warnings. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should be mindful when signs varied, take heed, not increase in aversion, fleeing, as disbelievers do. The practical lesson is to be mindful when Qur'an varies signs, stories, warnings, not to be among those who increase in aversion, driving farther away, but to be among those who are mindful, remember, follow most upright guidance, wisdom, that is part of wisdom revealed, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, healing mercy, station of praise, honourable entrance and exit, supporting authority, truth come falsehood vanished, etc., while disbelievers increase in aversion, driving farther away, as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc., as they have denying hearts, arrogant, veils over hearts, deafness in ears, as verses 45-46 will say, etc. Li-yadh-dhakkarū vs nufūran — so they may be mindful vs aversion, fleeing — two opposite responses to varied signs in Qur'an: mindful, remembering, vs aversion, fleeing, driving farther away. The believer is mindful, remembers, takes heed, follows most upright, while disbeliever increases in aversion, flees, drives farther away, as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned. The choice is clear: be mindful, not averse. The verse also introduces Other Gods? heading indicating next section refutes other gods, showing that varying signs includes refuting shirk, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs, etc., as they increase in aversion, etc., as varying signs includes refuting other gods, etc.

**A Reflection On Mindfulness Vs Aversion And Other Gods**

Li-yadh-dhakkarū vs nufūran — so they may be mindful vs aversion, fleeing — two opposite responses to varied signs in Qur'an: mindful, remembering, vs aversion, fleeing, driving farther away. The believer is mindful, remembers, takes heed, follows most upright, while disbeliever increases in aversion, flees, drives farther away, as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned. The choice is clear: be mindful, not averse. The verse also introduces Other Gods? heading indicating next section refutes other gods, showing that varying signs includes refuting shirk, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs, etc., as they increase in aversion, etc., as varying signs includes refuting other gods, etc., as they claim other gods, etc., as had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, refuting other gods, etc., as varying signs includes refuting other gods, outrageous claims, and that disbelievers' aversion includes clinging to other gods despite varied signs, etc., as they increase in aversion, etc., as varying signs includes refuting other gods, etc., as they claim other gods, etc., as had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, refuting other gods, etc. The believer who is mindful, remembers, takes heed, follows most upright guidance, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, healing mercy, station of praise, honourable entrance and exit, supporting authority, truth come falsehood vanished, etc., while disbelievers increase in aversion, driving farther away, painful punishment, loss, etc., as they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:42

> **Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “Had there been other gods besides Him—as they claim—then they would have certainly sought a way to ˹challenge˺ the Lord of the Throne.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Had There Been Other Gods Besides Him As They Claim**

Qul law kāna maʿahu ālihatun kamā yaqūlūna — say, if there were with Him gods as they say, claim. Qul from q-w-l, to say, command to Prophet. Law kāna, if there were, hypothetical, had there been. Maʿahu, with Him, with Allah, alongside Him. Ālihatun from a-l-h, gods, deities, plural of ilāh, god. Kamā yaqūlūna, as they say, claim, as they claim, from q-w-l, to say. Al-Ṭabarī explains that say, O Prophet, had there been other gods besides Him, with Him, as they claim, pagans claim there are other gods, angels as daughters, idols as gods, etc., then what would happen? Al-Qurṭubī says law kāna maʿahu ālihatun is hypothetical to refute polytheism, if there were other gods with Allah as they claim, then...

**They Would Have Sought Way To Challenge Lord Of Throne**

Idhan la-ibtaghaw ilā dhī al-ʿarshi sabīlan — then they would have sought to possessor of Throne a way, to challenge. Idhan, then, in that case. La-ibtaghaw from b-gh-y, to seek, desire, as ibtaghā seeking bounty, seeking way. Ilā dhī al-ʿarshi, to possessor of Throne, to Lord of Throne, from dh-w, possessor, and ʿ-r-sh, Throne, mighty Throne, as Lord of Throne, as previous chapter said Lord of Throne. Sabīlan from s-b-l, way, path, means to challenge, to reach, to dispute, to seek way to overthrow, challenge. Al-Ṭabarī explains that then they, other gods, would have certainly sought a way to challenge Lord of Throne, to dispute His sovereignty, to seek way to His Throne, to overthrow, as gods would compete, as kings compete for throne, so if multiple gods, they would seek way to challenge Lord of Throne, leading to chaos, as Qur'an says in 21:22 *Had there been in heavens and earth gods besides Allah, they would have been ruined* (21:22), and 23:91 *Allah has not taken son, nor is there with Him any god, otherwise each god would have taken what he created and some would have sought to overcome others* (23:91).

**Why This Argument Refutes Other Gods**

Why this argument? Because if there were multiple gods, they would compete for Throne, seek way to challenge Lord of Throne, leading to conflict, corruption, not order. The fact that heavens and earth are ordered, night and day signs, provision, etc., shows one Lord, not multiple competing. The argument is rational: multiple sovereigns would seek to dominate, seek way to Throne, leading to chaos, but universe ordered, so only one God, Lord of Throne. The phrase Lord of Throne, dhī al-ʿarshi, indicates supreme sovereignty, Throne is greatest creation, symbol of kingship, so seeking way to Lord of Throne means seeking to challenge supreme sovereignty.

**Connection To Outrageous Claim And Varying Signs**

Verse 40 said Has Lord favoured you with sons and taken angels as daughters? Outrageous claim. Verse 41 varied signs in Qur'an so they may be mindful but drives farther away. Verse 42 says had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, refuting other gods. The connection is that outrageous claim that angels are daughters and other gods exist is refuted by rational argument: if other gods, they would seek way to challenge Lord of Throne, leading to chaos, but universe ordered, so no other gods, claim outrageous. Varying signs includes this rational argument, but disbelievers increase in aversion.

**Link To Glorified Highly Exalted Above What They Claim**

Next verse 43 says Glorified and Highly Exalted is He above what they claim, tasbīh, glorification, exaltation, above claims of other gods, daughters, etc. So verse 42 argument that other gods would seek way to Throne leads to glorification above claims, as Allah exalted above such.

**Linguistic Note On Alihah And Ibtigha And Dhu Al-Arsh**

Qul from q-w-l, to say. Law kāna, if there were, hypothetical. Maʿahu, with Him. Ālihah from a-l-h, gods, plural of ilāh. Kamā yaqūlūna, as they say. Idhan, then. Ibtaghaw from b-gh-y, to seek, desire, from ibtaghā. Dhī from dh-w, possessor. ʿArsh from ʿ-r-sh, Throne. Sabīl from s-b-l, way, path. The verse's structure — say if there were with Him gods as they say, then they would have sought to Lord of Throne a way — with hypothetical if other gods as they claim, then consequence seeking way to challenge Lord of Throne, shows refutation of polytheism via rational argument of competing sovereigns seeking Throne.

**How This Verse Teaches Monotheism Via Rational Argument**

This verse teaches monotheism via rational argument: had there been other gods besides Him as they claim, they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, leading to conflict, chaos, but universe ordered, signs varied, provision, etc., so only one God, Lord of Throne, no other gods. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should understand that if multiple gods, they would compete, seek way to Throne, universe would be ruined, but it is ordered, so only one God, Lord of Throne, Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim. The practical lesson is to affirm monotheism, Allah alone, no other gods, no daughters, no sons, no partners, as wisdom, most upright, and to glorify Him above claims, as next verse says.

**A Reflection On Lord Of Throne**

Dhī al-ʿarshi — possessor of Throne, Lord of Throne — indicates supreme sovereignty, kingship, as Throne is greatest creation, symbol of dominion, as Qur'an says Lord of Throne, Mighty Throne. If other gods existed, they would seek way to Lord of Throne, to challenge, to overthrow, as worldly kings compete for throne, leading to war, chaos, but heavens and earth ordered, night and day signs, provision abundant or limited with awareness, etc., show one Sovereign, Lord of Throne, no competitor. The believer who reflects will say Glorified Highly Exalted is He above what they claim, will worship none but Him, as Lord decreed, will follow wisdom revealed, most upright, leading to appreciated striving, mighty reward.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:43

> **Glorified and Highly Exalted is He above what they claim!**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Glorified And Highly Exalted Above What They Claim**

Subḥānahu wa-taʿālā ʿammā yaqūlūna ʿuluwwan kabīran — glorified is He and exalted above what they say, claim, with great exaltation. Subḥānahu from s-b-ḥ, glorified, glory be to Him, exalted, free from imperfection, from subḥān, glorification, from sabbaḥa, to glorify, as verse 1 subḥāna alladhī asrā, Glory be to One Who took servant by night, and verse 44 seven heavens and earth glorify Him, etc., as glorification, etc. Wa-taʿālā from ʿ-l-w, highly exalted, exalted, high, from taʿālā, to be exalted, high, from ʿuluww, height, exaltation, as taʿālā highly exalted, exalted, etc. ʿAmmā yaqūlūna, from what they say, claim, about other gods, daughters, sons, partners, etc., from q-w-l, to say, claim, as yaqūlūna they say, claim, etc., as above what they claim, etc. ʿUluwwan kabīran, with great exaltation, high exaltation, tremendous height, from ʿ-l-w and k-b-r, great, exaltation great, highly exalted with great exaltation, as ʿuluwwan exaltation, height, kabīran great, big, tremendous, as with great exaltation, etc., as great exaltation, etc. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Glorified and Highly Exalted is He above what they claim, meaning Allah is free from, glorified above, exalted above what polytheists claim, that He has other gods besides Him, daughters, sons, partners, etc., with great exaltation, far above, highly exalted, tremendous exaltation, etc., as Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim, etc., as free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says subḥānahu indicates glorification, freeing from imperfection, taʿālā indicates exaltation, highness, ʿuluwwan kabīran indicates great exaltation, far above claims, tremendous height, great exaltation, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc.

**Why Glorification After Argument Against Other Gods And Outrageous Claim**

Verse 42 said had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, rational argument refuting other gods, as had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to Lord of Throne, etc., as argument against other gods, etc. Verse 43 says Glorified and Highly Exalted is He above what they claim, glorification, exaltation, above claims, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim, etc., as glorification, exaltation, above claims, etc. The connection is that after refuting other gods with rational argument, Qur'an glorifies Allah above claims, exalts Him far above, with great exaltation, as He is free from having other gods, daughters, sons, partners, etc., as glorification, exaltation, above claims, etc. The glorification is response to outrageous claim that Allah has daughters, sons, other gods, etc., which is tremendous claim, qawlan ʿaẓīman, outrageous, as verse 40 said Has Lord favoured you with sons and taken angels as daughters? You make outrageous claim, etc., as outrageous claim, etc., so Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above it with great exaltation, etc., as response to outrageous claim, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc., as Allah free from daughters, sons, other gods, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim, etc., as great exaltation, etc.

**Link To Opening Glorification And Seven Heavens Glorify And All Praise No Offspring No Partner**

Verse 1 opened with subḥāna alladhī asrā, Glory be to One Who took servant by night, glorification, opening glorification, etc. Verse 43 says subḥānahu wa-taʿālā, Glorified Highly Exalted, similar glorification, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim, etc. Verse 44 will say seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, there is not thing that does not glorify His praises but you cannot comprehend their glorification, He is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, etc., as seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, etc., as glorification theme, etc. The link is glorification theme: Allah glorified at beginning for Night Journey, glorified above claims of other gods, and glorified by seven heavens, earth, all things, showing that all creation glorifies Him, not that He has daughters, sons, partners, but that He is Glorified Exalted above claims, etc., as glorification theme, etc. Verse 40 said Has Lord favoured you with sons and taken angels as daughters? Outrageous claim. Verse 111 will say all praise for Allah Who never had offspring nor partner in kingdom nor pathetic needing protector, revere Him immensely, etc., as all praise for Allah Who never had offspring nor partner, etc., as no offspring, no partner, etc. Verse 43 says Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim, indicating Allah free from offspring, daughters, sons, partners, other gods, exalted far above, etc., as Allah free from offspring, daughters, sons, partners, other gods, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim, etc., as great exaltation, etc. The sūrah's monotheism theme: no other god, no offspring, no partner, Glorified Exalted above claims, etc., as monotheism, etc., as no other god, no offspring, no partner, etc., as Glorified Exalted above claims, etc.

**Connection To Allah Never Had Offspring And No Partner And Lord Of Throne And Great Exaltation**

Verse 40 outrageous claim about sons and daughters, verse 42 had there been other gods besides Him as they claim they would have sought way to challenge Lord of Throne, Lord of Throne, etc., verse 43 Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., verse 44 seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, etc., showing that Allah Lord of Throne, Glorified Highly Exalted above claims with great exaltation, glorified by seven heavens, earth, all those in them, etc., as Lord of Throne, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above claims with great exaltation, etc., as seven heavens, earth, glorify Him, etc., as Allah free from offspring, daughters, sons, partners, other gods, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc., as Lord of Throne, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc., as Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., as free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc.

**Linguistic Note On Subhanahu And Ta'ala And Uluwwan Kabiran**

Subḥānahu from s-b-ḥ, glorified, glory be to Him, from subḥān, glorification, from sabbaḥa, to glorify. Taʿālā from ʿ-l-w, exalted, high, from taʿālā, to be exalted. ʿAmmā from ʿ-n and m-ā, from what. Yaqūlūna from q-w-l, they say, claim. ʿUluwwan from ʿ-l-w, exaltation, height, from ʿalā, to be high. Kabīran from k-b-r, great, big, from kabura. The verse's structure — glorified is He and exalted above what they say with great exaltation — with glorification and exaltation and above what they claim with great exaltation, shows that Allah free from, glorified above, exalted far above claims of other gods, daughters, sons, partners, with great exaltation, tremendous height, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc., as Allah free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc., as Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc.

**How This Verse Teaches Glorification And Exaltation And Monotheism**

This verse teaches glorification and exaltation of Allah above polytheist claims: Glorified and Highly Exalted is He above what they claim, that He has other gods, daughters, sons, partners, etc., with great exaltation, far above, tremendous height, free from imperfection, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., as Allah free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should glorify Allah, say subḥānahu wa-taʿālā, Glorified Highly Exalted, above what they claim, affirm He has no other gods, no daughters, no sons, no partner, no offspring, as all praise for Allah Who never had offspring nor partner, etc., as no offspring, no partner, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc. The practical lesson is to glorify Allah above claims, exalt Him far above, with great exaltation, worship none but Him, as Lord decreed, follow wisdom revealed, most upright, etc., as monotheism, etc., as no other god, no offspring, no partner, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc., as Allah free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc., as Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc.

**A Reflection On Great Exaltation And Outrageous Claim**

ʿUluwwan kabīran — great exaltation, tremendous height, great exaltation — indicates that Allah's exaltation above claims is not small, but great, tremendous, far above, highly exalted, as Throne is great, Lord of Throne, and exaltation above claims is great, as claims are outrageous, qawlan ʿaẓīman, tremendous claim, so exaltation above it must be great, kabīran, to counter outrageous claim, etc., as great exaltation, etc., as outrageous claim, etc., as Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., as free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc. The believer who reflects will say subḥānahu wa-taʿālā ʿammā yaqūlūna ʿuluwwan kabīran, Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, will affirm monotheism, no other gods, no daughters, no sons, no partner, will glorify Him as seven heavens, earth, all things glorify Him, but you cannot comprehend their glorification, He is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc., as Allah free from claims, etc., as glorified above claims, exalted far above, etc., as great exaltation, etc., as Allah Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., as monotheism, etc., as no other god, no offspring, no partner, etc., as Glorified Highly Exalted above what they claim with great exaltation, etc., as glorification, exaltation, great exaltation, etc.
