## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:44

> **The seven heavens, the earth, and all those in them glorify Him. There is not a single thing that does not glorify His praises—but you ˹simply˺ cannot comprehend their glorification. He is indeed Most Forbearing, All-Forgiving.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Seven Heavens, Earth, All Those In Them Glorify Him**

Tusabbiḥu lahu al-samāwātu al-sabʿu wa-al-arḍu wa-man fīhinna — glorify Him the seven heavens and earth and whoever is in them. Tusabbiḥu from s-b-ḥ, to glorify, praise, from sabbaḥa, to glorify, as subḥāna glorified, subḥānahu glorified. Lahu, Him, for Him, to Him. Al-samāwātu al-sabʿu, seven heavens, from s-m-w, heavens, sky, and sabʿ, seven. Wa-al-arḍu, and earth, from a-r-ḍ. Wa-man fīhinna, and whoever is in them, all those in them, from m-n, who, and fī, in. Al-Ṭabarī explains that seven heavens, earth, and all those in them, all creation, angels, humans, jinn, animals, etc., glorify Him, declare His glory, free from imperfection, exalted above claims. Al-Qurṭubī says tusabbiḥu indicates glorification, tasbīh, by all creation, seven heavens, earth, and whoever in them, all glorify Him, as all creation glorifies, praises.

**Not A Single Thing That Does Not Glorify His Praises**

Wa-in min shay'in illā yusabbiḥu bi-ḥamdihi — and there is not a thing except glorifies with His praise. Wa-in, and not, there is not. Min shay'in, a thing, any thing, from sh-y-ʾ, thing. Illā, except. Yusabbiḥu from s-b-ḥ, glorifies, praises, as tusabbiḥu. Bi-ḥamdihi, with His praise, with His hamd, praise, from ḥ-m-d, praise, hamd. Al-Ṭabarī says there is not a single thing, any thing, that does not glorify His praises, meaning every thing, all creation, glorifies Him with His praise, declares glory and praise, even if you do not comprehend. Al-Rāzī says this indicates universal glorification: everything glorifies Him with praise, not only seven heavens, earth, and those in them, but every thing, all creation, glorifies.

**You Cannot Comprehend Their Glorification**

Wa-lākin lā tafqahūna tasbīḥahum — but you do not comprehend, understand their glorification. Wa-lākin, but, however. Lā tafqahūna from f-q-h, to comprehend, understand, have fiqh, from faqiha, to comprehend, understand. Tasbīḥahum from s-b-ḥ, their glorification, tasbīh, from sabbaḥa. Al-Ṭabarī explains that you, humans, do not comprehend, understand their glorification, how they glorify, because their glorification is in ways you do not understand, languages you do not comprehend, as all things glorify but you cannot comprehend. Al-Qurṭubī says lā tafqahūna tasbīḥahum means you do not understand their tasbīh, glorification, because it is not in human language, or because you are heedless, as seven heavens, earth, all things glorify but humans often heedless.

**He Is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving**

Innahu kāna ḥalīman ghafūran — indeed He is Most Forbearing, All-Forgiving. Innahu, indeed He, Allah. Kāna, is, was. Ḥalīman from ḥ-l-m, Most Forbearing, forbearing, clement, from ḥaluma, to be forbearing, from ḥilm, forbearance, clemency. Ghafūran from gh-f-r, All-Forgiving, forgiving, from ghafara, to forgive, as Ghafūr All-Forgiving. Al-Ṭabarī says He is Most Forbearing, All-Forgiving, forbearing, not hastening punishment despite claims, outrageous claims that He has daughters, other gods, etc., and forgiving to those who repent, turn, as awwābīn, constantly turning. Al-Rāzī says ḥalīman ghafūran indicates forbearance and forgiveness, as Allah forbears despite polytheist claims, does not hasten punishment, and forgives those who repent.

**Why Universal Glorification After Glorified Exalted Above Claims**

Verse 43 said Glorified Highly Exalted is He above what they claim, with great exaltation. Verse 44 says seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, not a thing that does not glorify His praises but you cannot comprehend their glorification, He is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving. The connection is that after stating Allah Glorified Exalted above claims of other gods, daughters, etc., Qur'an says all creation, seven heavens, earth, all those in them, and every thing, glorifies Him with praise, showing that true glorification is by all creation, not that He has daughters, sons, partners, but that He is Glorified by all, exalted above claims. The fact that you cannot comprehend their glorification indicates that glorification is universal but human comprehension limited.

**Link To Night Journey And Signs And Bounty**

Verse 1 opened with Glory be to One Who took servant by night, glorification for Night Journey. Verse 44 says seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, universal glorification, showing that Night Journey through heavens is part of universal glorification, and that all things glorify, including those seen in Night Journey. The sūrah's theme of signs: night and day signs, shadows, etc., all signs that glorify, as seven heavens, earth, all things glorify Him, sign of monotheism.

**Linguistic Note On Tusabbihu And Hamd And Tafqahuna And Halim Ghafur**

Tusabbiḥu from s-b-ḥ, to glorify, from sabbaḥa. Samāwāt from s-m-w, heavens. Sabʿ from s-b-ʿ, seven. Arḍ from a-r-ḍ, earth. Man fīhinna, whoever in them. Shay' from sh-y-ʾ, thing. Yusabbiḥu from s-b-ḥ, glorifies. Ḥamd from ḥ-m-d, praise. Tafqahūna from f-q-h, to comprehend, understand, from faqiha. Tasbīḥ from s-b-ḥ, glorification. Ḥalīm from ḥ-l-m, Forbearing, from ḥaluma. Ghafūr from gh-f-r, Forgiving, from ghafara. The verse's structure — seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, not a thing except glorifies with His praise, but you do not comprehend their glorification, indeed He is Forbearing Forgiving — with universal glorification by heavens, earth, all in them, every thing, but human incomprehension, and divine forbearance forgiveness, shows that all creation glorifies, human often fails to comprehend, but Allah forbearing forgiving.

**How This Verse Teaches Universal Glorification And Forbearance**

This verse teaches universal glorification: seven heavens, earth, all those in them, and every thing glorifies Him with praise, but you cannot comprehend their glorification, and He is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, forbearing despite claims, forgiving to those who repent. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should glorify Allah, join universal glorification, say subḥān Allāh, Glorified is Allah, as all things glorify, and should know that all things glorify but you cannot comprehend how, so be humble, not arrogant, and know Allah Forbearing Forgiving, forbears despite polytheist claims, forgives those who turn. The practical lesson is to glorify Allah often, as all creation does, to be mindful that all things glorify, and to seek forgiveness, as He is Forbearing Forgiving.

**A Reflection On Forbearing Forgiving After Claims**

Ḥalīman ghafūran — Most Forbearing, All-Forgiving — after universal glorification and incomprehension, indicates that despite polytheist claims that Allah has daughters, other gods, etc., which are outrageous, tremendous, Allah is Forbearing, does not hasten punishment, forbears, and Forgiving to those who repent, turn, as awwābīn. The fact that seven heavens, earth, all things glorify Him, but polytheists claim daughters, sons, other gods, shows their claims false, against universal glorification, but Allah forbears, does not hasten, forgives if they repent. The believer who reflects will join universal glorification, glorify Him with praise, comprehend that all things glorify, even if he cannot comprehend how, and will seek forbearance and forgiveness, as He is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:45

> **When you ˹O Prophet˺ recite the Quran, We put a hidden barrier between you and those who do not believe in the Hereafter.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**When You Recite Quran We Put Hidden Barrier Between You And Those Who Do Not Believe In Hereafter**

Wa-idhā qara'ta al-Qur'āna jaʿalnā baynaka wa-bayna al-ladhīna lā yu'minūna bi-l-ākhirati ḥijāban mastūran — and when you recite Qur'an, We made between you and those who do not believe in Hereafter a hidden barrier, veil. Wa-idhā, and when, when, conditional, when you recite Qur'an, etc. Qara'ta from q-r-ʾ, to recite, read, as iqra' read, Qur'an recitation, as qara'ta you recite, you read, as when you recite Qur'an, etc., as qara'ta al-Qur'āna when you recite Qur'an, etc. Al-Qur'āna, Qur'an, recitation, from q-r-ʾ, recitation, as Qur'an, etc., as when you recite Qur'an, etc. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make, put, set, as jaʿalnā We made, put, set, as We made, put, set, etc., as We put hidden barrier, etc., as jaʿalnā baynaka wa-bayna al-ladhīna lā yu'minūna bi-l-ākhirati ḥijāban mastūran We made between you and those who do not believe in Hereafter hidden barrier, etc. Baynaka wa-bayna, between you and between, between you and those, as bayn between, as baynaka between you, wa-bayna and between, as between you and those, etc. Al-ladhīna lā yu'minūna bi-l-ākhirati, those who do not believe in Hereafter, deny Hereafter, disbelieve in Hereafter, as lā yu'minūna do not believe, from a-m-n, to believe, as yu'minūna they believe, lā yu'minūna do not believe, as those who do not believe in Hereafter, etc., as verse 10 warns those who do not believe in Hereafter painful punishment prepared, etc., and verse 45-46 about veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as those who do not believe in Hereafter, etc. Ḥijāban from ḥ-j-b, barrier, veil, partition, from ḥajaba, to veil, barrier, as ḥijāb barrier, veil, partition, as ḥijāban barrier, veil, etc., as hidden barrier, etc. Mastūran from s-t-r, hidden, veiled, concealed, from satara, to veil, hide, from mastūr, hidden, veiled, as mastūran hidden, veiled, concealed, as hidden barrier, veiled barrier, etc. Al-Ṭabarī explains that when Prophet recites Qur'an, Allah puts hidden barrier between him and those who do not believe in Hereafter, veil that prevents them from benefiting, understanding, as they have veils over hearts, deafness in ears, aversion, as next verse says, We cast veils over hearts unable to comprehend and deafness in ears, when you mention Lord alone in Qur'an they turn backs in aversion, etc., as barrier, veil, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as they disbelieve in Hereafter, so barrier, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says ḥijāban mastūran means hidden barrier, veil that is hidden, not seen, prevents them from understanding Qur'an, as they disbelieve in Hereafter, so barrier, hidden barrier, veil, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as they have veils over hearts, deafness in ears, aversion, etc., as barrier, etc.

**Why Hidden Barrier For Those Who Do Not Believe In Hereafter And Connection To Varying Signs And Aversion And Veils**

Why hidden barrier? Because those who do not believe in Hereafter have denying hearts, arrogant, as previous chapter said, and have veils over hearts, deafness in ears, aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as next verse says, We cast veils over hearts unable to comprehend and deafness in ears, when you mention Lord alone in Qur'an they turn backs in aversion, etc., so they cannot comprehend Qur'an, even when recited, as they have veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, etc. The barrier is hidden, mastūran, not visible, but effective, preventing benefit, as they are veiled, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as veils over hearts, deafness in ears, are hidden, not visible, but prevent comprehension, etc., as hidden barrier, etc. The sūrah's theme: Qur'an guides to most upright, gives good news to believers who do good mighty reward, warns those who do not believe in Hereafter painful punishment, but those who do not believe have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, so they do not benefit, etc., as Qur'an guides to most upright, etc., as varying signs, etc. Verse 41 said We varied signs in Qur'an so they may be mindful but it only drives them farther away, nufūran, aversion, etc., as varying signs should make mindful, but disbelievers increase in aversion, nufūran, because barrier, veils, deafness, aversion prevent mindfulness, as they disbelieve in Hereafter, so barrier hidden, etc., as varying signs should make mindful, but they increase in aversion, etc., as barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, etc.

**Link To Hearing Sight Heart Accountable And Not Comprehending Glorification And Veils Over Hearts Deafness Aversion**

Verse 36 said do not follow without sure knowledge, hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as do not follow without sure knowledge, etc., as hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc. Verse 44 said you cannot comprehend their glorification, seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, there is not thing that does not glorify His praises but you cannot comprehend their glorification, He is Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, etc., as you cannot comprehend their glorification, etc., as comprehension, etc. Verse 45-46 says hidden barrier, veils over hearts unable to comprehend, deafness in ears, aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, veils over hearts unable to comprehend, deafness in ears, aversion, etc., as veils over hearts unable to comprehend, deafness in ears, aversion, etc., as comprehension, etc. The link is comprehension: you cannot comprehend glorification of all things, and disbelievers cannot comprehend Qur'an due to veils, deafness, barrier, as they disbelieve in Hereafter, have denying hearts, arrogant, so hearing, sight, heart not used properly, accountable, etc., as hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as veils over hearts, deafness in ears, aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as they cannot comprehend, etc., as hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as comprehension, etc., as hidden barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as they disbelieve in Hereafter, etc.

**Linguistic Note On Qara'ta And Hijaban Masturan And Ladhina La Yu'minuna Bil-Akhirah**

Idhā from a-dh-, when. Qara'ta from q-r-ʾ, to recite, read, from qara'a. Qur'ān from q-r-ʾ, recitation, from qara'a. Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make, put, from jaʿala. Bayn from b-y-n, between, from bayna. Ladhīna lā yu'minūna, those who do not believe, from a-m-n, to believe, from amina. Ākhirah from ā-kh-r, Hereafter, from ākhar, last. Ḥijāb from ḥ-j-b, barrier, veil, from ḥajaba, to veil. Mastūr from s-t-r, hidden, veiled, from satara, to veil, hide. The verse's structure — when you recite Qur'an, We made between you and those who do not believe in Hereafter hidden barrier — with condition when recite Qur'an and result hidden barrier between Prophet and disbelievers in Hereafter, shows that recitation of Qur'an, which guides to most upright, is barrier for those who disbelieve in Hereafter, preventing benefit, as they have veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as they disbelieve in Hereafter, etc., as barrier, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as they have veils over hearts, deafness in ears, aversion, etc., as barrier, etc.

**How This Verse Teaches Barrier For Disbelievers In Hereafter And Mindfulness**

This verse teaches that when Prophet recites Qur'an, Allah puts hidden barrier between him and those who do not believe in Hereafter, veil that prevents them from benefiting, understanding, as they have veils over hearts, deafness in ears, aversion, as next verse says, We cast veils over hearts unable to comprehend and deafness in ears, when you mention Lord alone in Qur'an they turn backs in aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as barrier, etc., as they disbelieve in Hereafter, so barrier, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe in Hereafter, so no barrier, veil, and should recite Qur'an and benefit, be mindful, not increase in aversion, as disbelievers do, etc., as believer believes in Hereafter, no barrier, etc., as believer recites Qur'an, benefits, mindful, etc., as disbelievers have barrier, etc. The practical lesson is to believe in Hereafter, so that no hidden barrier, veil, deafness, aversion, and to recite Qur'an, be mindful, follow most upright guidance, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, healing mercy, station of praise, honourable entrance and exit, supporting authority, truth come falsehood vanished, etc., while disbelievers in Hereafter have hidden barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, increases in nufūr, driving farther away, as verse 41 says, and turns backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc., as barrier, etc.

**A Reflection On Hidden Barrier And Veils And Hearing Sight Heart**

Ḥijāban mastūran — hidden barrier, veiled barrier, concealed veil — indicates barrier that is hidden, not seen, but effective, preventing disbelievers in Hereafter from benefiting from Qur'an recitation, as they have veils over hearts, deafness, aversion, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as veils over hearts, deafness in ears, are hidden, not visible, but prevent comprehension, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as barrier, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as veils over hearts, deafness in ears, are hidden, not visible, but prevent comprehension, etc., as hidden barrier, etc. The barrier is hidden, mastūran, meaning veiled, concealed, not visible, but real, as veils over hearts, deafness in ears, are hidden, not visible, but prevent comprehension, etc., as hidden barrier, etc., as veils over hearts, deafness in ears, are hidden, not visible, but prevent comprehension, etc., as hidden barrier, etc. The believer who believes in Hereafter has no barrier, no veil, no deafness, no aversion, benefits from Qur'an, is mindful, follows most upright, while disbeliever in Hereafter has hidden barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, increases in nufūr, driving farther away, as verse 41 says, and turns backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as verse 46 says, etc., as barrier, etc., as hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as do not follow without sure knowledge, hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as hearing, sight, heart accountable, etc., as hidden barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, etc., as they disbelieve in Hereafter, etc., as barrier, etc.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:46

> **We have cast veils over their hearts—leaving them unable to comprehend it—and deafness in their ears. And when you mention your Lord alone in the Quran, they turn their backs in aversion.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Veils Over Hearts Unable To Comprehend And Deafness In Ears**

Wa-jaʿalnā ʿalā qulūbihim akinnatan an yafqahūhu wa-fī ādhānihim waqran — and We made over their hearts veils that they not comprehend it and in their ears deafness. Wa-jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make, put, as previous jaʿalnā barrier. ʿAlā qulūbihim, over their hearts, upon their hearts, from q-l-b, hearts, plural of qalb, heart. Akinnatan from k-n-n, veils, coverings, that cover, from kanna, to cover, veil, from akinnah, veils, coverings, plural of kinān, veil, covering. An yafqahūhu, that they comprehend it, understand it, Qur'an, from f-q-h, to comprehend, understand, as lā tafqahūna tasbīḥahum, you do not comprehend their glorification, and lā tafqahūna comprehension. Wa-fī ādhānihim, and in their ears, from a-dh-n, ears, plural of udhun, ear. Waqran from w-q-r, deafness, heaviness, from waqara, to be heavy, deaf, from waqr, deafness, heaviness, as deafness. Al-Ṭabarī explains that We cast veils, coverings over their hearts, so they cannot comprehend Qur'an, cannot understand, and deafness, heaviness in their ears, so they cannot hear, benefit, as they disbelieve in Hereafter, have denying hearts, arrogant, barrier hidden, as previous verse. Al-Qurṭubī says akinnatan means veils, coverings, that prevent comprehension, and waqran means deafness, heaviness, preventing hearing, both prevent benefiting from Qur'an.

**When You Mention Lord Alone In Quran They Turn Backs In Aversion**

Wa-idhā dhakarta rabbaka fī al-Qur'āni waḥdahu wallaw ʿalā adbārihim nufūran — and when you mention your Lord alone in Qur'an, they turn on their backs in aversion, fleeing. Wa-idhā, and when. Dhakarta from dh-k-r, to mention, remember, as li-yadh-dhakkarū so they may be mindful, remember. Rabbaka, your Lord, from r-b-b. Fī al-Qur'āni, in Qur'an. Waḥdahu, alone, only Him, alone, from w-ḥ-d, alone, one, as waḥdahu alone, only Allah alone. Wallaw from w-l-y, to turn, turn away, from wallā, to turn, turn away, to turn back. ʿAlā adbārihim, on their backs, upon their backs, from d-b-r, back, rear, from dubur, back. Nufūran from n-f-r, aversion, fleeing, as verse 41 nufūran aversion, driving farther away, and verse 46 nufūran aversion. Al-Ṭabarī explains that when you, O Prophet, mention your Lord alone, only Allah alone, in Qur'an, without mentioning other gods, idols, they turn their backs in aversion, fleeing, disliking, as they dislike monotheism, want other gods mentioned, as they claim other gods, daughters, etc., so when Lord alone mentioned, they turn backs in aversion, flee.

**Why Veils, Deafness, Aversion For Mentioning Lord Alone**

Why veils over hearts, deafness in ears, turning backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned? Because they disbelieve in Hereafter, have denying hearts, arrogant, as previous chapter said, and have outrageous claim that Allah has daughters, other gods, etc., so when Lord alone mentioned, monotheism, they dislike, turn backs in aversion, flee, as they want other gods, idols, daughters, etc. The veils, deafness, aversion are punishment for disbelief in Hereafter, arrogance, denying hearts, and for outrageous claims, as they have barrier hidden, veils, deafness, aversion, so they cannot comprehend Qur'an, cannot benefit, increase in nufūr, aversion, driving farther away.

**Connection To Hidden Barrier And Varying Signs And Aversion**

Verse 45 said when you recite Qur'an We put hidden barrier between you and those who do not believe in Hereafter. Verse 46 says We cast veils over hearts unable to comprehend and deafness in ears, when you mention Lord alone in Qur'an they turn backs in aversion. Verse 41 said We varied signs in Qur'an so they may be mindful but it only drives them farther away, nufūran. The connection is that hidden barrier, veils, deafness, aversion are reasons why varying signs drives them farther away, increases in aversion, as they cannot comprehend, cannot hear, turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as they dislike monotheism, want other gods.

**Link To Hearing Sight Heart Accountable And Not Comprehending**

Verse 36 said hearing, sight, heart accountable, will be questioned. Verse 44 said you cannot comprehend their glorification, all things glorify but you cannot comprehend. Verse 46 says veils over hearts unable to comprehend, deafness in ears, turning backs in aversion, showing that disbelievers cannot comprehend Qur'an due to veils, deafness, as they have denying hearts, arrogant, barrier, and they turn backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, as they dislike monotheism, while believers comprehend, benefit, glorify, join universal glorification.

**Linguistic Note On Akinnah And Waqr And Wallaw And Nufur**

Jaʿalnā from j-ʿ-l, to make. Qulūb from q-l-b, hearts, plural of qalb. Akinnah from k-n-n, veils, coverings, plural of kinān, veil, from kanna, to cover. Yafqahū from f-q-h, to comprehend, understand. Ādhān from a-dh-n, ears, plural of udhun. Waqr from w-q-r, deafness, heaviness, from waqara, to be heavy, deaf. Dhakara from dh-k-r, to mention, remember. Rabb from r-b-b, Lord. Waḥdahu from w-ḥ-d, alone, from waḥada. Wallaw from w-l-y, to turn, turn away, from wallā. Adbār from d-b-r, backs, plural of dubur, back. Nufūr from n-f-r, aversion, fleeing, from nafara. The verse's structure — We made over hearts veils that they not comprehend and in ears deafness, and when you mention Lord alone in Qur'an they turn on backs in aversion — with veils preventing comprehension and deafness preventing hearing and turning backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned, shows that disbelievers in Hereafter have veils, deafness, aversion, preventing benefit from Qur'an, disliking monotheism.

**How This Verse Teaches Veils And Aversion For Disbelievers**

This verse teaches that for those who do not believe in Hereafter, Allah casts veils over hearts leaving them unable to comprehend Qur'an and deafness in ears, and when Lord alone mentioned in Qur'an they turn backs in aversion, fleeing, disliking monotheism, wanting other gods. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe in Hereafter, so no veils, no deafness, no aversion, should mention Lord alone in Qur'an and love monotheism, not turn backs in aversion, should be mindful, comprehend, benefit, as Qur'an guides to most upright, varies signs so they may be mindful. The practical lesson is to believe in Hereafter, remove veils, deafness, aversion, by faith, humility, not arrogance, and to mention Lord alone, love monotheism, not turn backs, as most upright.

**A Reflection On Mentioning Lord Alone And Turning Backs**

Wa-idhā dhakarta rabbaka fī al-Qur'āni waḥdahu wallaw ʿalā adbārihim nufūran — when you mention your Lord alone in Qur'an, they turn on backs in aversion — indicates that disbelievers dislike monotheism, Lord alone, want other gods, daughters, sons, partners, etc., so when Lord alone mentioned, without other gods, they turn backs in aversion, fleeing, disliking, as they have denying hearts, arrogant, veils, deafness, barrier, and increase in nufūr, aversion, driving farther away, as varying signs should make mindful but drives farther away. The believer who believes in Hereafter mentions Lord alone and loves, does not turn backs, benefits, is mindful, follows most upright, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, while disbeliever turns backs in aversion, has veils, deafness, barrier, painful punishment prepared.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:47

> **We know best how they listen to your recitation and what they say privately—when the wrongdoers say, “You would only be following a bewitched man.”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**We Know Best How They Listen And What They Say Privately**

Naḥnu aʿlamu bi-mā yastamiʿūna bihi idh yastamiʿūna ilayka wa-idh hum najwā — We know best with what they listen with it when they listen to you and when they are private conversation. Naḥnu aʿlamu, We know best, more knowing, from ʿ-l-m, to know, as aʿlamu knows best, as verse 25 Lord knows best what is within selves, and verse 54 Lord knows you best, and verse 55 Lord knows best those in heavens and earth. Bi-mā yastamiʿūna bihi, with what they listen, how they listen, with what they listen with it, from s-m-ʿ, to listen, hear, as samʿ hearing. Idh yastamiʿūna ilayka, when they listen to you, O Prophet, reciting Qur'an. Wa-idh hum najwā, and when they are private, private conversation, secret counsel, from n-j-w, private, secret, from najwā, private conversation, secret counsel. Al-Ṭabarī explains that We know best how they listen to your recitation, with what intention, mockery, spying, etc., and what they say privately, secret counsel, when they are in private, what they say secretly, as they have veils, deafness, aversion, barrier, but listen to Qur'an with mockery, spying, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says naḥnu aʿlamu indicates Allah knows best how they listen, with mockery, denial, and what they say privately in secret counsel.

**When Wrongdoers Say You Only Following Bewitched Man**

Idh yaqūlu al-ẓālimūna in tattabiʿūna illā rajulan masḥūran — when wrongdoers say, you follow not except a man bewitched, enchanted. Idh, when, as when they say privately. Yaqūlu al-ẓālimūna from ẓ-l-m, wrongdoers, oppressors, unjust, from ẓalama, to wrong, oppress, from ẓālim, wrongdoer, oppressor. In tattabiʿūna, you follow not, you follow only, from t-b-ʿ, to follow. Illā rajulan, except a man, only a man. Masḥūran from s-ḥ-r, bewitched, enchanted, affected by magic, from saḥara, to bewitch, enchant, from siḥr, magic, bewitchment, from masḥūr, bewitched, enchanted. Al-Ṭabarī explains that wrongdoers, oppressors, polytheists, say privately, in secret counsel, you would only be following a bewitched man, meaning Prophet Muhammad is bewitched, enchanted, affected by magic, so his Qur'an is magic, not revelation, so do not follow, as they say to followers, you follow only bewitched man, to dissuade. Al-Qurṭubī says this is what wrongdoers say privately, in najwā, secret counsel, to discourage, that Prophet is masḥūr, bewitched, so Qur'an is from bewitchment, not from Allah, as they claim.

**Why Wrongdoers Say Bewitched Man**

Why say bewitched man? Because they cannot comprehend Qur'an due to veils over hearts, deafness in ears, hidden barrier, aversion when Lord alone mentioned, so they think Qur'an is magic, bewitchment, as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, have denying hearts, arrogant. Saying bewitched man is attempt to explain Qur'an's effect, eloquence, varying signs, without believing, as they have no sure knowledge, follow conjecture. The sūrah's theme: those who do not believe in Hereafter have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, so they say bewitched man.

**Connection To Hidden Barrier, Veils, Deafness, Aversion, Varying Signs**

Verses 45-46 said hidden barrier, veils over hearts unable to comprehend, deafness in ears, turning backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned. Verse 47 says We know best how they listen and what they say privately, when wrongdoers say you follow only bewitched man. The connection is that despite barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, they still listen to Qur'an, but with mockery, spying, and say privately bewitched man, to explain Qur'an without believing, as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, have denying hearts. Varying signs in Qur'an should make mindful but drives farther away, as they say bewitched man.

**Link To See How They Call You Names So Astray Cannot Find Way**

Next verse 48 says observe how they label you, O Prophet, so misguided they cannot locate correct path. Verse 47 says they say you follow only bewitched man, which is calling name, saying masḥūr, bewitched, as name, insult. So following verse says observe how they label you, so far astray unable to find way, as they call bewitched, etc., as wrongdoers.

**Linguistic Note On Yastami'una And Najwa And Zalimuna And Mashur**

Aʿlam from ʿ-l-m, knows best. Yastamiʿūna from s-m-ʿ, to listen, from istamaʿa, to listen. Najwā from n-j-w, private conversation, secret counsel, from najā, to be private, secret. Yaqūlu from q-w-l, to say. Ẓālimūn from ẓ-l-m, wrongdoers, oppressors, from ẓalama. Tattabiʿūna from t-b-ʿ, to follow. Rajul from r-j-l, man. Masḥūr from s-ḥ-r, bewitched, enchanted, from saḥara, to bewitch. The verse's structure — We know best how they listen when they listen to you and when they are private, when wrongdoers say you follow only bewitched man — with knowledge of how they listen and private saying, and quotation of wrongdoers saying bewitched man, shows that Allah knows their listening with mockery and private counsel saying bewitched man, to dissuade, as they have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion.

**How This Verse Teaches Allah Knows Listening And Private Counsel**

This verse teaches that Allah knows best how disbelievers listen to Prophet's recitation, with what intention, mockery, spying, etc., and what they say privately in secret counsel, when wrongdoers say you follow only bewitched man, to dissuade, as they cannot comprehend due to veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, and have no sure knowledge. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should know Allah knows how people listen and private counsel, so be sincere in listening, not mock, and should not say Prophet bewitched, as wrongdoers do, but believe Qur'an is revelation, wisdom, most upright, guides to most upright, varies signs so mindful. The practical lesson is to listen to Qur'an with mindfulness, not mockery, to believe, not say bewitched man, as wrongdoers do, who have gone far astray cannot find Right Way.

**A Reflection On Bewitched Man Accusation**

Rajulan masḥūran — man bewitched, enchanted — accusation that Prophet is bewitched, affected by magic, so Qur'an is magic, not revelation, as wrongdoers say privately to dissuade followers, you follow only bewitched man, meaning if you follow Prophet you follow bewitched, not guided. This accusation is similar to previous accusations that Qur'an is magic, poet, etc., as disbelievers have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant. The believer knows Prophet not bewitched, Qur'an not magic, but revelation, wisdom, most upright, guides to most upright, explains everything in detail, varies signs so mindful, and that Allah knows best how they listen and private counsel, and that wrongdoers say bewitched man to dissuade, but see how they call names so far astray cannot find Right Way, as next verse says.

---

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:48

> **See how they call you names ˹O Prophet˺! So they have gone so ˹far˺ astray that they cannot find the ˹Right˺ Way.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**See How They Call You Names O Prophet**

Unẓur kayfa ḍarabū laka al-amthāla — see how they coined, struck for you examples, parables, names. Unẓur from n-ẓ-r, to see, look, consider, as verse 21 unẓur kayfa faḍḍalnā, see how We favoured. Kayfa, how. Ḍarabū from ḍ-r-b, to coin, strike, give, set forth, from ḍaraba, to strike, coin, give example, as ḍaraba mathal, to coin example, parable. Laka, for you, to you, O Prophet. Al-amthāla from m-th-l, examples, parables, likenesses, names, from mathal, example, parable, likeness. Al-Ṭabarī explains that see how they call you names, O Prophet, how they coined for you examples, parables, names, like bewitched man, poet, magician, madman, etc., as they say you follow only bewitched man, as previous verse, and other names. Al-Qurṭubī says ḍarabū laka al-amthāla means they coined for you examples, parables, names, calling you bewitched, poet, magician, etc., to insult, to dissuade, as wrongdoers do.

**So Far Astray Cannot Find Right Way**

Fa-ḍallū fa-lā yastaṭīʿūna sabīlan — so they went astray, so they cannot find way, cannot find Right Way. Fa-ḍallū from ḍ-l-l, to go astray, stray, be misguided, from ḍalla, to stray, as ḍalla astray. Fa-lā yastaṭīʿūna from ṭ-w-ʿ, to be able, can, from istaṭāʿa, to be able, can, as lā yastaṭīʿūna cannot. Sabīlan from s-b-l, way, path, Right Way, way to guidance, as sabīl way, as ibn al-sabīl son of way. Al-Ṭabarī says so they have gone so far astray, strayed, misguided, that they cannot find Right Way, way to guidance, truth, because they coined examples, names, like bewitched man, poet, etc., which are false, astray, so they cannot find Right Way, cannot find guidance, as they have veils over hearts, deafness in ears, hidden barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, barrier, so astray cannot find way.

**Why Calling Names Indicates Going Far Astray**

Why calling names indicates going far astray? Because calling Prophet bewitched, poet, magician, etc., shows they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, so they coin false examples, names, to insult, dissuade, which are astray, not Right Way, so they cannot find Right Way, cannot find guidance, as they are far astray. The phrase fa-ḍallū fa-lā yastaṭīʿūna sabīlan indicates consequence: because they coined false examples, names, they went astray, so cannot find way, cannot find Right Way, guidance, truth.

**Connection To Bewitched Man And Private Counsel And Veils**

Verse 47 said We know best how they listen and what they say privately, when wrongdoers say you follow only bewitched man. Verse 48 says see how they call you names, O Prophet, so far astray cannot find Right Way. The connection is that saying bewitched man is example of calling names, coining examples, parables, names, like bewitched, poet, magician, etc., which shows going far astray, cannot find Right Way, as they have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, no sure knowledge.

**Link To Varying Signs And Aversion And Hidden Barrier**

Verse 41 varied signs so they may be mindful but drives farther away, nufūran, aversion. Verses 45-46 hidden barrier, veils, deafness, turning backs in aversion when Lord alone mentioned. Verse 47-48 listening with mockery, saying bewitched man, calling names, so far astray cannot find Right Way. The link is that varying signs should make mindful, but disbelievers with barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, listening with mockery, saying bewitched man, calling names, go far astray, cannot find Right Way, increase in aversion, driving farther away.

**Linguistic Note On Darabu And Amthal And Dallu And Sabil**

Unẓur from n-ẓ-r, to see, look, consider. Kayfa from k-y-f, how. Ḍarabū from ḍ-r-b, to coin, strike, give example, from ḍaraba. Amthāl from m-th-l, examples, parables, likenesses, names, plural of mathal. Ḍallū from ḍ-l-l, to go astray, from ḍalla. Yastaṭīʿūna from ṭ-w-ʿ, to be able, can, from istaṭāʿa. Sabīl from s-b-l, way, path, Right Way. The verse's structure — see how they coined for you examples, so they went astray so they cannot find way — with command to see how they coined examples and consequence they went astray cannot find way, shows that coining false examples, names, like bewitched man, leads to going far astray, cannot find Right Way, guidance.

**How This Verse Teaches Consequence Of Calling Names**

This verse teaches consequence of calling Prophet names, coining false examples, parables: see how they call you names, O Prophet, how they coined for you examples, like bewitched man, poet, magician, etc., so they have gone so far astray that they cannot find Right Way, cannot find guidance, truth, way to truth, as they have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should not call Prophet names, not coin false examples, should see how disbelievers call names and go far astray cannot find Right Way, and should avoid, believe, follow most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to. The practical lesson is to not coin false examples, names, for Prophet, not say bewitched, poet, etc., but believe he is Prophet, revelation, wisdom, most upright, and to follow Right Way, not go astray, cannot find way.

**A Reflection On Far Astray Cannot Find Way**

Fa-ḍallū fa-lā yastaṭīʿūna sabīlan — so they went astray so they cannot find way — indicates that calling names, coining false examples, leads to going far astray, misguided, so far that they cannot find Right Way, cannot find guidance, cannot find path to truth, as they have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant. The believer who believes in Hereafter, has no barrier, no veils, no deafness, no aversion, mentions Lord alone, loves monotheism, listens to Qur'an with mindfulness, not mockery, does not say bewitched man, does not call names, does not go far astray, can find Right Way, follows most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, while disbelievers far astray cannot find Right Way, have painful punishment prepared.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:49

> **And they say ˹mockingly˺, “When we are reduced to bones and ashes, will we really be raised as a new creation?”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**They Say When Reduced To Bones And Ashes Will We Be Raised New Creation**

Wa-qālū a-idhā kunnā ʿiẓāman wa-rufātan a-innā la-mabʿūthūna khalqan jadīdan — and they said, when we are bones and crumbled, dust, ashes, will we be raised new creation? Wa-qālū from q-w-l, to say, they said, mockingly, disbelieving, saying mockingly, etc. A-idhā, when, interrogative, when, when, as when we are reduced to bones and ashes, etc. Kunnā from k-w-n, we are, were, become, as kunnā we are, were, become, as when we are reduced to bones and ashes, etc. ʿIẓāman from ʿ-ẓ-m, bones, plural of ʿaẓm, bone, as ʿiẓām bones, as bones, as reduced to bones, etc. Wa-rufātan from r-f-t, crumbled, dust, ashes, broken pieces, from rafata, to crumble, break into pieces, from rufāt, crumbled, dust, ashes, broken, as rufāt crumbled, dust, ashes, etc., as bones and ashes, bones and crumbled dust, etc. A-innā, will we indeed, interrogative, will we, will we really, as a-innā will we indeed, will we really, etc. La-mabʿūthūna from b-ʿ-th, raised, resurrected, sent, from baʿatha, to raise, resurrect, send, from mabʿūth, raised, resurrected, as la-mabʿūthūna will we be raised, resurrected, etc., as raised, resurrected, etc. Khalqan jadīdan, new creation, new creation, from kh-l-q, creation, and j-d-d, new, from jadīd, new, as khalq creation, jadīd new, as new creation, new creation, etc. Al-Ṭabarī explains that they, disbelievers, polytheists, say mockingly, disbelieving in Hereafter, resurrection, when we are reduced to bones and ashes, crumbled dust, will we really be raised as new creation, new creation? They mock, deny resurrection, think impossible that bones and ashes become new creation, etc., as they mock, deny resurrection, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says this is saying of those who disbelieve in Hereafter, deny resurrection, mock, saying when we are bones and rufāt, crumbled dust, ashes, will we be raised new creation? They think impossible, so mock, etc., as they disbelieve in Hereafter, deny resurrection, etc., as mocking, etc.

**Why Bones And Ashes And New Creation And Disbelief In Hereafter**

Why bones and ashes, rufāt, and new creation? Because after death body becomes bones, then crumbled dust, ashes, rufāt, broken, so they think how can bones and dust become new creation, new creation, again? They deny power of Allah to recreate, as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, far astray cannot find Right Way, so they say this mockingly, disbelieving, etc., as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, etc. The phrase khalqan jadīdan, new creation, indicates new creation, recreation, as first creation, as verse 51 will say One Who created you first time will bring you back, etc., as first creation, etc., as new creation, etc. Verses 10, 45-48 about disbelief in Hereafter, painful punishment, hidden barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, calling names bewitched man, far astray cannot find Right Way, etc., as disbelief in Hereafter, etc. Verse 49 says they say mockingly when we are bones and ashes will we be raised new creation, which is disbelief in Hereafter, resurrection, as they disbelieve in Hereafter, deny resurrection, mock, as they have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray, cannot find Right Way, so they deny resurrection, say bones and ashes new creation impossible, etc. The link is disbelief in Hereafter leads to denying resurrection, mocking, etc., as disbelief in Hereafter, etc., as mocking, etc.

**Connection To Previous Chapter About Bones And Resurrection And Hour And Answer Become Stones Or Iron**

Previous chapter said command at hand, Hour, resurrection, etc., and disbelievers swore Allah will not raise dead, etc. This verse says similar: when we are bones and ashes will we be raised new creation, denying resurrection, as previous chapter said they swear Allah will not raise dead, etc. The sūrah's theme of Hereafter, resurrection, Hour, appears: painful punishment for those who do not believe in Hereafter, hidden barrier for those who do not believe in Hereafter, now mocking resurrection, bones and ashes new creation, etc., as Hereafter, resurrection, Hour, etc., as painful punishment for those who do not believe in Hereafter, etc., as hidden barrier for those who do not believe in Hereafter, etc., as mocking resurrection, etc., as bones and ashes new creation, etc. Next verses answer: say become stones or iron or whatever you think harder to bring back to life, One Who created you first time will bring you back, they will say who will bring us back, say One Who created you first time, they will shake heads and say when will that be, say perhaps soon, etc., as answer to bones and ashes new creation, etc., as become stones or iron, etc., as One Who created first time will bring back, etc., as answer, etc., as resurrection, etc.

**Linguistic Note On Izam And Rufat And Mab'uthuna And Khalq Jadid**

Qālū from q-w-l, to say. Idhā, when. Kunnā from k-w-n, to be. ʿIẓām from ʿ-ẓ-m, bones, plural of ʿaẓm. Rufāt from r-f-t, crumbled, dust, ashes, from rafata, to crumble. Mabʿūthūn from b-ʿ-th, raised, resurrected, from baʿatha. Khalq from kh-l-q, creation, from khalaqa, to create. Jadīd from j-d-d, new, from jadda, to be new. The verse's structure — they said when we are bones and crumbled will we be raised new creation — with condition when bones and crumbled and question will we be raised new creation, shows disbelief, mocking, denying resurrection, thinking impossible, as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, far astray, etc., as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, etc., as far astray, etc., as disbelief, mocking, denying resurrection, etc., as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, etc.

**How This Verse Teaches Disbelief In Resurrection And Belief**

This verse teaches disbelief in resurrection by polytheists: they say mockingly, when we are reduced to bones and ashes, crumbled dust, will we really be raised as new creation, new creation? They mock, deny, think impossible, as they disbelieve in Hereafter, have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, far astray cannot find Right Way, no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, etc., as disbelief, mocking, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe in resurrection, that Allah Who created first time can recreate, even if bones and ashes, new creation, as He is All-Aware All-Seeing, All-Powerful, as next verses will say become stones or iron or whatever harder, One Who created you first time will bring you back, perhaps soon, etc., as answer, etc., as resurrection, etc. The practical lesson is to believe in resurrection, new creation, not mock, not deny, as disbelievers do, who have painful punishment prepared, barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray, etc., as disbelievers have painful punishment prepared, etc., as barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray, etc., as disbelief, mocking, etc., as believer believes in resurrection, new creation, etc., as Allah Who created first time can recreate, etc., as He is All-Aware All-Seeing, All-Powerful, Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, but also severe in punishment for disbelievers, etc.

**A Reflection On Bones And Ashes And New Creation And Stones Or Iron**

ʿIẓāman wa-rufātan — bones and crumbled, ashes, dust — indicates stages of decay after death: bones remain, then crumble into rufāt, dust, ashes, broken pieces, as body decays, etc., as bones and ashes, etc. Khalqan jadīdan — new creation, new creation — indicates recreation, new creation after decay, as first creation, as Allah created first time, etc., as new creation, etc. Disbelievers think bones and ashes cannot become new creation, mock, deny, as they have no sure knowledge, follow without knowledge, have veils, deafness, barrier, aversion, denying hearts, arrogant, far astray, etc., as disbelief, mocking, etc. Believer knows Allah Who created heavens and earth, seven heavens, earth, all things glorify Him, can recreate bones and ashes as new creation, as He created first time, as He is All-Aware All-Seeing, All-Powerful, Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, but also severe in punishment for disbelievers, etc. The verse therefore introduces resurrection denial that will be answered in next verses: become stones or iron or whatever harder, One Who created first time will bring you back, perhaps soon, etc., as answer, etc., as become stones or iron, etc., as One Who created first time will bring you back, etc., as answer, etc., as resurrection, etc., as believer believes in resurrection, new creation, etc., as Allah Who created first time can recreate, etc.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:50

> **Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “˹Yes, even if˺ you become stones, or iron,**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Say Even If You Become Stones Or Iron**

Qul kūnū ḥijāratan aw ḥadīdan — say, be stones or iron. Qul from q-w-l, to say, command to Prophet to answer disbelievers who mock resurrection, bones and ashes new creation. Kūnū from k-w-n, to be, become, imperative, be, become, plural, be, become, etc. Ḥijāratan from ḥ-j-r, stones, rocks, plural of ḥajar, stone, rock, as ḥijārah stones, rocks, etc. Aw ḥadīdan from ḥ-d-d, iron, from ḥadīd, iron, hard metal, etc. Al-Ṭabarī explains that say, O Prophet, to disbelievers who say when we are bones and ashes will we be raised new creation, mocking, yes even if you become stones or iron, even if you become stones, rocks, or iron, hard, you will be raised, recreated, as Allah Who created first time can recreate even if you become stones or iron, harder than bones and ashes, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says kūnū ḥijāratan aw ḥadīdan means even if you become stones or iron, harder, more resistant to decay, you will be raised, as Allah able to recreate stones, iron, as He created first time, as He is All-Powerful, All-Aware All-Seeing, etc., as Allah able to recreate stones, iron, etc., as He created first time, etc., as All-Powerful, etc.

**Why Stones Or Iron And Harder Than Bones And Ashes**

Why stones or iron? Because disbelievers said when we are bones and ashes, rufāt, crumbled dust, will we be raised new creation, thinking bones and ashes cannot be recreated, as bones and ashes weak, crumbled, but stones and iron are harder, more solid, more resistant, so even if you become stones or iron, harder than bones and ashes, you will be raised, as Allah able to recreate even harder, as argument from harder to easier: if Allah can recreate stones, iron, harder, then can recreate bones and ashes, easier, etc., as argument from harder to easier, etc. The phrase kūnū ḥijāratan aw ḥadīdan is hypothetical, even if you become stones or iron, you will be raised, as Allah's power not limited by hardness, as He created heavens and earth, seven heavens, earth, all things glorify Him, etc., as Allah's power not limited by hardness, material, as He created heavens and earth, etc., as He created first time, etc., as All-Powerful, etc. Stones and iron are examples of hard materials, harder than bones and ashes, rufāt, crumbled dust, indicating that even if you become hardest, stones or iron, you will be raised, recreated, as Allah's power not limited by hardness, material, etc., as He created heavens and earth, etc., as He created first time, etc., as All-Powerful, etc., as argument, etc.

**Connection To Bones And Ashes And New Creation And Whatever Harder And One Who Created First Time Will Bring Back**

Verse 49 said they say mockingly when we are bones and ashes will we be raised new creation, denying resurrection, etc., as bones and ashes new creation, etc., as denial, etc. Verse 50 says say, O Prophet, yes even if you become stones or iron, and verse 51 says or whatever you think is harder to bring to life, then they will ask who will bring us back, say One Who created you first time, they will shake heads and ask when will that be, say perhaps soon, etc., as answer, etc. The connection is that bones and ashes denial answered by even if stones or iron or whatever harder, you will be raised, One Who created first time will bring back, perhaps soon, as resurrection is soon, not far, as Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking you remained only little while, as verse 52 says, etc., as Day call, etc., as resurrection, etc. So bones and ashes denial answered by stones or iron or whatever harder, One Who created first time will bring back, perhaps soon, Day call, etc., as resurrection, etc., as answer, etc., as bones and ashes new creation, etc., as stones or iron, etc., as whatever harder, etc., as One Who created first time will bring back, etc., as perhaps soon, etc., as Day call, etc.

**Link To Allah Created First Time And Perhaps Soon And Day Call And Power To Recreate**

Verse 51-52 will say One Who created first time will bring back, perhaps soon, Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while, etc., as One Who created first time will bring back, etc., as perhaps soon, etc., as Day call, etc. Verse 50 says become stones or iron, indicating even if harder, you will be raised, as Allah Who created first time able to recreate, as He created heavens and earth, seven heavens, earth, all things, etc., and resurrection soon, Day call, etc., so believe, not mock, etc., as Allah created first time able to recreate, etc., as He created heavens and earth, etc., as resurrection soon, etc., as Day call, etc., as believe, not mock, etc., as Allah created first time able to recreate, etc., as He created heavens and earth, etc., as resurrection soon, etc., as Day call, etc., as believe, not mock, etc.

**Linguistic Note On Kunu And Hijarah And Hadid And Harder**

Qul from q-w-l, to say. Kūnū from k-w-n, to be, become, imperative plural, be, become. Ḥijārah from ḥ-j-r, stones, rocks, plural of ḥajar, from ḥajara, to be stone. Ḥadīd from ḥ-d-d, iron, from ḥadda, to be sharp, iron sharp. The verse's structure — say be stones or iron — with command to say and hypothetical be stones or iron, indicates even if you become stones or iron, harder than bones and ashes, you will be raised, recreated, as Allah able, as argument from harder to easier, showing power to recreate any, even hardest, etc., as say be stones or iron, etc., as hypothetical, etc., as even if you become stones or iron, etc., as harder than bones and ashes, etc., as you will be raised, etc., as Allah able, etc., as argument from harder to easier, etc., as power to recreate any, even hardest, etc.

**How This Verse Teaches Power To Recreate Even Stones Iron And Whatever Harder**

This verse teaches power of Allah to recreate even if you become stones or iron, harder than bones and ashes, as answer to mocking denial of resurrection when bones and ashes new creation, etc., as answer, etc. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe Allah Who created first time can recreate even if become stones or iron, harder, as He is All-Powerful, All-Aware All-Seeing, Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, but also severe, and resurrection perhaps soon, Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while, etc., as answer, etc., as resurrection, etc. The practical lesson is to believe in resurrection, that Allah can recreate even stones, iron, whatever harder, as He created first time, not mock, not deny, as disbelievers do, who have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray cannot find Right Way, painful punishment prepared, etc., as disbelievers have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray cannot find Right Way, painful punishment prepared, etc., as believer believes in resurrection, etc., as Allah can recreate even stones, iron, whatever harder, as He created first time, etc., as He is All-Powerful, All-Aware All-Seeing, Most Forbearing All-Forgiving, etc.

**A Reflection On Stones And Iron And Power And First Creation**

Ḥijāratan aw ḥadīdan — stones or iron — two hard materials, stones, rocks, hard, and iron, metal, hard, strong, harder than bones and ashes, rufāt, crumbled dust, indicating that even if you become hardest, stones or iron, you will be raised, recreated, as Allah's power not limited by hardness, material, as He created heavens and earth, seven heavens, earth, all things glorify Him, all things, not a thing except glorifies with praise, but you cannot comprehend their glorification, He is Forbearing Forgiving, etc., as Allah's power not limited by hardness, material, etc., as He created heavens and earth, etc. The believer who reflects will believe that even if become stones or iron, Allah can recreate, as He created first time, One Who created first time will bring back, perhaps soon, Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while, so prepare, believe in Hereafter, follow most upright guidance, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, healing mercy, station of praise, honourable entrance and exit, supporting authority, truth come falsehood vanished, etc., while disbelievers mock resurrection, deny, etc., painful punishment, loss, etc., as they have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray cannot find Right Way, etc.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:51

> **or whatever you think is harder to bring to life!” Then they will ask ˹you˺, “Who will bring us back ˹to life˺?” Say, “The One Who created you the first time.” They will then shake their heads at you and ask, “When will that be?” Say, “Perhaps it is soon!”**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Or Whatever You Think Harder To Bring To Life**

Aw khalqan mimmā yakburu fī ṣudūrikum — or creation from what is greater in your chests, whatever you think harder to bring to life. Aw, or, or even. Khalqan from kh-l-q, creation, creature, from khalaqa, to create. Mimmā, from what, whatever. Yakburu from k-b-r, to be greater, harder, bigger, from kabura, to be great, as akbar greater, kabīran great. Fī ṣudūrikum, in your chests, in your breasts, in your minds, hearts, from ṣ-d-r, chest, breast, as ṣadr, chest. Al-Ṭabarī explains that or whatever you think is harder to bring to life, greater in your chests, harder to recreate, like heavens, earth, mountains, etc., whatever you think greater, harder than stones, iron, bones, ashes, you will be raised, as Allah Who created first time can recreate whatever, even greater, harder. Al-Qurṭubī says mimmā yakburu fī ṣudūrikum means whatever is greater in your chests, harder in your minds, to bring to life, to recreate, like heavens, earth, mountains, etc., even if you become that, you will be raised.

**Who Will Bring Us Back, One Who Created First Time**

Fa-sa-yaqūlūna man yuʿīdunā qul alladhī faṭarakum awwala marratin — then they will say who will bring us back, return us, say One Who created you first time, originated you first time. Fa-sa-yaqūlūna from q-w-l, then they will say, future, they will ask. Man yuʿīdunā from ʿ-w-d, to bring back, return, repeat, from aʿāda, to bring back, return, as yuʿīdunā will bring us back, return us to life. Qul, say, O Prophet, answer. Alladhī faṭarakum, One Who created you, originated you, from f-ṭ-r, to create, originate, split, from faṭara, to create, originate, as faṭara, Creator, Originator, as faṭara heavens and earth. Awwala marratin, first time, first occurrence, from a-w-l, first, and m-r-r, time, occurrence, from marrah, time, occurrence. Al-Ṭabarī explains that then they will ask, who will bring us back to life, return us, after we become bones, ashes, stones, iron, whatever harder? Say, O Prophet, One Who created you first time, originated you first time, will bring you back, return you, as He created first time from nothing, can recreate, bring back, as He is All-Powerful, All-Aware All-Seeing, as He created heavens and earth, seven heavens, earth, all things.

**They Shake Heads And Ask When Will That Be, Perhaps Soon**

Fa-sa-yun-ghiḍūna ilayka ru'ūsahum wa-yaqūlūna matā huwa qul ʿasā an yakūna qarīban — then they will shake, nod to you their heads and say when is it, when will that be, say perhaps it is near, soon. Fa-sa-yun-ghiḍūna from gh-ḍ-ḍ, to shake, nod, move, from anghaḍa, to shake, nod, move, as yun-ghiḍūna shake, nod. Ilayka, to you, towards you. Ru'ūsahum from r-ʾ-s, heads, plural of ra's, head. Wa-yaqūlūna, and they say. Matā huwa, when is it, when will that be, resurrection, bringing back, from m-t-y, when. Qul, say. ʿAsā an yakūna qarīban, perhaps it is near, soon, from ʿ-s-y, perhaps, maybe, and k-w-n, to be, and q-r-b, near, close, soon, from qarīb, near, soon. Al-Ṭabarī explains that they will shake, nod their heads at you, in mockery, denial, disbelief, and ask when will that be, when will resurrection, bringing back be? Say, O Prophet, perhaps it is soon, near, close, not far, as Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while, as verse 52 says, and Hour near, command at hand, etc.

**Why Whatever Harder And First Creation And Perhaps Soon**

Why whatever harder, first creation, perhaps soon? Because disbelievers said bones and ashes new creation impossible, mocking, denying resurrection, as they have no sure knowledge, barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray cannot find Right Way, so answer: even if stones, iron, whatever harder you think greater in chests, you will be raised, One Who created first time will bring back, perhaps soon, near, not far, as Day call, etc., so believe, prepare, not mock. The argument: first creation from nothing is harder than recreation from bones, ashes, stones, iron, whatever harder, so if Allah created first time from nothing, He can recreate, bring back, as He is All-Powerful.

**Connection To Bones And Ashes And Stones Iron And Day Call**

Verse 49 bones and ashes new creation mocking denial, verse 50 stones or iron, verse 51 whatever harder, who will bring back, One Who created first time, shake heads when will that be, perhaps soon, verse 52 Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while. The connection is sequence of resurrection argument: bones and ashes denial, even stones, iron, whatever harder, you will be raised, One Who created first time will bring back, perhaps soon, Day call, etc., showing resurrection real, near, soon, not far, so believe, prepare.

**Linguistic Note On Yakburu Fi Sudurikum And Yu'iduna And Fatara And Yun-ghiduna And Qariban**

Khalq from kh-l-q, creation. Mimmā, from what, whatever. Yakburu from k-b-r, to be greater, harder, bigger, from kabura. Ṣudūr from ṣ-d-r, chests, breasts, plural of ṣadr. Yuʿīdunā from ʿ-w-d, to bring back, return, from aʿāda. Faṭara from f-ṭ-r, to create, originate, from faṭara. Awwal from a-w-l, first. Marrah from m-r-r, time, occurrence. Yun-ghiḍūna from gh-ḍ-ḍ, to shake, nod, from anghaḍa. Ru'ūs from r-ʾ-s, heads. Matā from m-t-y, when. ʿAsā from ʿ-s-y, perhaps, maybe. Qarīb from q-r-b, near, soon, close. The verse's structure — or creation from what is greater in your chests, then they will say who will bring us back, say One Who created you first time, then they will shake heads and say when is it, say perhaps it is near — with hypothetical whatever harder, question who will bring back, answer One Who created first time, shaking heads mocking, question when, answer perhaps soon, shows resurrection argument, first creation proof, nearness, soon, Day call.

**How This Verse Teaches Resurrection Via First Creation And Nearness**

This verse teaches resurrection via first creation and nearness: or whatever you think harder to bring to life, whatever greater in your chests, you will be raised, One Who created you first time will bring you back, return you, as He created first time from nothing, can recreate, bring back, perhaps it is soon, near, close, not far, as Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe resurrection, that One Who created first time will bring back, even if become bones, ashes, stones, iron, whatever harder, greater in chests, as first creation harder than recreation, and that resurrection perhaps soon, near, close, Day call, etc., so prepare, believe in Hereafter, not mock, not deny, as disbelievers do, who have barrier, veils, deafness, aversion, far astray cannot find Right Way, painful punishment prepared, hidden barrier.

**A Reflection On First Creation And Perhaps Soon**

Alladhī faṭarakum awwala marratin — One Who created you first time, originated you first time — is proof of resurrection: if He created first time from nothing, no bones, no ashes, no stones, no iron, from nothing, He can recreate, bring back, return, from bones, ashes, stones, iron, whatever harder, as recreation easier than first creation, as Qur'an says He is One Who begins creation then repeats it and it is easier for Him. ʿAsā an yakūna qarīban — perhaps it is soon, near — indicates resurrection, Hour, near, soon, not far, as command at hand, Hour near, Day He will call you soon, thinking remained only little while, as verse 52 says, so prepare, believe, follow most upright guidance, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, while disbelievers mock, shake heads, ask when, but perhaps soon, Day call, etc.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:52

> **On the Day He will call you, you will ˹instantly˺ respond by praising Him, thinking you had remained ˹in the world˺ only for a little while.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**On Day He Will Call You, You Will Respond Praising Him**

Yawma yadʿūkum fa-tastajībūna bi-ḥamdihi — Day He will call you, then you will respond with His praise, praising Him. Yawma from y-w-m, Day, Day of Resurrection, Day He will call. Yadʿūkum from d-ʿ-w, to call, summon, as yadʿu human prays, calls. Fa-tastajībūna from j-w-b, to respond, answer, from istajāba, to respond, answer, as istajāba response. Bi-ḥamdihi from ḥ-m-d, with His praise, praising Him, from ḥamd, praise, as yusabbiḥu bi-ḥamdihi glorifies with His praise, all things glorify with praise. Al-Ṭabarī explains that on Day He will call you, summon you, for resurrection, reckoning, you will instantly respond, answer, by praising Him, with His praise, meaning you will respond praising Him, acknowledging, as all will respond, even disbelievers, praising, as Day call, as previous verse perhaps soon, resurrection near. Al-Qurṭubī says yawma yadʿūkum indicates Day He will call you for resurrection, you will respond with praise, praising Him, as all respond, as creation.

**Thinking You Remained Only Little While**

Wa-taẓunnūna in labithtum illā qalīlan — and you will think you remained not except little, only little while. Wa-taẓunnūna from ẓ-n-n, to think, assume, from ẓanna, to think. In labithtum from l-b-th, to remain, stay, tarry, from labitha, to remain, stay. Illā qalīlan, except little, only little while, from q-l-l, little, small, few, from qalīl, little. Al-Ṭabarī explains that you will think, assume, you remained in world, in graves, only little while, short time, as time passes quickly, as previous chapter said when Hour comes criminals swear they remained not except hour, etc., and as Qur'an says they will think they remained only little while, as worldly life short compared to Hereafter, as Hereafter far greater in rank and favour, as verse 21 says. Al-Rāzī says taẓunnūna in labithtum illā qalīlan indicates that on Day call, you will think worldly life, grave stay, was only little while, short, as time perception changes, as Hereafter long, worldly short.

**Why Day Call And Respond Praising And Little While**

Why Day call, respond praising, thinking remained only little while? Because resurrection perhaps soon, near, as previous verse said perhaps soon, and Day He will call you, you will respond praising Him, thinking remained only little while, showing nearness, shortness of worldly life, grave stay, compared to Hereafter, which is far greater, eternal, as verse 21 Hereafter far greater in rank and favour. The response with praise, bi-ḥamdihi, indicates that even disbelievers will praise Him on Day, acknowledge, as all will respond, as all things glorify Him with praise, but you cannot comprehend their glorification, and on Day you will respond with praise, as Day call.

**Connection To Bones And Ashes And Stones Iron And Whatever Harder And First Creation And Perhaps Soon**

Verses 49-51 bones and ashes new creation mocking, stones or iron, whatever harder, who will bring back, One Who created first time, shake heads when, perhaps soon. Verse 52 Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while, showing resurrection soon, Day call, response with praise, short stay, as proof that resurrection near, not far, as perhaps soon, and that worldly life short, little while, compared to Hereafter far greater, so prepare, believe, not mock.

**Link To Command At Hand And Hour And Little While**

Previous chapter opened with command at hand, do not hasten it, and said criminals swear they remained not except hour, etc. This verse says Day He will call you respond praising Him thinking remained only little while, similar theme: worldly life, grave stay, short, little while, Hour near, command at hand, perhaps soon, so do not hasten, do not mock, believe, prepare.

**Linguistic Note On Yad'ukum And Tastajibuna And Hamd And Labithtum And Qalilan**

Yawm from y-w-m, Day. Yadʿūkum from d-ʿ-w, to call, summon. Tastajībūna from j-w-b, to respond, answer, from istajāba. Ḥamd from ḥ-m-d, praise. Taẓunnūna from ẓ-n-n, to think, assume. Labithtum from l-b-th, to remain, stay, tarry, from labitha. Qalīl from q-l-l, little, small, few. The verse's structure — Day He will call you then you will respond with His praise and you will think you remained not except little — with Day call, response with praise, thinking remained only little, shows resurrection Day, response praising, short stay perception, nearness, shortness of worldly life compared to Hereafter.

**How This Verse Teaches Resurrection Day Call And Short Stay**

This verse teaches resurrection Day call and short stay perception: On Day He will call you, summon you for resurrection, you will instantly respond, answer, by praising Him, with His praise, thinking, assuming you remained in world, graves, only little while, short time, as worldly life short, grave stay short, compared to Hereafter far greater, eternal, as Hereafter far greater in rank and favour. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should believe Day He will call, you will respond praising Him, and that worldly life short, little while, so prepare for Hereafter, believe, follow most upright guidance, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving, far greater rank and favour, while disbelievers mock, say bones and ashes new creation, stones, iron, whatever harder, who will bring back, shake heads when, but perhaps soon, Day call, etc., and will think remained only little while, but painful punishment prepared.

**A Reflection On Responding With Praise And Little While**

Bi-ḥamdihi — with His praise, praising Him — indicates that on Day call, you will respond praising Him, acknowledging, glorifying, as all things glorify Him with praise, seven heavens, earth, all those in them glorify Him, not a thing except glorifies with praise, but you cannot comprehend their glorification, He is Forbearing Forgiving, and on Day you will respond with praise, even disbelievers will praise, acknowledge, as Day call, as all respond. In labithtum illā qalīlan — you remained not except little, only little while — indicates shortness of worldly life, grave stay, compared to Hereafter, which is far greater, eternal, so worldly life little, short, not to be sole goal, fleeting, ʿājilah, hasty, immediate, but Hereafter, ākhirah, lasting, far greater in rank and favour, so desire Hereafter, strive accordingly, be believer, appreciated striving, mighty reward.

## Sūrah al-Isrāʾ 17:53

> **Tell My ˹believing˺ servants to say only what is best. Satan certainly seeks to sow discord among them. Satan is indeed a sworn enemy to humankind.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**Tell My Believing Servants To Say Only What Is Best**

Wa-qul li-ʿibādī yaqūlū allatī hiya aḥsanu — and say to My servants they say what is best, most excellent. Wa-qul from q-w-l, to say, command to Prophet. Li-ʿibādī, to My servants, My believing servants, from ʿ-b-d, servants, slaves, worshippers, as ʿibādihi His servants. Yaqūlū, they say, they should say. Allatī hiya aḥsanu, what is best, most excellent, best, from ḥ-s-n, good, best, as aḥsanu best, as previous aḥsanu best, and ḥasan good, and aḥsantum do good, and iḥsānan excellence. Al-Ṭabarī explains that tell My believing servants to say only what is best, most excellent, best speech, kind, gentle, as qawlan karīman noble word for parents, qawlan maysūran kind word when lack means, and now what is best, most excellent, as best speech, to avoid discord, as Satan seeks to sow discord among them. Al-Qurṭubī says allatī hiya aḥsanu means what is best, most excellent, best speech, kind, gentle, truthful, as best speech prevents discord, Satan seeks discord, so say best.

**Satan Seeks To Sow Discord Among Them**

Inna al-shayṭāna yanzaghu baynahum — indeed Satan sows discord, incites, seeks to sow discord among them, between them. Inna, indeed, surely. Al-shayṭāna from sh-ṭ-n, Satan, Devil, from shaṭana, to be far. Yanzaghu from n-z-gh, to sow discord, incite, provoke, cause discord, from nazagha, to sow discord, incite, provoke, from nazgh, discord, incitement, provocation, as Satan's incitement. Baynahum, between them, among them, among servants, believers. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Satan seeks to sow discord among them, among believing servants, incite, provoke, cause enmity, discord, via evil speech, harsh words, etc., so tell servants to say best to prevent Satan's discord. Al-Rāzī says yanzaghu baynahum indicates Satan seeks to sow discord, incite, provoke, cause enmity, via speech, so saying best prevents his discord.

**Satan Sworn Enemy To Humankind**

Inna al-shayṭāna kāna li-l-insāni ʿaduwwan mubīnan — indeed Satan is to human enemy clear, sworn, manifest enemy. Inna, indeed. Al-shayṭāna, Satan. Kāna, is, was, indicates he is. Li-l-insāni, to human, for human, to humankind, from a-n-s, human, as insān human, ever hasty. ʿAduwwan from ʿ-d-w, enemy, foe, from ʿadā, to be enemy, from ʿaduww, enemy. Mubīnan from b-y-n, clear, manifest, sworn, evident, from bāna, to be clear, manifest, from mubīn, clear, manifest. Al-Ṭabarī explains that Satan is indeed sworn enemy to humankind, clear, manifest enemy, not hidden, as he refused to prostrate to Adam, vowed to take hold of descendants except few, as verse 62-64 will say, and seeks to sow discord, incite, as sworn enemy. Al-Qurṭubī says ʿaduwwan mubīnan means clear, manifest, sworn enemy, evident enmity, not hidden, so beware, say best to prevent his discord.

**Why Say Best To Prevent Satan Discord**

Why say best to prevent Satan discord? Because Satan, Devil, ever ungrateful to Lord, kafūran, ever ungrateful, as verse 27 says, and is sworn enemy to humankind, clear enemy, seeks to sow discord among servants, believers, via evil speech, harsh words, so saying best, most excellent, prevents his discord, as best speech, kind, gentle, truthful, prevents enmity, discord. The sūrah's moral code included honouring parents with noble word, kind word when lack means, now best speech among servants, to prevent Satan discord, as Satan seeks discord.

**Connection To Wasteful Brothers To Devils And No Authority Over Faithful**

Verse 27 said wasteful brothers to devils, Devil ever ungrateful. Verse 65 will say you will have no authority over My faithful servants, sufficient Lord as Guardian. Verse 53 says tell believing servants to say best, Satan seeks to sow discord among them, Satan sworn enemy to humankind. The connection is that wasteful brothers to devils follow Devil, but faithful servants who say best, most excellent, have no authority for Satan over them, Lord Guardian, sufficient, as Satan seeks discord but saying best prevents discord, as faithful servants protected.

**Link To Lower Wing Of Humility And Noble Word And Kind Word**

Verse 23-24 noble word for parents, lower wing of humility out of mercy, verse 28 kind word when lack means, verse 53 best speech among servants to prevent Satan discord, all about good speech: noble word, kind word, best speech, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to, as Qur'an guides to most upright, including speech, most upright speech.

**Linguistic Note On Ahsanu And Yanzaghu And Aduwwan Mubinan**

Qul from q-w-l, to say. ʿIbād from ʿ-b-d, servants. Yaqūlū from q-w-l, to say. Aḥsan from ḥ-s-n, best, most excellent, from ḥasuna, to be good. Shayṭān from sh-ṭ-n, Satan, Devil. Yanzaghu from n-z-gh, to sow discord, incite, provoke, from nazagha. Bayn from b-y-n, between. Insān from a-n-s, human. ʿAduww from ʿ-d-w, enemy, from ʿadā. Mubīn from b-y-n, clear, manifest, from bāna. The verse's structure — say to My servants they say what is best, indeed Satan sows discord among them, indeed Satan is to human enemy clear — with command to say best, reason Satan sows discord, and statement Satan sworn enemy, shows that saying best prevents Satan discord, as Satan sworn enemy seeks discord, so say best, most excellent, to prevent.

**How This Verse Teaches Best Speech To Prevent Satan Discord**

This verse teaches best speech to prevent Satan discord: tell My believing servants to say only what is best, most excellent, best speech, kind, gentle, truthful, as Satan seeks to sow discord among them, incite, provoke, cause enmity, and Satan is indeed sworn enemy to humankind, clear, manifest enemy, not hidden, so beware, say best to prevent his discord. Al-Qurṭubī says believer should say best, most excellent, best speech, kind, gentle, truthful, to prevent Satan discord, enmity, as Satan seeks to sow discord among servants, believers, via evil speech, harsh words, so saying best, most excellent, prevents, as part of most upright guidance, wisdom, that Qur'an guides to. The practical lesson is to say best, noble word, kind word, best speech, to parents, relatives, poor, traveller, servants, all, to prevent Satan discord, as Satan sworn enemy, clear enemy, seeks discord, but faithful servants who say best have no authority for Satan over them, Lord Guardian sufficient.

**A Reflection On Satan Sworn Enemy**

ʿAduwwan mubīnan — enemy clear, manifest, sworn, evident — indicates Satan's enmity to humankind is clear, manifest, not hidden, sworn, evident, as he refused to prostrate to Adam, said should I prostrate to one created from mud, vowed to take hold of descendants except few, if delayed until Day Judgment, as verse 62 says, and Allah said be gone, whoever follows you Hell ample reward, etc., as verses 63-65 will say, and Satan incites with voice, cavalry, infantry, manipulates wealth and children, promises delusion, but no authority over faithful servants, sufficient Lord as Guardian. The believer who knows Satan sworn enemy, clear enemy, will say best, most excellent, to prevent his discord, will seek protection, will be faithful servant, no authority for Satan, Lord Guardian sufficient, as most upright guidance, wisdom, leading to mighty reward, appreciated striving.
